# Clinical Logic Database
> **NOTE TO SYSTEM:** This file is read by the app. Do not alter the formatting of lines starting with '##', '* REQ:', or '* EXC:'.

## [IS_AAC_MATERIAL]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Physical Contradiction: Hand Compromise limits AAC Use] Actively biting or scratching one's own hands physically precludes the simultaneous functional fine-motor use of AAC materials like an iPad or PECS book.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EXERTION] | [Physical Contradiction: High Exertion limits AAC Material] Utilizing a fine-motor AAC device, tablet, or PECS book is physically unfeasible while actively engaging in high-exertion activities like jumping, running an obstacle course, or catching a ball.

---

## [IS_AAC_TARGET]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_AAC_MATERIAL] | [Physical/Environmental Requirement for AAC] Teaching a learner an AAC target, such as using an AAC device, physically and environmentally requires the presence of an AAC material (e.g., a speech device, tablet, or PECS book).

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_GI_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Skill Acquisition during Medical Distress] Enforcing skill acquisition and communication targets while the client is actively experiencing medical GI distress or refusing meals due to illness is an ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Physical Contradiction: Hand Compromise limits AAC Targets] Hand compromise behaviors like biting or violently scratching one's hands physically impair the fine motor capabilities required to independently use an AAC device.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_AROUSAL] | [Methodological Mismatch: Skill Acquisition during High Arousal] Teaching fine motor AAC targets is methodologically unfeasible when a client is highly agitated, crying, or pacing rapidly.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Skill Targeting during Punishment] Targeting functional communication skills during high-effort positive practice overcorrection is methodologically incorrect, as punishment suppresses behavior while skill acquisition requires reinforcement.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EXERTION] | [Physical Contradiction: AAC Targets during High Exertion] The precise fine motor and visual attention required for AAC communication targeting is physically incompatible with simultaneous high physical exertion such as running or jumping.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Safety Violation: Skill Targeting during Crisis] Attempting to teach fine motor communication targets while the client is engaging in imminent danger (e.g., eloping, severe self-injury) is a severe safety and ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Physical Impossibility: AAC Target During Medical Emergency] It is physically and medically impossible to meaningfully prompt or expect a client to utilize an AAC device to communicate while they are actively experiencing an incapacitating medical crisis, such as a tonic-clonic seizure or severe asthma attack.

---

## [IS_ACADEMIC]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_CONFIDENTIAL_DISCUSSION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Demands During Consultation] Running discrete academic trials effectively is methodologically impossible while simultaneously engaged in a confidential discussion (e.g., reviewing IEP goals, parent meetings).
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | [Ethical Violation: Demands During Crisis] Presenting academic demands during an active behavioral crisis involving physical blocking or tactical breaks violates de-escalation protocols.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical Impossibility: Visual Tasks in the Dark] It is physically impossible to conduct visual academic tasks (e.g., identifying colors, tracing letters, completing worksheets) in a dark sensory room.
* EXC: [IS_GI_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Academic Demands during Medical Distress] Enforcing seated academic targets like patterning, tracing, or counting when the child is experiencing physical GI issues is unethical and methodologically inappropriate.
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Physical Contradiction: Hand Compromise limits Fine Motor Academics] Biting or aggressively scratching hands physically precludes the ability to engage in academic fine motor tasks like tracing letters or writing one's name.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_AROUSAL] | [Methodological Mismatch: Academics during High Arousal] Expected seated academic tasks (tracing, sorting) cannot be methodologically implemented while a client is highly agitated, pacing the room rapidly, or highly distracted.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Academics during Overcorrection] Conducting academic readiness and skill acquisition tasks simultaneously with positive practice overcorrection is methodologically contradictory.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EXERTION] | [Methodological Contradiction: Academics during High Exertion] Engaging in high-exertion gross motor activities precludes the physical stability and focus required for seated academic tasks like tracing or counting.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Safety Violation: Academics during Crisis] Implementing academic demands like counting or tracing while a client is in imminent danger (bolting, severe self-injury) is a severe safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical/Physical Violation: Academics During Medical Emergency] Continuing or presenting academic demands (e.g., counting, identifying shapes) while a client is experiencing a life-threatening medical crisis is both physically impossible and constitutes severe medical neglect and abuse.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Academic Demands during Respiratory Crisis] Presenting structured academic demands (e.g., worksheets, counting) to a client in active respiratory distress is physically dangerous, medically contraindicated, and ethically prohibited.
* EXC: [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Cognitive Distraction during Traffic Navigation] Teaching cognitive and fine-motor academic skills (e.g., tracing, counting) during active traffic hazard navigation creates a profound cognitive and visual distraction, strictly dividing the learner's attention away from life-threatening environmental hazards.

---

## [IS_ACADEMIC_MATERIAL]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | [Methodological Contradiction: Materials During Crisis] Academic materials must be cleared during a physical crisis for safety; attempting to utilize them during an active block or crisis is methodologically incorrect.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical Impossibility: Visual Materials in the Dark] Using visual academic materials (worksheets, 2D picture cards) in a dark sensory room is physically impossible due to lack of visibility.
* EXC: [IS_GI_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Academic Materials during Medical Distress] Enforcing the use of academic materials (worksheets, flashcards) when a client is experiencing physical GI distress is unethical.
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Physical Contradiction: Hand Compromise limits Academic Material Use] You cannot functionally utilize fine motor academic materials (worksheets, crayons, cards) while actively biting or violently scratching your own hands.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EXERTION] | [Physical Contradiction: Academic Materials during High Exertion] Utilizing worksheets, crayons, or dry-erase markers is physically impractical while a client is simultaneously engaging in high-exertion activities such as running, jumping, or navigating an obstacle course.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Safety Mismatch: Academic Materials during Imminent Danger] Utilizing worksheets, crayons, or flashcards is completely physically unmanageable and inappropriate during a crisis like bolting or severe SIB.
* EXC: [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Material Distraction in Traffic] Requiring the learner to manage and focus on academic materials (such as worksheets, flashcards, or whiteboards) while navigating a parking lot or crossing the street distracts essential visual attention away from oncoming traffic, presenting a physical safety hazard.

---

## [IS_ADL_TARGET]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_CHAINED] | Derived Chain (IS_ADL_TARGET -> IS_CHAINING_FORMAT -> IS_CHAINED): [Methodological Dependency: ADL Tasks Require Chaining Formats] Methodologically, teaching an Activity of Daily Living (ADL) conceptually requires a chaining format or task analysis because ADLs inherently consist of sequential behavioral steps rather than single discrete responses. | [Methodological Dependency: Chaining Format] Chaining intervention formats (such as Forward Chaining, Backward Chaining, Total Task Chaining, and Task Analysis) are systematically designed to teach complex, multi-step routines. Applying a chaining methodology clinically and logically requires a chained target behavior (e.g., ADLs, vocational routines) to break down and teach.
* REQ: [IS_CHAINING_FORMAT] | [Methodological Dependency: ADL Tasks Require Chaining Formats] Methodologically, teaching an Activity of Daily Living (ADL) conceptually requires a chaining format or task analysis because ADLs inherently consist of sequential behavioral steps rather than single discrete responses.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_CONFIDENTIAL_DISCUSSION] | [Methodological Mismatch: ADLs During Consultation] Teaching complex, chained ADL routines requires close supervision and prompting, which cannot be done while simultaneously conducting a confidential meeting or review.
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | [Ethical Violation: ADL Demands During Crisis] Running ADL demands (e.g., forcing a toileting routine or teeth brushing) during an active crisis indicator escalates the crisis and violates safety protocols.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Safety Hazard: Unsupervised ADLs in the Dark] Teaching self-care and ADL targets (toileting, dressing, brushing teeth) in a dark room creates an extreme safety hazard and is physically impractical.
* EXC: [IS_DISCRETE] | [Methodological Mismatch: Chained vs. Discrete] ADL targets are fundamentally chained, multi-step routines. Teaching them purely as discrete trials (DTT) violates methodological definitions of chaining.
* EXC: [IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED] | [Physical Impossibility: Proximal Prompts from a Distance] Teaching ADLs requires close proximity for physical prompting and safety supervision, making it physically impossible to conduct if a required safety distance must be maintained.
* EXC: [IS_ELOPEMENT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ADL_TARGET' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_ELOPEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ADL_TARGET' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Physical Contradiction: Hand Compromise limits ADL Execution] Tying shoes, opening containers, or washing hands requires functional hand use, which is physically impossible while simultaneously engaging in biting or scratching one's hands.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_AROUSAL] | [Methodological Mismatch: ADLs during High Arousal] Teaching precise daily living skills (like tying shoes or proper utensil use) is methodologically unfeasible when the client is agitated, crying, or highly hyperactive.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EXERTION] | [Physical Contradiction: ADLs during High Exertion] Executing precise daily living skills like brushing teeth or tying shoes is physically incompatible with engaging in high-exertion gross motor activities simultaneously.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Safety Violation: ADL Demands during Crisis] Forcing daily living skill demands while a client is in imminent danger (eloping, severe self-injury) is medically dangerous and unethical.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical/Physical Violation: ADLs During Medical Emergency] Targeting daily living skills such as shoe tying, packing a backpack, or washing hands cannot physically or ethically occur while a client is in the throes of a medical emergency like a seizure or respiratory crisis.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ADL_TARGET' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ADL_TARGET' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_PICA] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ADL_TARGET' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_PICA' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: ADL Demands during Respiratory Crisis] Mandating activities of daily living (such as brushing teeth or eating) during an active asthma attack or respiratory distress is physically hazardous and ethically forbidden.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ADL_TARGET' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_SIB' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ADL_TARGET' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL] | [Methodological & Safety Contradiction: ADLs in Traffic] Running structured Activities of Daily Living (ADL) targets such as brushing teeth, dressing, or using utensils while simultaneously practicing safe street crossing or navigating a parking lot is methodologically absurd and creates an immense physical safety hazard.

## [IS_AGGRESSION]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Physical Safety Risk] Implementing planned ignoring (attention extinction) in response to direct, severe physical assault (e.g., head-butting, biting, punching) prevents the practitioner from safely blocking strikes, posing an imminent physical danger to staff and peers.
* EXC: [IS_CONFIDENTIAL_DISCUSSION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Consultation During Aggression] Attempting to conduct confidential reviews (IEP goals, parent meetings) during active client aggression is clinically irresponsible and methodologically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Severe Safety Hazard: Aggression in Zero Visibility] Active aggression (hitting, kicking, punching) occurring in a dark sensory room creates a critical safety hazard, as staff cannot see to block strikes safely or evade.
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Safety Violation: Gross Motor Targets during Aggression] Attempting structured gross motor targets like jumping or catching a ball while the client is actively engaging in aggression (punching, kicking, head-butting) is physically unfeasible and dangerous.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Safety Mismatch: High Effort Punishment during Active Aggression] Utilizing high-effort physical punishment like overcorrection while a client is actively engaging in aggression (punching, kicking) is a severe safety risk that will escalate dangerous behavior.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Aggression vs Independence] A client cannot simultaneously engage in severe aggression (punching, biting others) while displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior and transitioning smoothly and independently.
* EXC: [IS_LOW_AROUSAL] | [Physical Impossibility: High-Energy Aggression vs. Lethargy] Directed, high-energy aggressive acts (punching, kicking, head-butting) are physically contradictory to a state of extreme low arousal, characterized by passive slumping, sluggishness, and severe lethargy.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Physical Impossibility: Aggression During Incapacitating Crisis] A client actively experiencing an incapacitating medical crisis (e.g., a seizure) cannot simultaneously engage in intentional, directed high-energy aggressive acts like punching or hair-pulling.
* EXC: [IS_PROXIMITY_PAIRING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Pairing during Aggression] Initiating physical proximity pairing (e.g., tickles, deep pressure) during active aggression (punching, biting) is physically unsafe and methodologically invalid, as it provides direct positive reinforcement for aggressive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Behavioral Mismatch: Aggression vs Regulated State] Active physical aggression (e.g., punching, biting, swiping) inherently involves high levels of maladaptive behavior, which contradicts a regulated state defined by calmness, cooperation, and an absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DEMAND] | [Physical Mismatch: Aggression and Respiratory Demands] A client actively engaged in severe physical aggression (punching, kicking, biting) cannot concurrently engage in structured respiratory compliance demands such as slow, deep breathing.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Safety Violation: Aggression and Sharp Objects] Permitting access to sharp objects like scissors while a client is actively aggressive poses an immediate, severe physical safety hazard.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [State Contradiction: Aggression and Regulation] Active aggression directly contradicts states of being highly regulated, happily paired, or exhibiting quick instructional control.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Aggression and Transitions] A client engaging in active aggression cannot simultaneously transition smoothly and happily without resistance.
* EXC: [IS_VISUAL_DEPENDENT_TASK] | [Physical & Methodological Impossibility: Precision Visual Tasks during Aggression] It is physically and methodologically impossible for a learner to successfully execute visually dependent fine-motor tasks (such as tracing letters, writing, or matching items) while actively engaging in severe physical aggression like punching, kicking, or hair-pulling.
* EXC: [IS_VOCATIONAL] | [Physical & Methodological Impossibility: Vocational Routines during Aggression] It is physically and methodologically impossible to accurately perform structured vocational tasks (such as assembling items or sorting mail) while simultaneously engaging in severe topographies of physical aggression.

---

## [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_ATTENTION_MAINTAINED] | [Methodological Dependency for Attention Extinction] Applying attention extinction (e.g., planned ignoring) methodologically requires that the target behavior is maintained by attention. Implementing this strategy for behaviors with other functions (like escape or automatic) is clinically invalid and contraindicated.
* REQ: [IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT] | [Ethical Dependency: Extinction Requires Reinforcement] Methodologically and ethically, implementing an extinction procedure such as attention extinction (planned ignoring) requires the concurrent use of a differential reinforcement procedure to teach and reinforce functionally equivalent alternative behaviors.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AGGRESSION] | [Ethical Violation: Physical Safety Risk] Implementing planned ignoring (attention extinction) in response to direct, severe physical assault (e.g., head-butting, biting, punching) prevents the practitioner from safely blocking strikes, posing an imminent physical danger to staff and peers.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Medical Neglect] Implementing planned ignoring (attention extinction) for a client who is actively experiencing medical pain or distress from an ear infection constitutes medical neglect and directly violates BACB guidelines requiring the prioritization of medical well-being.
* EXC: [IS_AUTOMATIC_MAINTAINED] | [Methodological Mismatch: Extinction Procedure vs Behavior Function] Withholding social attention (attention extinction) is methodologically ineffective and contradicts Skinnerian principles when applied to behaviors that are automatically maintained by internal sensory feedback, as the behavior does not rely on social mediation.
* EXC: [IS_BIO_HAZARD] | [Ethical Violation: Medical Neglect and Harm Prevention] Applying planned ignoring (attention extinction) to dangerous bio-hazards such as pica, scatolia, or disrobing is a severe ethical violation that places the client at imminent medical risk.
* EXC: [IS_CHOKING_HAZARD] | [Ethical Violation: Lethal Harm Prevention] Using planned ignoring when a client is actively engaging in a choking hazard (e.g., swallowing small objects) is a lethal medical and ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_CONTAGIOUS] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Medical Symptoms] Applying attention extinction (ignoring) to verifiable contagious illness symptoms constitutes medical neglect and violates BACB ethical guidelines regarding client welfare.
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | [Methodological Contradiction: Crisis Management] Planned ignoring cannot be implemented during an active crisis indicator that requires physical intervention (e.g., physical blocking to prevent exit), as blocking inherently breaks the extinction procedure.
* EXC: [IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' forces 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT'. 'IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching During Crisis] Formal differential reinforcement of alternative behaviors requires the client to be receptive to teaching; it cannot be methodologically implemented during a life-threatening, imminent danger crisis where safety/blocking is the sole priority.
* EXC: [IS_ELOPEMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Harm Prevention During Elopement] Utilizing attention extinction (planned ignoring) during active elopement is a profound safety violation, as ignoring a fleeing client presents an immediate risk of severe physical harm or death.
* EXC: [IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' forces 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT'. 'IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching During Crisis] Formal differential reinforcement of alternative behaviors requires the client to be receptive to teaching; it cannot be methodologically implemented during a life-threatening, imminent danger crisis where safety/blocking is the sole priority.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_MAINTAINED] | [Methodological Mismatch: Attention Extinction for Escape Function] Attention extinction removes social attention, which is methodologically contraindicated for escape-maintained behavior, as the functional reinforcer is demand removal rather than attention. This often inadvertently reinforces the escape behavior.
* EXC: [IS_GI_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction of Medical Symptoms] Utilizing planned ignoring for distress behaviors stemming from medical GI issues (constipation, lack of appetite) violates ethical guidelines regarding necessary medical care.
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction for Self-Injury] Planned ignoring is ethically contraindicated for self-injurious behavior like biting own hand or scratching skin due to the significant risk of permanent tissue damage.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Simultaneous Extinction and Punishment] Planned ignoring (withholding attention) and positive practice overcorrection (which requires physical guidance and interaction) are methodologically contradictory and cannot be implemented simultaneously.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction during Imminent Danger] Using planned ignoring for behaviors that present an imminent danger (e.g., elopement, head-banging, gouging eyes) is a severe ethical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Extinction on Independence] Planned ignoring is an extinction procedure for maladaptive behavior, which methodologically contradicts a state of independence where the client is displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction During Medical Crisis] Utilizing planned ignoring while a client is experiencing an acute medical emergency (seizure, severe dehydration) constitutes severe medical neglect and violates all duty-of-care guidelines.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction of Pain/Distress Communication] Implementing planned ignoring when a client is communicating or exhibiting signs of active medical distress (e.g., ear infection, dental pain, illness) is an ethical violation. Medical and physiological needs must be addressed rather than placed on extinction.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Ignoring Medical Distress] Utilizing planned ignoring (attention extinction) for behaviors that are stemming from or accompanied by active oral distress (like dental pain) is an ethical violation of client care and safety.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_CONTACT_WITH_THERAPIST] | [Physical Impossibility: Ignoring Direct Physical Attacks] It is methodologically and physically impossible to safely utilize planned ignoring (attention extinction) while a client is actively making direct physical contact with the therapist (e.g., biting or head-butting the therapist). Active physical intervention is required for practitioner safety.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' forces 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT'. 'IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching During Crisis] Formal differential reinforcement of alternative behaviors requires the client to be receptive to teaching; it cannot be methodologically implemented during a life-threatening, imminent danger crisis where safety/blocking is the sole priority.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' forces 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT'. 'IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching During Crisis] Formal differential reinforcement of alternative behaviors requires the client to be receptive to teaching; it cannot be methodologically implemented during a life-threatening, imminent danger crisis where safety/blocking is the sole priority.
* EXC: [IS_PICA] | [Ethical Violation: Harm Prevention in Pica] Using planned ignoring (attention extinction) is an ethical and safety violation when a client is engaging in Pica (swallowing small objects), as active intervention is required to prevent choking or internal injury.
* EXC: [IS_PROXIMITY_PAIRING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Extinction and Proximity Pairing] Planned ignoring requires the strict withholding of attention, which physically and methodologically cannot co-occur with proximity pairing actions like tickles or physical touch.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Methodological Mismatch: Extinction on Regulated Client] Attention extinction (planned ignoring) targets active maladaptive behaviors. Applying it when a client is regulated, cooperative, and displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior is a fundamental methodological error.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction during Respiratory Crisis] Implementing planned ignoring while a client is experiencing a life-threatening or acute medical crisis (asthma, severe respiratory distress) is physically dangerous and severely violates BACB ethical codes regarding client welfare.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction during Sensory Distress] Using planned ignoring to address behaviors stemming directly from acute medical/physiological pain (such as a headache) violates ethical guidelines concerning the management of physiological needs.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_OVERLOAD] | [Ethical Violation: Ignoring Sensory Overload] Utilizing planned ignoring when a client is experiencing physiological sensory overload is ethically and medically contraindicated.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Ethical Violation: Ignoring Severe Behavior] Ignoring severe topographies such as bolting, head-butting, or eye-gouging poses a direct threat to the client's physical safety and violates ethical mandates.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | [Ethical Violation: Ignoring SIB] Planned ignoring cannot be safely or ethically used for Self-Injurious Behavior due to the immediate physical risk of harm to the client.
* EXC: [IS_SOCIAL_PLAY] | [Methodological Mismatch: Ignoring during Social Play] Implementing planned ignoring is methodologically incompatible with facilitating and reinforcing interactive social play and peer turn-taking.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Safety Violation: Ignoring Directed Aggression] Utilizing planned ignoring during active staff-directed aggression (e.g., hitting, biting staff) is a physical safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_TANGIBLE_MAINTAINED] | [Methodological Mismatch: Attention Extinction for Tangibles] Planned ignoring is an extinction procedure for attention-maintained behaviors, making it methodologically incorrect for behaviors maintained by tangible access.
* EXC: [IS_TOXIC_HAZARD] | [Ethical Violation: Harm Prevention Protocol] Implementing attention extinction or planned ignoring when a learner accesses or interacts with toxic household cleaning supplies violates ethical mandates for harm prevention, risking severe chemical exposure or ingestion.
* EXC: [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL] | [Ethical Violation: Severe Safety Hazard] Utilizing planned ignoring when a learner is navigating a parking lot or running into the street is a critical safety and ethical violation, as it actively prevents necessary physical intervention to protect the learner's life from vehicular traffic.

---

## [IS_ATTENTION_MAINTAINED]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Escape Extinction for Attention Function] Applying escape extinction prevents a client from avoiding a demand, which is methodologically illogical for attention-maintained behavior, as the intervention fails to address the functional reinforcer of attention.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_REINFORCER] | [Methodological Mismatch: Escape Reinforcer for Attention Function] Providing an escape reinforcer (e.g., a break) for attention-maintained behavior is methodologically mismatched, as removing the client from the demand environment often removes social interaction, failing to deliver the target reinforcer.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Methodological Mismatch: Medical Crisis vs. Attention Function] A medical crisis (e.g., seizure, acute asthma) is an involuntary physiological event, not an operant behavior maintained by social attention.

---

## [IS_AUDITORY_BLOCKER]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_ELECTRONIC_DEVICE] | [Physical/Environmental Requirement: Auditory Blockers Require Electronic Devices] Providing an auditory blocker (such as playing music, interactive videos, or using active noise-canceling headphones) physically requires an electronic device to generate the blocking media or soundwaves.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_DEMAND] | [Methodological Contradiction: Nullification of Stimulus] Providing a client with noise-canceling headphones (auditory blocker) prevents the auditory stimulus from being experienced, completely negating the mechanism required to simultaneously run a therapeutic demand to 'tolerate loud noises'.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_DISTRESS] | [Physical Impossibility: Medical Harm via Pressure] Placing noise-canceling headphones (auditory blocker) over or inside the ears of a client with an active ear infection places direct physical pressure on inflamed tissue, causing immediate pain and medical harm.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Safety Mismatch: Auditory Blockers during Imminent Danger] Wearing noise-canceling headphones or interacting with music players during imminent danger (running into the street, eloping) prevents the client from hearing crucial safety directives, posing a severe physical risk.
* EXC: [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL] | [Severe Physical Danger: Sensory Blocking in Traffic] Allowing a learner to use auditory blocking devices (such as noise-cancelling headphones) while navigating traffic hazards completely removes their ability to hear approaching vehicles or verbal warnings, posing a critical, life-threatening danger.
* EXC: [IS_VOCAL_VERBAL_TARGET] | [Methodological Impossibility: Sensory Blocking during Vocal Targets] It is methodologically impossible to accurately run precision vocal-verbal targets (such as echoic imitation or conversational turn-taking) while the learner's auditory input is intentionally blocked by noise-cancellation headphones or loud media, as they cannot hear the discriminative stimulus or their own response.

---

## [IS_AUDITORY_DEMAND]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_BLOCKER] | [Methodological Contradiction: Nullification of Stimulus] Providing a client with noise-canceling headphones (auditory blocker) prevents the auditory stimulus from being experienced, completely negating the mechanism required to simultaneously run a therapeutic demand to 'tolerate loud noises'.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Medical Contraindication of Demands] Presenting therapeutic demands to tolerate loud noises to a client actively suffering from an ear infection (auditory distress) physically aggravates their condition, causing pain and violating the ethical mandate to rule out or accommodate medical variables.
* EXC: [IS_CONFIDENTIAL_DISCUSSION] | [Privacy Violation: Confidentiality in Loud Environments] Conducting a confidential discussion in an environment intentionally made loud to run an auditory demand target breaches privacy guidelines and makes verbal exchange physically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | [Ethical Violation: Aversive Sensory Demands During Crisis] Presenting an auditory demand (tolerating loud noise) while a client is in an active crisis (agitated, crying, requiring physical blocks) violates de-escalation protocols.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Loud Noise in a Quiet Room] Running an auditory demand program designed for tolerating loud noises within a designated quiet/dark sensory room is a direct environmental and methodological contradiction.
* EXC: [IS_GI_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Exposure Therapy during Medical Distress] Enforcing auditory demands (tolerating loud noises) while a client is actively suffering from GI distress or physical illness is an ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Safety Mismatch: Auditory Demands during Crisis] Attempting to teach tolerance to loud noises while the client is engaging in imminent danger (gouging eyes, bolting) is methodologically and physically inappropriate.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical Violation: Sensory Demands During Medical Emergency] Subjecting a client to loud noises or demanding tolerance of a loud environment during an active medical emergency is physically unsafe and abusive.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Sensory Demands During Medical Distress] Requiring a client to tolerate loud environments when they are actively suffering from a headache, ear infection, or other acute pain is physically agonizing and an ethical violation of client dignity and care.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Auditory Demand during Respiratory Crisis] Placing clinical demands on a client to tolerate loud noises while they are experiencing an acute asthma or respiratory crisis is medically contraindicated and physically abusive.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Auditory Demand during Sensory Distress] Forcing a client to tolerate loud environments while they are actively suffering from a headache exacerbates physiological pain, violating core ethical guidelines around medical care.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_OVERLOAD] | [Ethical Violation: Demands during Overload] Placing demands for a client to tolerate loud noises or environments during an active sensory overload violates ethical guidelines for harm prevention.

---

## [IS_AUDITORY_DISTRESS]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Medical Neglect] Implementing planned ignoring (attention extinction) for a client who is actively experiencing medical pain or distress from an ear infection constitutes medical neglect and directly violates BACB guidelines requiring the prioritization of medical well-being.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_BLOCKER] | [Physical Impossibility: Medical Harm via Pressure] Placing noise-canceling headphones (auditory blocker) over or inside the ears of a client with an active ear infection places direct physical pressure on inflamed tissue, causing immediate pain and medical harm.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_DEMAND] | [Ethical Violation: Medical Contraindication of Demands] Presenting therapeutic demands to tolerate loud noises to a client actively suffering from an ear infection (auditory distress) physically aggravates their condition, causing pain and violating the ethical mandate to rule out or accommodate medical variables.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | [Ethical Violation: Harm Prevention and Medical Variables] Applying aversive procedures (such as escape extinction or response cost) to behaviors evoked by acute, verifiable medical pain (e.g., an ear infection) is abusive and severely violates ethical codes regarding the accommodation of medical symptoms.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Escape Extinction During Medical Distress] Applying escape extinction to force compliance when a client is experiencing acute medical pain (e.g., an ear infection) is unethical and violates BACB mandates regarding ruling out, accommodating, and treating medical conditions.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Ethical Violation: Timeout for Medical Distress] Utilizing exclusionary timeout as a consequence for behaviors stemming from acute medical distress (e.g., ear infection) is an ethical violation. Medical symptoms must be evaluated and accommodated, not punished.
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Medical Contradiction: Gross Motor Targets during Ear Infection] Implementing gross motor demands like jumping or catching a ball is medically contraindicated when a client has an ear infection, which affects vestibular balance and causes significant pain.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Punishment during Medical Vulnerability] Implementing overcorrection or high-effort punishment on a client actively suffering from the pain and imbalance of an ear infection is an ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EXERTION] | [Medical Contradiction: High Exertion during Ear Infection] High physical exertion (obstacle courses, running) is medically contraindicated for a client experiencing an active ear infection due to vestibular disruption and pain.
* EXC: [IS_NO_MEDICAL] | [Medical Contradiction: Auditory Distress vs No Medical] Reporting an active ear infection (auditory distress) fundamentally contradicts reporting that no medical conditions are present.
* EXC: [IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Restrictive Punishment during Auditory Distress] Applying restrictive punishers or requiring prolonged tactical breaks for a client suffering from acute ear infection pain is abusive and violates medical/ethical boundaries.

---

## [IS_AUDITORY_SENSITIVITY]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Forced Compliance During Sensory Pain] Applying escape extinction to a client whose behavior is triggered by severe sensory pain (e.g., loud noises) forces them to endure an aversive physiological trigger without escape, violating ethical mandates for client dignity and harm prevention.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Ethical Violation: Timeout for Sensory Sensitivity] Placing a client in exclusionary timeout as a consequence for behaviors driven by sensory sensitivity (e.g., triggered by loud noise) is ethically contraindicated, as it punishes a sensory deficit and fails to address the environmental antecedent.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Sensitivities vs Independence] Being triggered by loud noises implies distress and reactivity, which behaviorally contradicts transitioning smoothly and independently with an absence of maladaptive behaviors.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Behavioral Mismatch: Auditory Sensitivity vs Regulated State] A client being actively triggered by loud noises (auditory sensitivity) is in a state of neurological/sensory distress, which physically and behaviorally precludes them from simultaneously being calm, relaxed, and regulated.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Antecedent Contradiction: Auditory Sensitivity and Regulation] Being triggered into dysregulation by a loud noise contradicts an ongoing state of happy transition and extended pairing.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Sensitivity and Smooth Transitions] A client triggered into dysregulation by loud noises cannot simultaneously transition smoothly and happily without resistance.

---

## [IS_AUTOMATIC_MAINTAINED]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Extinction Procedure vs Behavior Function] Withholding social attention (attention extinction) is methodologically ineffective and contradicts Skinnerian principles when applied to behaviors that are automatically maintained by internal sensory feedback, as the behavior does not rely on social mediation.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Escape Extinction for Automatic Function] Escape extinction involves blocking socially-mediated escape from demands. If a behavior is automatically maintained (internally reinforcing), applying escape extinction is methodologically illogical because the reinforcer is not environmentally contingent escape.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_REINFORCER] | [Methodological Mismatch: Escape Reinforcer for Automatic Function] Providing an escape reinforcer (a break from demands) for an automatically maintained behavior is methodologically mismatched, as the behavior relies on internal sensory feedback rather than the removal of external task demands.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Timeout for Automatic Function] Placing a client in an exclusionary timeout for automatically maintained behavior is methodologically contraindicated; isolating the client in a barren environment removes competing stimuli and inadvertently reinforces the internal behavior (e.g., stereotypy).
* EXC: [IS_INTENSIVE_TEACHING] | [Methodological Contradiction: Unstructured Time vs. Intensive Demands] Unstructured time (allowing for automatic/free-operant behaviors) inherently lacks external demands, which directly contradicts the methodology of Intensive Teaching (DTT, Fluency), characterized by continuous, rapid-paced, highly structured demands.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical Mismatch: Unstructured Time During Emergency Protocol] A medical crisis requires immediate, highly structured, emergency intervention and protocol adherence, making it fundamentally incompatible with coding 'unstructured free time.'
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Function Contradiction: Automatic and Staff Directed] Staff-directed aggression is inherently socially mediated and cannot logically be classified as purely automatically maintained.
* EXC: [IS_TANGIBLE_MAINTAINED] | [Function Contradiction: Automatic and Tangible] A single behavioral instance cannot logically be both strictly automatically maintained (non-social) and tangible-maintained (socially mediated).

---

## [IS_AVERSIVE]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT] | [Ethical Dependency: Aversives Require Reinforcement] According to behavior analytic ethical guidelines, the use of aversive or punishment procedures conceptually and ethically requires being paired with a differential reinforcement procedure to build appropriate, functional alternative skills.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Harm Prevention and Medical Variables] Applying aversive procedures (such as escape extinction or response cost) to behaviors evoked by acute, verifiable medical pain (e.g., an ear infection) is abusive and severely violates ethical codes regarding the accommodation of medical symptoms.
* EXC: [IS_CONTAGIOUS] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Illness] Applying aversive procedures (response cost, overcorrection, planned ignoring) to contagious illness symptoms is a direct violation of BACB ethical codes against punishing medical conditions.
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | [Ethical and Safety Violation: Aversives During Crisis] Implementing aversive procedures (e.g., overcorrection, escape extinction) during an active crisis involving physical blocking escalates the danger and violates ethical guidelines prioritizing immediate safety and de-escalation.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Safety Hazard: Unmonitored Aversives] Executing hands-on aversives like positive practice overcorrection in a sensory dark room presents a severe safety risk, as staff lack the visibility required to guide safely and prevent injury.
* EXC: [IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_AVERSIVE' forces 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT'. 'IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching During Crisis] Formal differential reinforcement of alternative behaviors requires the client to be receptive to teaching; it cannot be methodologically implemented during a life-threatening, imminent danger crisis where safety/blocking is the sole priority.
* EXC: [IS_ELOPEMENT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_AVERSIVE' forces 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT'. 'IS_ELOPEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching During Crisis] Formal differential reinforcement of alternative behaviors requires the client to be receptive to teaching; it cannot be methodologically implemented during a life-threatening, imminent danger crisis where safety/blocking is the sole priority.
* EXC: [IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_AVERSIVE' forces 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT'. 'IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching During Crisis] Formal differential reinforcement of alternative behaviors requires the client to be receptive to teaching; it cannot be methodologically implemented during a life-threatening, imminent danger crisis where safety/blocking is the sole priority.
* EXC: [IS_GI_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Aversives during Medical Distress] Implementing aversive procedures like escape extinction or response cost while the client is physically suffering from GI distress is highly unethical.
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Ethical Violation: Aversives on Self-Injury] Applying extinction or aversive procedures like planned ignoring to self-injurious behavior (biting hands, scratching) poses an unethical risk of tissue damage.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Safety Violation: Aversives during Imminent Crisis] Applying planned ignoring or response cost during an imminent danger crisis (like bolting into the street or gouging eyes) prevents necessary physical safety interventions and is ethically prohibited.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Punishing Independence] Applying aversive procedures (punishment, extinction) to a client who is behaving independently and displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior is methodologically and ethically prohibited.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical Violation: Aversives During Medical Crisis] Applying aversive behavioral procedures (such as overcorrection, response cost, or escape extinction) while a client is suffering a physiological emergency is highly abusive and medically dangerous.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Aversives During Pain and Distress] Implementing aversives when a client is in active medical distress (dental pain, ear infection, illness) directly violates the BACB ethical mandate to rule out and address medical/biological variables before applying behavioral reduction procedures.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing a Medical Symptom] Applying aversive procedures, such as escape extinction or overcorrection, to a client suffering from dental pain (oral distress) violates BACB ethical guidelines that mandate addressing underlying medical or physiological pain before implementing behavioral reductive procedures.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_AVERSIVE' forces 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT'. 'IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching During Crisis] Formal differential reinforcement of alternative behaviors requires the client to be receptive to teaching; it cannot be methodologically implemented during a life-threatening, imminent danger crisis where safety/blocking is the sole priority.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_AVERSIVE' forces 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT'. 'IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching During Crisis] Formal differential reinforcement of alternative behaviors requires the client to be receptive to teaching; it cannot be methodologically implemented during a life-threatening, imminent danger crisis where safety/blocking is the sole priority.
* EXC: [IS_PICA] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_AVERSIVE' forces 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT'. 'IS_PICA' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching During Crisis] Formal differential reinforcement of alternative behaviors requires the client to be receptive to teaching; it cannot be methodologically implemented during a life-threatening, imminent danger crisis where safety/blocking is the sole priority.
* EXC: [IS_PROXIMITY_PAIRING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Aversives and Proximity Pairing] Simultaneously applying aversive interventions (e.g., response cost, overcorrection) and providing positive proximity pairing (e.g., tickles, deep pressure) presents a direct Skinnerian contradiction of concurrent punishment and reinforcement.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Regulated Behavior] Applying aversive or punishing procedures (e.g., overcorrection, response cost, escape extinction) to a regulated, cooperative client represents a strict ethical and methodological violation.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Aversives during Respiratory Crisis] Applying aversive interventions (e.g., overcorrection, escape extinction) during a respiratory emergency is medically hazardous, physically dangerous, and strictly unethical.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Aversives during Sensory Distress] Subjecting a client to aversive punishment procedures while they are actively suffering from medical pain (e.g., headache) is a severe ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_OVERLOAD] | [Ethical Violation: Aversives during Overload] Applying aversive procedures during active sensory overload is ethically abusive, as it penalizes physiological dysregulation.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Ethical Violation: Aversives during Severe Topography] Implementing aversive procedures such as planned ignoring or escape extinction during severe topography (e.g., eye-gouging, bolting) is a severe safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | [Ethical Violation: Aversives for SIB] Utilizing aversive procedures like planned ignoring or escape extinction for self-injurious behavior is ethically contraindicated due to immediate safety risks.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Methodological Contradiction: Aversives and Regulation] Implementing aversive procedures such as overcorrection or response cost is physically contradictory to a state of quick control and happy transition.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Aversives and Transitions] Aversives like overcorrection or escape extinction imply resistance or problem behavior, physically contradicting a smooth transition without resistance.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Safety Violation: Aversives during Directed Aggression] Implementing planned ignoring or response cost during active, staff-directed aggression presents a major physical safety risk.
* EXC: [IS_TOXIC_HAZARD] | [Ethical Violation: Chemical Safety Risk] Using aversive procedures like escape extinction or planned ignoring while a learner handles household cleaning supplies represents a severe ethical and safety hazard, risking chemical burns or ingestion.
* EXC: [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Aversive Procedures in Traffic] Enforcing aversive procedures such as escape extinction or overcorrection in the middle of an active traffic hazard (e.g., a street or parking lot) exposes the learner to immediate vehicular danger and restricts necessary life-saving movements.

---

## [IS_BCBA_LEVEL_TASK]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | [Safety Priority: Consultation During Crisis] A BCBA cannot safely or ethically conduct parent coaching, review data graphs, or discuss IEP goals while actively managing a behavioral crisis requiring physical blocks or tactical breaks.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Clinical Review in Sensory Room] Conducting BCBA-level tasks such as reviewing IEP goals, data graphs, or engaging in parent coaching inside a dark sensory room is methodologically and practically unfeasible.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Safety Violation: Administrative Tasks during Crisis] A BCBA cannot methodologically or safely engage in administrative tasks like reviewing IEP goals or discussing strategies while the client is actively in an imminent danger state.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical/Methodological Violation: Administrative Tasks During Crisis] Routine administrative or pedagogical BCBA tasks (e.g., reviewing IEP goals, discussing theoretical generalization strategies) must be suspended during an active medical crisis, which demands immediate, emergency health and safety interventions.

---

## [IS_BIO_HAZARD]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Medical Neglect and Harm Prevention] Applying planned ignoring (attention extinction) to dangerous bio-hazards such as pica, scatolia, or disrobing is a severe ethical violation that places the client at imminent medical risk.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Safety Hazard: Management of Bio-Hazards] Managing severe bio-hazards such as scatolia (fecal smearing) or spitting requires full visual capability for proper sanitation and medical safety. Conducting such management in a dark environment is extremely hazardous and physically impractical.
* EXC: [IS_EDIBLE] | [Medical Hazard: Edible Consumption During Bio-Hazard] Providing or consuming edibles while a client is actively engaging in unsanitary bio-hazardous behaviors (e.g., fecal smearing, licking the floor) presents a direct and severe medical risk of pathogen ingestion and illness.
* EXC: [IS_FRAGMENTABLE_MATERIAL] | [Safety Hazard: Ingestion Risk] Providing fragmentable materials like playdough or kinetic sand to a client exhibiting bio-hazardous behaviors like pica or mouthing non-edible items poses a severe ingestion and toxicity risk.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Contradiction: Maladaptive Behavior vs Independence] A client exhibiting severe bio-hazardous behaviors (pica, scatolia, disrobing) cannot simultaneously be demonstrating an absence of maladaptive behavior or transitioning smoothly and independently.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Physical Impossibility: Voluntary Biohazard during Incapacitating Medical Crisis] Active, voluntary biohazard behaviors, such as intentionally stripping completely naked, pica, or smearing feces, are physically impossible for a client to execute while simultaneously incapacitated by a severe medical event like a seizure.
* EXC: [IS_PROXIMITY_PAIRING] | [Infection Control: Bio-Hazard and Proximity Pairing] Engaging in proximity pairing activities (such as tickles, holding hands, or deep pressure) during active bio-hazardous behaviors (like spitting, fecal smearing, or pica) poses a severe hygiene and infection control risk.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Methodological Contradiction: Severe Maladaptive vs Regulated State] Bio-hazardous behaviors represent severe, active maladaptive occurrences, which methodologically contradicts the definition of a regulated state (characterized by calm behavior and the absence of maladaptive behaviors).
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Regulated vs Severe Bio-Hazard] Severe biological hazards, such as fecal smearing, pica, and stripping completely naked, are indicators of extreme dysregulation that logically cannot co-occur with a state of being highly motivated, cooperative, and demonstrating quick instructional control.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Smooth Transition vs Bio-Hazard] Engaging in extreme bio-hazardous behaviors (spitting at staff, smearing feces, disrobing) inherently involves severe non-compliance and physically contradicts transitioning smoothly, happily, and without resistance.

---

## [IS_CAREGIVER_ENGAGEMENT]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_CAREGIVER_UNAVAILABLE] | [Logical Impossibility: Caregiver Presence] It is logically and physically impossible for a caregiver to be actively engaged in session activities (e.g., coaching, reviewing data) while simultaneously being documented as unavailable or absent.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Caregiver Engagement During Timeout] Exclusionary timeout requires the temporary removal of all social reinforcement and attention. Caregiver engagement involves active social interaction and coaching, which strictly contradicts the methodology of timeout.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Ethical Violation: Distraction During Crisis] Engaging in caregiver coaching, reviewing data, or general discussion during an imminent safety crisis (elopement, severe SIB) represents severe clinical negligence and a failure to prioritize client safety.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical Violation: Caregiver Engagement during Medical Crisis] Engaging caregivers in routine program reviews, discussing mastered skills, or analyzing data graphs while the client is experiencing an active, life-threatening medical emergency is an extreme safety and ethical violation.

---

## [IS_CAREGIVER_UNAVAILABLE]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_CAREGIVER_ENGAGEMENT] | [Logical Impossibility: Caregiver Presence] It is logically and physically impossible for a caregiver to be actively engaged in session activities (e.g., coaching, reviewing data) while simultaneously being documented as unavailable or absent.
* EXC: [IS_CONFIDENTIAL_DISCUSSION] | [Logical Contradiction: Confidential Family Discussion] Confidential discussions, such as reviewing data graphs with family or discussing IEP goals, inherently require the presence of a caregiver or responsible adult. This physically cannot co-occur with the caregiver being unavailable.
* EXC: [IS_HOME_SETTING] | [Ethical Violation: Unsupervised Home Session] BACB and standard funding guidelines strictly prohibit conducting in-home therapy sessions when a responsible caregiver or adult is unavailable.
* EXC: [IS_REMOTE] | [Ethical Violation: Telehealth Supervision Requirements] Conducting a remote telehealth session without a responsible adult or caregiver available for a minor or dependent client is a significant clinical safety and ethical violation.

---

## [IS_CHAINED]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Chained Task During Timeout] Exclusionary timeout mandates the immediate cessation of all instructional demands and reinforcement. Engaging a client in a programmed chained task (e.g., daily living skills) during this time is methodologically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching ADLs During Crisis] It is methodologically invalid and physically unsafe to conduct chained daily living activities (e.g., tying shoes, hygiene routines) while a client is in imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Safety Violation: Chained ADLs during Medical Crisis] Prompting a client to perform multi-step activities of daily living (such as brushing teeth, dressing, or packing a bag) during an active seizure or respiratory crisis is physically impossible and highly unsafe.

---

## [IS_CHAINING_FORMAT]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_CHAINED] | [Methodological Dependency: Chaining Format] Chaining intervention formats (such as Forward Chaining, Backward Chaining, Total Task Chaining, and Task Analysis) are systematically designed to teach complex, multi-step routines. Applying a chaining methodology clinically and logically requires a chained target behavior (e.g., ADLs, vocational routines) to break down and teach.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' forces 'IS_CHAINED'. 'IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINED' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching ADLs During Crisis] It is methodologically invalid and physically unsafe to conduct chained daily living activities (e.g., tying shoes, hygiene routines) while a client is in imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_DISCRETE] | [Methodological Mismatch: Skill Complexity and Teaching Format] Discrete skills involve single, indivisible responses (e.g., echoic imitation, pointing) taught via Discrete Trial Training. It violates behavioral methodology to apply chaining formats (forward, backward, task analysis), which are exclusively designed for multi-step chained sequences, to a discrete skill.
* EXC: [IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' forces 'IS_CHAINED'. 'IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINED' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching ADLs During Crisis] It is methodologically invalid and physically unsafe to conduct chained daily living activities (e.g., tying shoes, hygiene routines) while a client is in imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_ELOPEMENT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' forces 'IS_CHAINED'. 'IS_ELOPEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINED' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching ADLs During Crisis] It is methodologically invalid and physically unsafe to conduct chained daily living activities (e.g., tying shoes, hygiene routines) while a client is in imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' forces 'IS_CHAINED'. 'IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINED' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching ADLs During Crisis] It is methodologically invalid and physically unsafe to conduct chained daily living activities (e.g., tying shoes, hygiene routines) while a client is in imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Chaining Format During Timeout] Chaining formats (e.g., forward/backward chaining, shaping) require active instruction and reinforcement, which directly violates the methodological requirement of removing all demands during exclusionary timeout.
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' forces 'IS_CHAINED'. 'IS_HAND_COMPROMISE' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINED' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching ADLs During Crisis] It is methodologically invalid and physically unsafe to conduct chained daily living activities (e.g., tying shoes, hygiene routines) while a client is in imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Methodological Mismatch: Chaining Formats during Medical Crisis] It is methodologically, physically, and ethically impossible to conduct shaping or chaining instructional formats during a life-threatening medical crisis such as a seizure or severe asthma attack.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' forces 'IS_CHAINED'. 'IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINED' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching ADLs During Crisis] It is methodologically invalid and physically unsafe to conduct chained daily living activities (e.g., tying shoes, hygiene routines) while a client is in imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' forces 'IS_CHAINED'. 'IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINED' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching ADLs During Crisis] It is methodologically invalid and physically unsafe to conduct chained daily living activities (e.g., tying shoes, hygiene routines) while a client is in imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_PICA] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' forces 'IS_CHAINED'. 'IS_PICA' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINED' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching ADLs During Crisis] It is methodologically invalid and physically unsafe to conduct chained daily living activities (e.g., tying shoes, hygiene routines) while a client is in imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' forces 'IS_CHAINED'. 'IS_SIB' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINED' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching ADLs During Crisis] It is methodologically invalid and physically unsafe to conduct chained daily living activities (e.g., tying shoes, hygiene routines) while a client is in imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' forces 'IS_CHAINED'. 'IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINED' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching ADLs During Crisis] It is methodologically invalid and physically unsafe to conduct chained daily living activities (e.g., tying shoes, hygiene routines) while a client is in imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Active Instructional Chaining vs. Demand Removal] Active instructional chaining formats (Forward Chaining, Backward Chaining, Total Task Analysis) require systematic, step-by-step prompting and instruction, which is methodologically incompatible with a Wait Out procedure that explicitly involves the deliberate removal of demands and cessation of prompting.

## [IS_CHOKING_HAZARD]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Lethal Harm Prevention] Using planned ignoring when a client is actively engaging in a choking hazard (e.g., swallowing small objects) is a lethal medical and ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Safety Hazard: Visibility of Choking Risks] Providing or allowing access to choking hazards (e.g., small beads, tokens) in a dark environment represents a severe physical and medical safety risk, as staff cannot adequately visually monitor the client to prevent ingestion or asphyxiation.
* EXC: [IS_FALL_HAZARD] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Choking Hazard During Fall Hazard] Permitting a client to interact with small objects (choking hazards) while simultaneously engaging in gross motor fall hazards (jumping, swinging) creates an extreme and preventable risk of aspiration and bodily injury.
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Physical Safety: Aspiration Risk During Motor Tasks] Allowing a client to hold or mouth choking hazards (tokens, beads) while engaging in gross motor targets (jumping, obstacle courses) creates an extreme risk of aspiration and choking.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EXERTION] | [Physical Safety: Aspiration Risk During Exertion] High physical exertion (running, rapid pacing) combined with access to choking hazards creates an extreme, immediate risk of accidental ingestion or aspiration.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Safety Violation: Choking Hazard during Medical Crisis] Introducing or failing to remove choking hazards near a client who is actively experiencing an incapacitating medical event, such as a seizure or respiratory distress, is a critical and life-threatening safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Medical Safety: Aspiration Risk] Exposing a client to choking hazards (such as small objects or kinetic sand) while they are experiencing active respiratory distress (e.g., asthma attacks) presents an acute medical risk of aspiration.

---

## [IS_CLINIC_SETTING]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_CONTAGIOUS] | [Ethical and Medical Violation: Contagion Risk in Clinic] It is an ethical and medical violation of health and safety protocols to provide clinic-based services to a client actively presenting with contagious illness symptoms, as it recklessly exposes other vulnerable clients and clinical staff to infectious disease.
* EXC: [IS_HOME_SETTING] | [Physical Impossibility: Simultaneous Distinct Locations] A session or specific event cannot physically occur in a clinical facility and a home setting simultaneously.
* EXC: [IS_PUBLIC] | [Physical Impossibility: Clinic vs Public Setting] A specialized clinical facility is an indoor, controlled environment, making it physically impossible to simultaneously occur in an open, public community setting like a store or playground.
* EXC: [IS_REMOTE] | [Physical Impossibility: Clinic vs Telehealth] An in-person clinical setting is physically and methodologically mutually exclusive from a remote telehealth session.
* EXC: [IS_SCHOOL_SETTING] | [Physical Impossibility: Clinic vs School Setting] A specialized clinic facility is physically distinct from and mutually exclusive to a school or classroom setting.

---

## [IS_CONFIDENTIAL_DISCUSSION]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ACADEMIC] | [Methodological Mismatch: Demands During Consultation] Running discrete academic trials effectively is methodologically impossible while simultaneously engaged in a confidential discussion (e.g., reviewing IEP goals, parent meetings).
* EXC: [IS_ADL_TARGET] | [Methodological Mismatch: ADLs During Consultation] Teaching complex, chained ADL routines requires close supervision and prompting, which cannot be done while simultaneously conducting a confidential meeting or review.
* EXC: [IS_AGGRESSION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Consultation During Aggression] Attempting to conduct confidential reviews (IEP goals, parent meetings) during active client aggression is clinically irresponsible and methodologically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_DEMAND] | [Privacy Violation: Confidentiality in Loud Environments] Conducting a confidential discussion in an environment intentionally made loud to run an auditory demand target breaches privacy guidelines and makes verbal exchange physically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_CAREGIVER_UNAVAILABLE] | [Logical Contradiction: Confidential Family Discussion] Confidential discussions, such as reviewing data graphs with family or discussing IEP goals, inherently require the presence of a caregiver or responsible adult. This physically cannot co-occur with the caregiver being unavailable.
* EXC: [IS_ELOPEMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Divided Attention During Elopement] Conducting a confidential discussion requires divided attention, which is a critical safety violation when a client is actively eloping and requires immediate, undivided physical tracking and intervention.
* EXC: [IS_FALL_HAZARD] | [Ethical Violation: Divided Attention During Fall Hazard] Engaging in a confidential discussion diverts practitioner attention, creating a severe physical safety risk when a client is simultaneously navigating gross motor fall hazards (e.g., swinging, obstacle courses).
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_MOBILITY_ENVIRONMENT] | [Ethical Violation: HIPAA/Confidentiality Breach] Discussing confidential IEP goals, behavior incidents, or data graphs in public, high-mobility environments (outdoors, playgrounds) violates HIPAA and privacy guidelines.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Ethical Violation: Neglecting Active Crisis] Attempting to conduct confidential discussions about data or schedules while the client is exhibiting behaviors that pose imminent danger is negligent and unsafe.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical Violation: Confidential Discussion during Medical Crisis] Engaging in confidential administrative discussions, such as reviewing data logs or IEP goals, while the client is suffering from an active, life-threatening medical emergency is an extreme ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_PUBLIC] | [Ethical Violation: HIPAA Privacy in Public Spaces] Openly discussing confidential client data, IEP goals, or behavior incidents in public community spaces (such as stores or playgrounds) violates HIPAA and BACB confidentiality mandates.
* EXC: [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL] | [Ethical Violation: Compromised Supervision During Traffic Hazard] Engaging in highly distracting, confidential administrative discussions (like reviewing data graphs or IEP goals) while actively managing a traffic hazard or practicing safety crossing is a severe ethical and safety violation due to compromised supervision in a life-threatening environment.

---

## [IS_CONFINED]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED] | [Physical Impossibility: Spatial Distance Limitations] Confined spaces such as vehicles or small restrooms do not provide the necessary square footage to physically allow staff to safely maintain a required physical safety distance from a client.
* EXC: [IS_FALL_HAZARD] | [Physical Impossibility: Fall Hazard in Confined Space] Confined spaces (e.g., vehicles, restrooms) lack the spatial capacity and safety infrastructure required for gross motor fall hazard activities like playgrounds, swinging, or obstacle courses, making their combination physically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_FOOD_PREP_AREA] | [Physical Impossibility: Confined Space vs Kitchen] A client cannot simultaneously be in a confined space (vehicle, bathroom) and a food preparation area (kitchen, dining room).
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Physical Safety: Gross Motor in Confined Space] Attempting gross motor targets like jumping, catching balls, or obstacle courses within a confined space like a vehicle or bathroom poses a severe risk of injury.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EXERTION] | [Physical Safety: High Exertion in Confined Space] Engaging in high-exertion behaviors (running, rapid pacing) within tightly confined spaces (vehicles, bathrooms) poses a direct physical hazard to the client and therapist.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_MOBILITY_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical Impossibility: Confined vs High Mobility] It is physically impossible for an environment to be simultaneously a tightly confined space (bathroom/vehicle) and a broad high-mobility environment (playground/outdoors).

---

## [IS_CONTAGIOUS]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Medical Symptoms] Applying attention extinction (ignoring) to verifiable contagious illness symptoms constitutes medical neglect and violates BACB ethical guidelines regarding client welfare.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Illness] Applying aversive procedures (response cost, overcorrection, planned ignoring) to contagious illness symptoms is a direct violation of BACB ethical codes against punishing medical conditions.
* EXC: [IS_CLINIC_SETTING] | [Ethical and Medical Violation: Contagion Risk in Clinic] It is an ethical and medical violation of health and safety protocols to provide clinic-based services to a client actively presenting with contagious illness symptoms, as it recklessly exposes other vulnerable clients and clinical staff to infectious disease.
* EXC: [IS_FOOD_PREP_AREA] | [Health Code Violation: Illness in Food Prep Area] Permitting a contagious client with active illness symptoms to be in a food preparation or dining area violates basic health, safety, and sanitation protocols.
* EXC: [IS_FRAGMENTABLE_MATERIAL] | [Health Code Violation: Contaminated Permeable Materials] Providing highly permeable and non-sanitizable materials (playdough, kinetic sand) to a contagious individual is a severe infection control violation.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Illness] Implementing high-effort punishment (like overcorrection or positive practice) while a client is suffering from contagious illness symptoms is medically contraindicated and highly unethical.
