* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EXERTION] | [Medical Contraindication: Exertion While Ill] Forcing or allowing high physical exertion for a client actively exhibiting contagious illness symptoms poses an acute medical and physiological risk.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_MOBILITY_ENVIRONMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Public Infection Risk] Taking a contagious client with active illness symptoms into public high-mobility environments (e.g., playgrounds) poses an unethical infection risk to the broader community.
* EXC: [IS_NO_MEDICAL] | [Logical Contradiction: Medical Status] It is logically contradictory to document that a client has active contagious illness symptoms while simultaneously reporting that there are no medical issues or symptoms present.
* EXC: [IS_PEER_TARGET] | [Ethical Violation: Infection Control] Facilitating or targeting interactions with non-sibling peers (e.g., sharing items, cooperative play, turn-taking) when a client is actively exhibiting contagious illness symptoms is a direct violation of standard health, safety, and infection control policies.
* EXC: [IS_PROXIMITY_PAIRING] | [Medical Safety: Infection Control during Pairing] Engaging in close physical proximity pairing (e.g., holding hands, physical touch) with an actively contagious client violates medical infection control standards.
* EXC: [IS_PUBLIC] | [Medical Safety: Public Health Risk] Taking a client actively presenting contagious illness symptoms into a public community setting poses a severe public health and transmission risk.
* EXC: [IS_SCHOOL_SETTING] | [Medical Safety: School Transmission Risk] Bringing a contagious client into a school setting introduces a direct disease transmission risk to peers and staff, violating basic medical safety protocols.
* EXC: [IS_SOCIAL_PLAY] | [Ethical Violation: Infection Control] It is a severe ethical and health safety violation to facilitate close-proximity social play, sharing of items, and peer interaction when a client is actively presenting contagious illness symptoms.

---

## [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Ethical and Methodological Dependency] In a clinical ABA context, implementing crisis interventions (such as environmental clearing or physical blocking) ethically and methodologically requires the presence of an imminent danger to self or others.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ACADEMIC] | [Ethical Violation: Demands During Crisis] Presenting academic demands during an active behavioral crisis involving physical blocking or tactical breaks violates de-escalation protocols.
* EXC: [IS_ACADEMIC_MATERIAL] | [Methodological Contradiction: Materials During Crisis] Academic materials must be cleared during a physical crisis for safety; attempting to utilize them during an active block or crisis is methodologically incorrect.
* EXC: [IS_ADL_TARGET] | [Ethical Violation: ADL Demands During Crisis] Running ADL demands (e.g., forcing a toileting routine or teeth brushing) during an active crisis indicator escalates the crisis and violates safety protocols.
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Methodological Contradiction: Crisis Management] Planned ignoring cannot be implemented during an active crisis indicator that requires physical intervention (e.g., physical blocking to prevent exit), as blocking inherently breaks the extinction procedure.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_DEMAND] | [Ethical Violation: Aversive Sensory Demands During Crisis] Presenting an auditory demand (tolerating loud noise) while a client is in an active crisis (agitated, crying, requiring physical blocks) violates de-escalation protocols.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | [Ethical and Safety Violation: Aversives During Crisis] Implementing aversive procedures (e.g., overcorrection, escape extinction) during an active crisis involving physical blocking escalates the danger and violates ethical guidelines prioritizing immediate safety and de-escalation.
* EXC: [IS_BCBA_LEVEL_TASK] | [Safety Priority: Consultation During Crisis] A BCBA cannot safely or ethically conduct parent coaching, review data graphs, or discuss IEP goals while actively managing a behavioral crisis requiring physical blocks or tactical breaks.
* EXC: [IS_CHAINING_FORMAT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' forces 'IS_CHAINED'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINED' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching ADLs During Crisis] It is methodologically invalid and physically unsafe to conduct chained daily living activities (e.g., tying shoes, hygiene routines) while a client is in imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Safety Hazard: Crisis Intervention Visibility] Implementing physical crisis interventions, such as environmental clearing or physical blocking for an agitated client, within a dark environment removes necessary visual monitoring, creating a severe physical danger for both the client and staff.
* EXC: [IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED] | [Physical Impossibility: Proximity vs. Distance] Crisis indicators such as physical blocking or using safety pads require immediate, close-proximity physical contact with the client, which is physically impossible to execute while simultaneously maintaining a required safety distance.
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Educational Tutoring During Active Crisis] A client in an active crisis state requiring physical safety blocks or tactical breaks is neurologically dysregulated, making strict academic readiness and educational tutoring demands ethically and methodologically inappropriate.
* EXC: [IS_EXECUTIVE_FUNCTIONING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Executive Functioning During Active Crisis] Executive functioning tasks demand high cognitive load and self-regulation, which is neurologically and methodologically incompatible with a client in an active behavioral crisis requiring physical blocking.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE' forces 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' cannot co-occur: [Ethical Violation: Extinction during Imminent Danger] Using planned ignoring for behaviors that present an imminent danger (e.g., elopement, head-banging, gouging eyes) is a severe ethical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_FINE_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Fine Motor Tools During Active Crisis] It is an extreme physical safety hazard to present fine motor tools (e.g., scissors, pencils) while a client is in an active crisis requiring physical blocking or incident reporting.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Contradiction: Crisis vs Independent Functioning] A client cannot simultaneously trigger a crisis indicator (requiring physical blocks or incident reports) while demonstrating an absence of maladaptive behavior and independent functioning.
* EXC: [IS_INTENSIVE_TEACHING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Intensive Teaching during Behavioral Crisis] Continuing highly structured, demand-heavy Intensive Teaching (such as max prompting or DTT) while a client is in an escalated behavioral crisis requiring physical blocks or tactical breaks is a methodological and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical Violation: Behavioral Restraint during Medical Crisis] Applying physical behavioral restraints (such as safety pads or physical blocking) during an involuntary neurological event like a seizure is contraindicated and represents an extreme medical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_NET_TEACHING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Natural Environment Teaching during Behavioral Crisis] Natural Environment Teaching requires a calm, engaged, and child-led state. Attempting NET while a client is in an active behavioral crisis that necessitates physical restraints or tactical breaks is methodologically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_PROXIMITY_PAIRING] | [Methodological Contradiction: Crisis vs Physical Pairing] Attempting physical proximity pairing (such as tickles or hand-holding) during an acute crisis requiring safety blocks or environmental clearing is unsafe and methodologically invalid.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Methodological Contradiction: Crisis vs Regulated State] A client actively presenting crisis indicators (e.g., agitated, requiring physical blocking) cannot simultaneously be in a calm, regulated state devoid of maladaptive behaviors.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Safety Hazard: Acute Crisis Escalation] It is a profound safety and ethical violation to provide a client with sharp objects (e.g., scissors) while they are exhibiting active crisis indicators (e.g., agitated crying, requiring physical blocking), as this creates an immediate, high risk of severe self-injury or harm to others.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Crisis vs Regulation] A client requiring active crisis intervention (e.g., physical blocking, tactical breaks, agitated crying) is in an acute state of distress, which physically precludes them from simultaneously being categorized as happily regulated or under quick instructional control.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Crisis vs Smooth Transition] Active crisis indicators, such as physical blocking to prevent elopement or agitated crying, represent acute resistance and distress, making it physically impossible to simultaneously transition smoothly and independently without resistance.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_INDEPENDENT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Independence] Active states of imminent danger (severe aggression, SIB, elopement) strictly contradict the definition of independence, which mandates safe navigation and a complete absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_TOILETING_ADL' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.

---

## [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_INDOOR_SETTING] | [Physical/Environmental Requirement] Creating a controlled dark environment (such as a sensory dark room) physically requires an indoor setting to effectively block out ambient light.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ACADEMIC] | [Physical Impossibility: Visual Tasks in the Dark] It is physically impossible to conduct visual academic tasks (e.g., identifying colors, tracing letters, completing worksheets) in a dark sensory room.
* EXC: [IS_ACADEMIC_MATERIAL] | [Physical Impossibility: Visual Materials in the Dark] Using visual academic materials (worksheets, 2D picture cards) in a dark sensory room is physically impossible due to lack of visibility.
* EXC: [IS_ADL_TARGET] | [Safety Hazard: Unsupervised ADLs in the Dark] Teaching self-care and ADL targets (toileting, dressing, brushing teeth) in a dark room creates an extreme safety hazard and is physically impractical.
* EXC: [IS_AGGRESSION] | [Severe Safety Hazard: Aggression in Zero Visibility] Active aggression (hitting, kicking, punching) occurring in a dark sensory room creates a critical safety hazard, as staff cannot see to block strikes safely or evade.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_DEMAND] | [Methodological Contradiction: Loud Noise in a Quiet Room] Running an auditory demand program designed for tolerating loud noises within a designated quiet/dark sensory room is a direct environmental and methodological contradiction.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | [Safety Hazard: Unmonitored Aversives] Executing hands-on aversives like positive practice overcorrection in a sensory dark room presents a severe safety risk, as staff lack the visibility required to guide safely and prevent injury.
* EXC: [IS_BCBA_LEVEL_TASK] | [Methodological Mismatch: Clinical Review in Sensory Room] Conducting BCBA-level tasks such as reviewing IEP goals, data graphs, or engaging in parent coaching inside a dark sensory room is methodologically and practically unfeasible.
* EXC: [IS_BIO_HAZARD] | [Safety Hazard: Management of Bio-Hazards] Managing severe bio-hazards such as scatolia (fecal smearing) or spitting requires full visual capability for proper sanitation and medical safety. Conducting such management in a dark environment is extremely hazardous and physically impractical.
* EXC: [IS_CHOKING_HAZARD] | [Safety Hazard: Visibility of Choking Risks] Providing or allowing access to choking hazards (e.g., small beads, tokens) in a dark environment represents a severe physical and medical safety risk, as staff cannot adequately visually monitor the client to prevent ingestion or asphyxiation.
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | [Safety Hazard: Crisis Intervention Visibility] Implementing physical crisis interventions, such as environmental clearing or physical blocking for an agitated client, within a dark environment removes necessary visual monitoring, creating a severe physical danger for both the client and staff.
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | [Physical Impossibility: Educational Tutoring in Dark Environment] Educational tutoring tasks (e.g., worksheets, tracing letters) require adequate lighting for visual discrimination, making them physically impossible to conduct in a strictly dark sensory environment.
* EXC: [IS_FALL_HAZARD] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Fall Hazard in Dark Environment] Engaging in gross motor fall hazards (e.g., swinging, jumping, obstacle courses) in a dark environment completely removes visual spotting capabilities, constituting an extreme physical safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_FINE_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Fine Motor Target in Dark Environment] Precision fine motor tasks (e.g., cutting with scissors, tracing) require clear visual feedback and supervision, making them physically impossible and medically unsafe to perform in a dark environment.
* EXC: [IS_FOOD_PREP_AREA] | [Safety Hazard: Food Prep in the Dark] Conducting activities in a food preparation area or kitchen within a dark environment is an extreme physical safety hazard.
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Safety Hazard: Gross Motor in the Dark] Running gross motor targets such as obstacle courses, jumping, or catching a ball in a sensory dark room creates a direct tripping and injury hazard.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Punitive Solitary Confinement] Enforcing high-effort punishment procedures (overcorrection) inside a dark room mirrors abusive solitary confinement practices and violates BACB ethical standards.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EXERTION] | [Safety Hazard: High Exertion in the Dark] Engaging in high exertion (rapid pacing, running) within a dark environment removes necessary visual feedback, creating a critical collision or tripping hazard.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_MOBILITY_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical Impossibility: Dark Sensory vs Outdoors] A setting cannot simultaneously be a controlled, indoor sensory dark room and a public, high-mobility outdoor environment like a playground.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Safety Hazard: Unmonitored Crisis] It is physically unsafe to manage a client in an imminent danger crisis (SIB, aggression) in a dark environment where clinicians cannot see to safely block or prevent injury.
* EXC: [IS_INTENSIVE_TEACHING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Intensive Teaching in Dark Environment] Intensive Teaching (DTT) relies on the rapid and clear presentation of visual and auditory discriminative stimuli, which is methodologically contradictory to the sensory-reduction purpose of a dark environment.
* EXC: [IS_PUBLIC] | [Physical Impossibility: Sensory Room in Public Space] A controlled sensory break requiring a dark, quiet room physically cannot be conducted in open, public areas such as playgrounds or stores.
* EXC: [IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Seclusion Risk] Implementing restrictive punishments (such as a tactical break or overcorrection) in a confined dark room constitutes seclusion, which is a severe ethical and legal violation under BACB guidelines.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Safety Hazard: Visual Impairment] It is a critical physical safety hazard to provide or allow the use of sharp objects (e.g., scissors) in a darkened environment designed for sensory breaks, as reduced visibility drastically increases the risk of severe lacerations.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | [Ethical and Physical Violation: Inappropriate Toileting Environment] Completing toileting ADLs in a designated dark sensory room is physically, hygienically, and ethically inappropriate; these routines require a functional bathroom environment with adequate lighting, plumbing, and sanitation.
* EXC: [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL] | [Physical Impossibility: Mutually Exclusive Environments] A designated dark sensory room and an active traffic hazard (such as a street or parking lot) are completely distinct, mutually exclusive physical environments that cannot be occupied simultaneously.
* EXC: [IS_VISUAL_DEPENDENT_TASK] | [Methodological Mismatch: Visual Acuity Requirement] It is physically and methodologically impossible to perform tasks requiring accurate visual discrimination (such as tracing letters, identifying colors, matching items, or reading worksheets) in a dark sensory room devoid of sufficient lighting.
* EXC: [IS_VOCATIONAL] | [Physical and Methodological Mismatch: Environmental Constraints for Vocational Tasks] Vocational tasks such as clocking in, completing assembly tasks, sorting mail, and following visual checklists require functional lighting and cannot be physically or methodologically executed in a dark sensory room.

---

## [IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Differential Reinforcement During Timeout] Differential reinforcement involves actively delivering reinforcement for alternative behaviors. This is methodologically incompatible with exclusionary timeout, which requires the complete withholding of all reinforcement.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching During Crisis] Formal differential reinforcement of alternative behaviors requires the client to be receptive to teaching; it cannot be methodologically implemented during a life-threatening, imminent danger crisis where safety/blocking is the sole priority.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical Violation: Differential Reinforcement during Medical Crisis] Attempting to apply behavioral reinforcement schedules while a client is actively experiencing a severe medical emergency (like a seizure or respiratory failure) is physically and ethically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Differential Reinforcement of Medical Distress] Applying behavioral differential reinforcement schedules to suppress or treat behaviors that are direct manifestations of active medical distress (e.g., dental pain, ear infections) is an ethical violation. Medical needs must be treated medically, not behaviorally.

---

## [IS_DISCRETE]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ADL_TARGET] | [Methodological Mismatch: Chained vs. Discrete] ADL targets are fundamentally chained, multi-step routines. Teaching them purely as discrete trials (DTT) violates methodological definitions of chaining.
* EXC: [IS_CHAINING_FORMAT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Skill Complexity and Teaching Format] Discrete skills involve single, indivisible responses (e.g., echoic imitation, pointing) taught via Discrete Trial Training. It violates behavioral methodology to apply chaining formats (forward, backward, task analysis), which are exclusively designed for multi-step chained sequences, to a discrete skill.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Discrete Trial During Timeout] Discrete trial teaching requires presenting academic or readiness demands and providing reinforcement, which is strictly prohibited during an exclusionary timeout that removes the client from the instructional environment.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Methodological Mismatch: DTT During Crisis] Discrete trial teaching (e.g., tracing, answering worksheets, matching) is methodologically impossible and fundamentally unsafe to conduct during an imminent danger crisis.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Safety Violation: Discrete Trials during Medical Crisis] Running academic or discrete trial demands (such as answering WH-questions or matching items) during an active medical emergency is physically impossible and a severe safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Active Discrete Trials vs. Demand Removal] Running active discrete trials (e.g., academic readiness, tacting, matching, echoing) is methodologically contradictory to using a Wait Out procedure, as one requires active instructional demands and engagement while the other necessitates the complete withdrawal of demands.

---

## [IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Safety Protocol Dependency] Enforcing a required safety distance is a physical protocol that fundamentally depends on the presence of an imminent danger (such as aggressive behavior) to be methodologically and ethically justified.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ADL_TARGET] | [Physical Impossibility: Proximal Prompts from a Distance] Teaching ADLs requires close proximity for physical prompting and safety supervision, making it physically impossible to conduct if a required safety distance must be maintained.
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' forces 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching During Crisis] Formal differential reinforcement of alternative behaviors requires the client to be receptive to teaching; it cannot be methodologically implemented during a life-threatening, imminent danger crisis where safety/blocking is the sole priority.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_AVERSIVE' forces 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching During Crisis] Formal differential reinforcement of alternative behaviors requires the client to be receptive to teaching; it cannot be methodologically implemented during a life-threatening, imminent danger crisis where safety/blocking is the sole priority.
* EXC: [IS_CHAINING_FORMAT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' forces 'IS_CHAINED'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINED' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching ADLs During Crisis] It is methodologically invalid and physically unsafe to conduct chained daily living activities (e.g., tying shoes, hygiene routines) while a client is in imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_CONFINED] | [Physical Impossibility: Spatial Distance Limitations] Confined spaces such as vehicles or small restrooms do not provide the necessary square footage to physically allow staff to safely maintain a required physical safety distance from a client.
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | [Physical Impossibility: Proximity vs. Distance] Crisis indicators such as physical blocking or using safety pads require immediate, close-proximity physical contact with the client, which is physically impossible to execute while simultaneously maintaining a required safety distance.
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING' forces 'IS_ACADEMIC'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_ACADEMIC' cannot co-occur: [Safety Violation: Academics during Crisis] Implementing academic demands like counting or tracing while a client is in imminent danger (bolting, severe self-injury) is a severe safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_ELOPEMENT] | [Safety Violation: Distance Maintenance During Elopement] It is medically and physically unsafe to maintain a strict safety distance when a client is actively eloping toward danger; immediate physical proximity and blocking are required to retrieve and secure the client.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE' forces 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' cannot co-occur: [Ethical Violation: Extinction during Imminent Danger] Using planned ignoring for behaviors that present an imminent danger (e.g., elopement, head-banging, gouging eyes) is a severe ethical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_FALL_HAZARD] | [Safety Violation: Distance Maintenance During Fall Hazard] Maintaining a strict safety distance from a client prevents the practitioner from providing necessary physical spotting and immediate intervention, making it medically unsafe during gross motor fall hazard activities.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Physical Contradiction: Distance vs Physical Guidance] Overcorrection typically requires close physical proximity or physical guidance, which directly contradicts a safety mandate requiring a safety distance between the clinician and client.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Contradiction: Required Distance vs Independence] Mandating a required safety distance (used for managing severe behaviors) contradicts documentation stating the client showed an absence of maladaptive behavior and independent functioning.
* EXC: [IS_INTENSIVE_TEACHING] | [Physical Impossibility: Safety Distance during Intensive Teaching] Intensive teaching techniques, particularly errorless learning and max prompting, require close physical proximity to the client to provide physical guidance, which directly contradicts maintaining a behavioral safety distance.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Safety Violation: Safety Distance during Medical Crisis] Maintaining a behavioral 'safety distance' during a medical emergency prevents the caregiver from rendering critical, life-saving medical aid, constituting a severe ethical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_PROXIMITY_PAIRING] | [Physical Impossibility: Safety Distance vs Proximity Touch] Mandating a required safety distance physically contradicts engaging in close proximity pairing actions like holding hands or deep pressure touch.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Safety Hazard: Weaponization Risk] If a client's behavior mandates a safety distance protocol to protect staff or peers, providing them with a sharp instructional object (such as scissors) fundamentally violates safety guidelines by potentially arming a dangerous individual.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION' forces 'IS_REGULATED'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_REGULATED' cannot co-occur: [Behavioral Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Regulated State] A client actively engaging in imminent danger (aggression, elopement, SIB) cannot be described as calm, regulated, or displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_SOCIAL_PLAY] | [Physical Contradiction: Proximity Requirements] Social play requires physical proximity to peers for sharing items and turn-taking, which strictly contradicts an active behavioral protocol requiring staff or peers to maintain a safety distance due to the threat of aggression or severe behavior.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_INDEPENDENT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Independence] Active states of imminent danger (severe aggression, SIB, elopement) strictly contradict the definition of independence, which mandates safe navigation and a complete absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_TOILETING_ADL' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.

---

## [IS_DYSREGULATED]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Definitional Contradiction: Dysregulated vs Independent] A client cannot be simultaneously dysregulated (e.g., crying, whining, visibly frustrated) and independent (demonstrating an absence of maladaptive behavior, transitioning smoothly and happily).
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DEMAND] | [Medical Contradiction: Aspiration Risk] Placing an oral demand (eating, brushing teeth) on an individual who is highly dysregulated (crying, screaming) poses a severe choking and aspiration hazard.
* EXC: [IS_PEER_TARGET] | [Methodological Mismatch: Dysregulation and Social Play] An individual cannot methodologically sustain cooperative peer play or conversational turn-taking while in a state of severe dysregulation (agitated, crying, screaming).
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Contradictory Clinical States: Dysregulation vs. Regulation] A client cannot simultaneously be documented as dysregulated (e.g., agitated, crying) and regulated (e.g., calm, displaying an absence of maladaptive behaviors).
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Dysregulation vs Regulation] A client cannot be simultaneously dysregulated (agitated, crying, displaying precursors) and completely regulated (happy, highly motivated, under quick control).
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Dysregulation vs Smooth Transition] A smooth, happy, and independent transition is physically contradictory to a transition characterized by dysregulation, whining, and precursor behaviors.

---

## [IS_EDIBLE]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_BIO_HAZARD] | [Medical Hazard: Edible Consumption During Bio-Hazard] Providing or consuming edibles while a client is actively engaging in unsanitary bio-hazardous behaviors (e.g., fecal smearing, licking the floor) presents a direct and severe medical risk of pathogen ingestion and illness.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Reinforcement During Timeout] Exclusionary timeout is explicitly defined as the temporary removal of access to positive reinforcement. Providing edibles to a client during this procedure fundamentally violates the definition and mechanics of timeout.
* EXC: [IS_FALL_HAZARD] | [Ethical Violation: Harm Prevention - Choking Hazard] Consuming edibles while simultaneously engaging in vigorous gross motor activities (e.g., swinging, jumping, obstacle courses) presents an acute choking hazard, representing a severe physical risk to the client.
* EXC: [IS_GI_DISTRESS] | [Medical Contraindication: Edibles during GI Distress] It is medically and ethically contraindicated to present edible items as reinforcement when a client is experiencing active GI distress, constipation, or a lack of appetite.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Reinforcement during Punishment] Providing edible reinforcement simultaneously with high-effort punishment (overcorrection) violates behavioral principles by confounding reinforcement and punishment contingencies.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EXERTION] | [Safety Hazard: Edibles during High Exertion] Providing edible items while a client is engaged in high physical exertion (jumping, pacing rapidly, running) presents a severe choking hazard.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical Violation: Edibles During Medical Emergency] Administering edible items during a medical crisis such as an active asthma attack or seizure poses a severe choking risk and is physically hazardous.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DISTRESS] | [Medical Contradiction: Feeding During Oral Distress] Administering edible reinforcers to an individual experiencing active oral distress, dental pain, or pooling saliva presents a direct choking hazard and can exacerbate physical pain.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION] | [Physical Contradiction: Swallowing Intervention] It is physically and methodologically contradictory to administer an edible item while simultaneously executing physical interventions (e.g., blocking an attempt to swallow or tactical breaks).
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DEMAND] | [Physical Safety: Choking Hazard with Respiratory Demands] Administering edibles while simultaneously requiring respiratory demands like deep breathing or blowing bubbles presents a high risk of aspiration and choking.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Physical Safety: Choking Hazard during Respiratory Distress] Administering edibles to a client experiencing acute respiratory distress such as an asthma attack presents a severe choking and aspiration hazard.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Medical Safety Hazard: Edibles During Severe Topography] Administering food or edible reinforcers while a client is in the midst of severe topography (like bolting, head banging, or severe aggression) poses an acute aspiration and choking hazard.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | [Medical Safety Hazard: Edibles During SIB] Administering edibles during active Self-Injurious Behavior introduces a high risk of choking or aspiration due to unpredictable, violent physical movements.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Medical Safety Hazard: Edibles During Lethargy] Administering edible reinforcers or food while a client is extremely lethargic, exhibiting slowed responses, or passively slumping poses a serious physiological aspiration and choking hazard.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Medical Safety Hazard: Edibles During Aggression] Providing edible items while a client is actively aggressing toward staff introduces an aspiration risk and acts as a severe methodological violation by risking accidental reinforcement of aggression.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | [Sanitary Violation: Contamination Risk] Providing and consuming edible reinforcers during a toileting routine creates a severe sanitary hazard and biological contamination risk.
* EXC: [IS_TOXIC_HAZARD] | [Safety Hazard: Toxic Ingestion Risk] Presenting edible reinforcers in the presence of or concurrently with household cleaning supplies poses a severe risk of toxic ingestion and chemical contamination.
* EXC: [IS_VOCAL_COMPROMISE] | [Medical Hazard: Choking and Aspiration] Administering edible items while a client is engaging in loud, sustained vocal protests or screaming presents a high risk of choking and aspiration, making concurrent application medically unsafe.

---

## [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_ACADEMIC] | [Methodological Dependency: Tutoring Requires Academic Target] Educational tutoring fundamentally represents the instructional methodology for scholastic and academic skills. Consequently, implementing a tutoring intervention conceptually and methodologically requires an academic target to be the subject of instruction.
* REQ: [IS_ACADEMIC_MATERIAL] | [Physical/Environmental Requirement: Tutoring Requires Academic Materials] Educational tutoring tasks (such as tracing letters, completing worksheets, and patterning) physically require the presence of academic instructional materials (e.g., paper, pencils, worksheets, manipulatives) to be executed.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | [Methodological Mismatch: Educational Tutoring During Active Crisis] A client in an active crisis state requiring physical safety blocks or tactical breaks is neurologically dysregulated, making strict academic readiness and educational tutoring demands ethically and methodologically inappropriate.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical Impossibility: Educational Tutoring in Dark Environment] Educational tutoring tasks (e.g., worksheets, tracing letters) require adequate lighting for visual discrimination, making them physically impossible to conduct in a strictly dark sensory environment.
* EXC: [IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING' forces 'IS_ACADEMIC'. 'IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_ACADEMIC' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Safety Violation: Academics during Crisis] Implementing academic demands like counting or tracing while a client is in imminent danger (bolting, severe self-injury) is a severe safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_ELOPEMENT] | [Physical Impossibility: Active Instruction During Elopement] A client cannot physically participate in structured, localized educational tutoring tasks (e.g., tracing letters, completing worksheets) while actively eloping or bolting away from the instructional area.
* EXC: [IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING' forces 'IS_ACADEMIC'. 'IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_ACADEMIC' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Safety Violation: Academics during Crisis] Implementing academic demands like counting or tracing while a client is in imminent danger (bolting, severe self-injury) is a severe safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Instruction During Timeout] Exclusionary timeout requires the removal of the individual from the instructional environment and all associated activities. Conducting structured educational tutoring during a timeout violates the procedural parameters of a tactical break.
* EXC: [IS_FRAGMENTABLE_MATERIAL] | [Physical Impossibility: Tutoring with Fragmentable Materials] It is physically impractical to complete writing or tracing worksheets while the hands are simultaneously manipulating fragmentable materials like kinetic sand or playdough.
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Physical Impossibility: Educational Tutoring and Gross Motor] It is physically impossible to complete fine-tuned educational tutoring tasks (tracing letters, completing worksheets) while simultaneously performing full-body gross motor targets (jumping, catching a ball).
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Physical Impossibility: Educational Tutoring with Hand Compromise] Educational tutoring tasks such as writing or tracing require functional use of the hands, which is physically impossible if the client is actively biting or scratching their own hands.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EXERTION] | [Physical Impossibility: Tutoring during High Exertion] Educational tutoring requires physical stability (sitting at a desk) and cannot be executed while a client is engaged in high exertion (running, pacing, jumping).
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_MOBILITY_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical Impossibility: Tutoring in High Mobility Environments] High mobility environments (gross motor obstacle courses, playground equipment) physically preclude safe and effective engagement in desk-based educational tutoring.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Methodological Contradiction: Tutoring during Imminent Danger] A client in imminent danger (SIB, aggression, bolting) cannot simultaneously engage in or be instructed to complete educational worksheets or tracing tasks.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical Violation: Academic Demands During Medical Crisis] Conducting structured educational tutoring or academic tasks during an incapacitating medical event is an ethical violation and medically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_NET_TEACHING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Structured Academics in Naturalistic Teaching] Formal educational tutoring involving worksheets and tracing letters fundamentally contradicts the child-led, embedded nature of Natural Environment Teaching (NET).
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_CONTACT_WITH_THERAPIST] | [Methodological Mismatch: Academic Tutoring and Severe Aggression] A therapist cannot safely or methodologically administer educational tutoring demands (worksheets, writing) while the client is actively directing severe physical aggression (biting, head-butting) at them.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING' forces 'IS_ACADEMIC'. 'IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_ACADEMIC' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Safety Violation: Academics during Crisis] Implementing academic demands like counting or tracing while a client is in imminent danger (bolting, severe self-injury) is a severe safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Academic Tutoring and Safety Management] Administering academic/worksheet demands while an individual is in a state requiring protective safety pads or physical blocking is methodologically and physically incompatible.
* EXC: [IS_PICA] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING' forces 'IS_ACADEMIC'. 'IS_PICA' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_ACADEMIC' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Safety Violation: Academics during Crisis] Implementing academic demands like counting or tracing while a client is in imminent danger (bolting, severe self-injury) is a severe safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Academic Demands During Medical Crisis] Placing educational or academic tutoring demands on a client actively experiencing a medical emergency like an asthma attack is an ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_OVERLOAD] | [Ethical Violation: Tutoring During Sensory Overload] It is methodologically and ethically inappropriate to force structured academic tutoring tasks while a client is in a state of acute sensory overload.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Physical Impossibility: Tutoring During Severe Topography] It is physically impossible to complete structured academic fine-motor tasks (like tracing or worksheets) while simultaneously engaging in severe topographies such as bolting, head-banging, or gouging eyes.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING' forces 'IS_ACADEMIC'. 'IS_SIB' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_ACADEMIC' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Safety Violation: Academics during Crisis] Implementing academic demands like counting or tracing while a client is in imminent danger (bolting, severe self-injury) is a severe safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Physical Impossibility: Tutoring During Aggression] A client cannot focus on or complete structured academic tutoring (like worksheets or patterning) while actively engaged in physical aggression toward the therapist.
* EXC: [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL] | [Safety Violation: Environmental Distraction] Engaging a client in localized, visually demanding academic tasks (e.g., worksheets, tracing) while actively navigating a parking lot or crossing a street is dangerously distracting and physically incompatible with executing life-saving pedestrian skills.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Academic Demand vs. Demand Suspension] Educational tutoring requires the active presentation of academic demands (e.g., worksheets), whereas a wait-out procedure specifically requires the temporary removal and suspension of demands. They cannot occur simultaneously.

---

## [IS_ELECTRONIC_DEVICE]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Physical Impossibility: Electronic Device during Gross Motor] It is highly unsafe and physically incompatible to meaningfully engage in gross motor targets (catching a ball, crossing midline) while actively holding or operating an electronic device.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Physical Impossibility: Electronic Device Usage During Medical Crisis] Operating electronic devices requires motor and cognitive control that is physically precluded by an active medical crisis such as a seizure.

---

## [IS_ELOPEMENT]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Clinical Safety Dependency] Elopement is inherently defined as a critical safety risk involving unauthorized departure from supervision, functionally constituting a state of imminent danger requiring immediate crisis protocols.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ADL_TARGET] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ELOPEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_ADL_TARGET' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Harm Prevention During Elopement] Utilizing attention extinction (planned ignoring) during active elopement is a profound safety violation, as ignoring a fleeing client presents an immediate risk of severe physical harm or death.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ELOPEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_AVERSIVE' forces 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching During Crisis] Formal differential reinforcement of alternative behaviors requires the client to be receptive to teaching; it cannot be methodologically implemented during a life-threatening, imminent danger crisis where safety/blocking is the sole priority.
* EXC: [IS_CHAINING_FORMAT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ELOPEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' forces 'IS_CHAINED'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINED' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching ADLs During Crisis] It is methodologically invalid and physically unsafe to conduct chained daily living activities (e.g., tying shoes, hygiene routines) while a client is in imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_CONFIDENTIAL_DISCUSSION] | [Ethical Violation: Divided Attention During Elopement] Conducting a confidential discussion requires divided attention, which is a critical safety violation when a client is actively eloping and requires immediate, undivided physical tracking and intervention.
* EXC: [IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED] | [Safety Violation: Distance Maintenance During Elopement] It is medically and physically unsafe to maintain a strict safety distance when a client is actively eloping toward danger; immediate physical proximity and blocking are required to retrieve and secure the client.
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | [Physical Impossibility: Active Instruction During Elopement] A client cannot physically participate in structured, localized educational tutoring tasks (e.g., tracing letters, completing worksheets) while actively eloping or bolting away from the instructional area.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ELOPEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE' forces 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' cannot co-occur: [Ethical Violation: Extinction during Imminent Danger] Using planned ignoring for behaviors that present an imminent danger (e.g., elopement, head-banging, gouging eyes) is a severe ethical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_FALL_HAZARD] | [Physical Impossibility: Simultaneous Bolting and Stationary Gross Motor] It is physically impossible for a client to be actively eloping (e.g., bolting toward an exit or running away) while simultaneously positioned on and engaging with stationary gross motor equipment such as swings or therapy balls.
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Methodological Contradiction: Gross Motor Target during Elopement] A client actively eloping or bolting cannot simultaneously receive clinical instruction in targeted gross motor skills like catching a ball.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Definitional Contradiction: Elopement vs Independent] Elopement (bolting, escaping the area) directly contradicts the definition of being independent (exhibiting an absence of maladaptive behaviors, transitioning smoothly).
* EXC: [IS_INTENSIVE_TEACHING] | [Methodological Contradiction: Elopement During Intensive Structured Teaching] Active elopement, such as bolting or leaving the instructional area, physically precludes the simultaneous administration of seated, highly structured discrete trial training.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Physical Impossibility: Operant Elopement During Medical Incapacitation] An individual experiencing a severe, incapacitating medical crisis like a seizure cannot simultaneously engage in active, goal-directed operant elopement.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DEMAND] | [Physical Contradiction: Elopement and Oral Demands] Attempting to feed or brush the teeth of an individual while they are actively eloping (running away, bolting) creates an acute choking and physical injury hazard.
* EXC: [IS_PEER_OR_SIBLING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Elopement and Cooperative Social Play] It is physically and methodologically impossible for an individual to engage in sustained cooperative play, joint attention, or sibling play while actively bolting or eloping from the area.
* EXC: [IS_PEER_TARGET] | [Methodological Mismatch: Elopement and Peer Targets] It is physically and methodologically impossible for an individual to execute targeted cooperative peer play or turn-taking while actively bolting or eloping from the instructional area.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Contradictory Clinical States: Elopement vs. Regulation] Active elopement is a maladaptive behavior, which inherently contradicts the documentation of a regulated client displaying an absence of maladaptive behaviors.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Elopement With Sharp Objects] A client actively eloping, bolting, or running away while holding a sharp object (such as scissors) presents a severe physical safety and laceration hazard.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Elopement vs Regulation] Actively eloping (bolting, hiding, fleeing) is mutually exclusive with being happily paired, regulated, and engaged with the therapist.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Elopement vs Smooth Transition] Eloping or bolting from the area is the behavioral opposite of a smooth, independent, and compliant transition.
* EXC: [IS_SOCIAL_PLAY] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Elopement vs Social Play] Active elopement (bolting, fleeing from staff) is behaviorally incompatible with sustaining seated, structured, or cooperative peer social play.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ELOPEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_INDEPENDENT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Independence] Active states of imminent danger (severe aggression, SIB, elopement) strictly contradict the definition of independence, which mandates safe navigation and a complete absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ELOPEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_TOILETING_ADL' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Ethical Violation: Negligence During Elopement] Utilizing a wait-out procedure (suspending demands and waiting without physical intervention) when a client is actively eloping toward exits or traffic is a severe safety and ethical violation, as immediate intervention is required to prevent harm.

---

## [IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Crisis Management Dependency] Environmental modification, such as sweeping an area for hazards or clearing a room, is a crisis management strategy that fundamentally depends on the presence or immediate risk of imminent danger to ensure physical safety.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ADL_TARGET] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_ADL_TARGET' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' forces 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching During Crisis] Formal differential reinforcement of alternative behaviors requires the client to be receptive to teaching; it cannot be methodologically implemented during a life-threatening, imminent danger crisis where safety/blocking is the sole priority.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_AVERSIVE' forces 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching During Crisis] Formal differential reinforcement of alternative behaviors requires the client to be receptive to teaching; it cannot be methodologically implemented during a life-threatening, imminent danger crisis where safety/blocking is the sole priority.
* EXC: [IS_CHAINING_FORMAT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' forces 'IS_CHAINED'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINED' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching ADLs During Crisis] It is methodologically invalid and physically unsafe to conduct chained daily living activities (e.g., tying shoes, hygiene routines) while a client is in imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING' forces 'IS_ACADEMIC'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_ACADEMIC' cannot co-occur: [Safety Violation: Academics during Crisis] Implementing academic demands like counting or tracing while a client is in imminent danger (bolting, severe self-injury) is a severe safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE' forces 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' cannot co-occur: [Ethical Violation: Extinction during Imminent Danger] Using planned ignoring for behaviors that present an imminent danger (e.g., elopement, head-banging, gouging eyes) is a severe ethical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION' forces 'IS_REGULATED'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_REGULATED' cannot co-occur: [Behavioral Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Regulated State] A client actively engaging in imminent danger (aggression, elopement, SIB) cannot be described as calm, regulated, or displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_INDEPENDENT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Independence] Active states of imminent danger (severe aggression, SIB, elopement) strictly contradict the definition of independence, which mandates safe navigation and a complete absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_TOILETING_ADL' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.

---

## [IS_ERROR_OR_STRUGGLE]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Skill Deficits] Applying a punishment procedure, such as exclusionary timeout, to an emerging skill, prompt dependence, or generalization failure is a severe ethical violation. Punishment must never be utilized for cognitive or motor skill deficits.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Definitional Contradiction: Error vs Independent] Demonstrating an error or struggle (prompt dependence, failure to generalize) contradicts achieving independent mastery of a skill.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Methodological Mismatch: Skill Assessment During Medical Emergency] It is medically invalid and ethically inappropriate to record skill acquisition errors or struggles during an active medical crisis.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | [Methodological Contradiction: Prompt Dependence vs Spontaneous] A behavior cannot be characterized as a 'spontaneous demonstration' if it simultaneously required prompts, fading, or was inconsistent and prompt-dependent.

---

## [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_ESCAPE_MAINTAINED] | [Methodological Dependency: Escape Extinction] In ABA methodology, implementing escape extinction (or related antecedent interventions like High-P and Premack for demands) conceptually requires the behavior or context to be maintained by escape/demand avoidance to be functionally and behaviorally valid.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_MAINTAINED] | [Methodological Mismatch: Escape Extinction for Attention Function] Applying escape extinction prevents a client from avoiding a demand, which is methodologically illogical for attention-maintained behavior, as the intervention fails to address the functional reinforcer of attention.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Escape Extinction During Medical Distress] Applying escape extinction to force compliance when a client is experiencing acute medical pain (e.g., an ear infection) is unethical and violates BACB mandates regarding ruling out, accommodating, and treating medical conditions.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_SENSITIVITY] | [Ethical Violation: Forced Compliance During Sensory Pain] Applying escape extinction to a client whose behavior is triggered by severe sensory pain (e.g., loud noises) forces them to endure an aversive physiological trigger without escape, violating ethical mandates for client dignity and harm prevention.
* EXC: [IS_AUTOMATIC_MAINTAINED] | [Methodological Mismatch: Escape Extinction for Automatic Function] Escape extinction involves blocking socially-mediated escape from demands. If a behavior is automatically maintained (internally reinforcing), applying escape extinction is methodologically illogical because the reinforcer is not environmentally contingent escape.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_REINFORCER] | [Methodological Mismatch: Contradictory Escape Contingencies] It is methodologically impossible to simultaneously apply escape extinction (which requires preventing a break or escape) and an escape reinforcer (which requires providing a break or escape) for the same target behavior.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Demand Removal During Escape Extinction] Escape extinction requires the client to remain within the demand context. Utilizing exclusionary timeout functionally removes the client from the demand, directly contradicting and nullifying the escape extinction procedure.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION' forces 'IS_ESCAPE_MAINTAINED'. 'IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE' forces 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION'. 'IS_ESCAPE_MAINTAINED' and 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Attention Extinction for Escape Function] Attention extinction removes social attention, which is methodologically contraindicated for escape-maintained behavior, as the functional reinforcer is demand removal rather than attention. This often inadvertently reinforces the escape behavior.
* EXC: [IS_GI_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Escape Extinction during GI Distress] Maintaining clinical demands via escape extinction when a client is sick or experiencing severe GI distress violates medical clearance and ethical care guidelines.
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Ethical Violation: Escape Extinction during SIB] Enforcing demands and blocking escape while a client is engaging in self-injurious behavior (hand-biting/scratching) compromises client safety.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Ethical Violation: Escape Extinction during Imminent Danger] Utilizing escape extinction (forcing compliance/blocking escape) while a client is engaged in severe imminent danger (e.g., SIB, eloping into a street) is a gross ethical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Definitional Contradiction: Escape Extinction vs Independent] Escape extinction requires the active presence of maladaptive escape behavior, which contradicts the client being fully independent and free of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical Violation: Escape Extinction During Medical Crisis] Utilizing escape extinction to force compliance and block escape during a life-threatening medical crisis is dangerous and violates BACB ethical guidelines regarding physiological needs.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Forcing Compliance During Medical Illness] Applying escape extinction to demand compliance when a client is experiencing active medical distress (e.g., severe pain, illness) is highly unethical and contradicts clinical safety guidelines.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Forcing Compliance Through Pain] Implementing escape extinction (forcing demand compliance) when a client is escaping due to acute medical or dental pain violates ethical guidelines regarding harm prevention.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Escape Extinction During Medical Crisis] Utilizing escape extinction to block escape or force compliance during an active medical event like respiratory distress is a severe ethical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Escape Extinction During Sensory Distress] Enforcing escape extinction (forcing compliance) during acute sensory distress, such as a severe headache, violates ethical guidelines regarding the humane treatment of medical symptoms.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_OVERLOAD] | [Ethical Violation: Escape Extinction During Sensory Overload] Using escape extinction (forcing compliance) when a client is escaping due to genuine sensory overload is an ethical violation of neurodivergent affirming care.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Extinction Burst in Severe Topography] Implementing escape extinction during severe, dangerous topographies (like bolting into the street or severe aggression) is clinically contraindicated due to the high risk of catastrophic harm during extinction bursts.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Extinction During SIB] Using escape extinction (blocking escape and maintaining demands) in the presence of Self-Injurious Behavior (SIB) is ethically prohibited and medically dangerous.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Ethical Violation: Escape Extinction During Lethargy] Forcing compliance via escape extinction when a client is visibly lethargic or fatigued due to poor sleep ignores biological necessities and is an ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Escape Extinction vs Smooth Transition] Escape extinction cannot be applied to a smooth, cooperative, and independent transition, as there is no escape-maintained behavior occurring to extinguish.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION' forces 'IS_ESCAPE_MAINTAINED'. 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_ESCAPE_MAINTAINED' and 'IS_INDEPENDENT' cannot co-occur: [Definitional Contradiction: Escape Maintained vs Independent] Engaging in active escape-maintained maladaptive behavior directly contradicts demonstrating an independent, smooth transition.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Extinction With Aggression] Applying unmodified escape extinction to direct, severe physical aggression risks dangerous escalation and exposes the practitioner to preventable physical harm.
* EXC: [IS_TACTILE_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Escape Extinction During Tactile Distress] Denying escape or breaks to a client experiencing acute tactile distress (such as a rash or skin irritation) is an ethical violation that ignores physical/medical wellbeing.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | [Ethical Violation: Bodily Autonomy] Utilizing escape extinction (physically or continuously forcing compliance) during toileting routines violates clinical ethical guidelines regarding client dignity, bodily autonomy, and the prevention of trauma.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Opposing Demand Paradigms] Escape extinction requires the practitioner to follow through with placing and maintaining a demand, whereas a wait-out procedure necessitates dropping or pausing the demand. These are methodologically opposite and mutually exclusive.

---

## [IS_ESCAPE_MAINTAINED]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Attention Extinction for Escape Function] Attention extinction removes social attention, which is methodologically contraindicated for escape-maintained behavior, as the functional reinforcer is demand removal rather than attention. This often inadvertently reinforces the escape behavior.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Reinforcing Escape Behavior] Utilizing exclusionary timeout as a consequence for an escape-maintained behavior is a critical methodological error, as the timeout functionally reinforces the problem behavior by providing the desired escape from the environment.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Definitional Contradiction: Escape Maintained vs Independent] Engaging in active escape-maintained maladaptive behavior directly contradicts demonstrating an independent, smooth transition.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Methodological Mismatch: Operant Function for Involuntary Medical Event] A physiological medical crisis such as a seizure is an involuntary, biological event and cannot be categorized as an operant behavior maintained by escape.
* EXC: [IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Timeout for Escape-Maintained Behavior] Applying a tactical break (exclusionary timeout) for an escape-maintained behavior is a methodological mismatch, as the timeout inadvertently provides the reinforcing escape.

---

## [IS_ESCAPE_REINFORCER]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_MAINTAINED] | [Methodological Mismatch: Escape Reinforcer for Attention Function] Providing an escape reinforcer (e.g., a break) for attention-maintained behavior is methodologically mismatched, as removing the client from the demand environment often removes social interaction, failing to deliver the target reinforcer.
* EXC: [IS_AUTOMATIC_MAINTAINED] | [Methodological Mismatch: Escape Reinforcer for Automatic Function] Providing an escape reinforcer (a break from demands) for an automatically maintained behavior is methodologically mismatched, as the behavior relies on internal sensory feedback rather than the removal of external task demands.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Contradictory Escape Contingencies] It is methodologically impossible to simultaneously apply escape extinction (which requires preventing a break or escape) and an escape reinforcer (which requires providing a break or escape) for the same target behavior.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Escape Reinforcer and High Effort Punishment] Delivering an escape reinforcer (break) completely contradicts implementing high-effort punishment (positive practice/overcorrection) at the same time.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Methodological Mismatch: Escape Reinforcement for Medical Emergency] Escape functions and operant reinforcement contingencies cannot be applied to involuntary, non-operant medical emergencies.
* EXC: [IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Timeout as an Escape Reinforcer] When escape is the primary reinforcer, applying a restrictive punishment such as a tactical break provides access to escape, creating a methodological contradiction.
* EXC: [IS_TANGIBLE_MAINTAINED] | [Methodological Mismatch: Escape Reinforcer for Tangible Function] Providing a break or escape to functionally reinforce a behavior that is exclusively maintained by access to tangibles represents a methodological mismatch in Skinnerian principles.

---

## [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT] | [Methodological Dependency] Exclusionary timeout procedures involve the systematic removal of a learner from a reinforcing environment and are definitionally categorized as a restrictive punishment procedure in behavior analysis.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Timeout for Medical Distress] Utilizing exclusionary timeout as a consequence for behaviors stemming from acute medical distress (e.g., ear infection) is an ethical violation. Medical symptoms must be evaluated and accommodated, not punished.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_SENSITIVITY] | [Ethical Violation: Timeout for Sensory Sensitivity] Placing a client in exclusionary timeout as a consequence for behaviors driven by sensory sensitivity (e.g., triggered by loud noise) is ethically contraindicated, as it punishes a sensory deficit and fails to address the environmental antecedent.
* EXC: [IS_AUTOMATIC_MAINTAINED] | [Methodological Contradiction: Timeout for Automatic Function] Placing a client in an exclusionary timeout for automatically maintained behavior is methodologically contraindicated; isolating the client in a barren environment removes competing stimuli and inadvertently reinforces the internal behavior (e.g., stereotypy).
* EXC: [IS_CAREGIVER_ENGAGEMENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Caregiver Engagement During Timeout] Exclusionary timeout requires the temporary removal of all social reinforcement and attention. Caregiver engagement involves active social interaction and coaching, which strictly contradicts the methodology of timeout.
* EXC: [IS_CHAINED] | [Methodological Mismatch: Chained Task During Timeout] Exclusionary timeout mandates the immediate cessation of all instructional demands and reinforcement. Engaging a client in a programmed chained task (e.g., daily living skills) during this time is methodologically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_CHAINING_FORMAT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Chaining Format During Timeout] Chaining formats (e.g., forward/backward chaining, shaping) require active instruction and reinforcement, which directly violates the methodological requirement of removing all demands during exclusionary timeout.
* EXC: [IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Differential Reinforcement During Timeout] Differential reinforcement involves actively delivering reinforcement for alternative behaviors. This is methodologically incompatible with exclusionary timeout, which requires the complete withholding of all reinforcement.
* EXC: [IS_DISCRETE] | [Methodological Mismatch: Discrete Trial During Timeout] Discrete trial teaching requires presenting academic or readiness demands and providing reinforcement, which is strictly prohibited during an exclusionary timeout that removes the client from the instructional environment.
* EXC: [IS_EDIBLE] | [Methodological Mismatch: Reinforcement During Timeout] Exclusionary timeout is explicitly defined as the temporary removal of access to positive reinforcement. Providing edibles to a client during this procedure fundamentally violates the definition and mechanics of timeout.
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Instruction During Timeout] Exclusionary timeout requires the removal of the individual from the instructional environment and all associated activities. Conducting structured educational tutoring during a timeout violates the procedural parameters of a tactical break.
* EXC: [IS_ERROR_OR_STRUGGLE] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Skill Deficits] Applying a punishment procedure, such as exclusionary timeout, to an emerging skill, prompt dependence, or generalization failure is a severe ethical violation. Punishment must never be utilized for cognitive or motor skill deficits.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Demand Removal During Escape Extinction] Escape extinction requires the client to remain within the demand context. Utilizing exclusionary timeout functionally removes the client from the demand, directly contradicting and nullifying the escape extinction procedure.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_MAINTAINED] | [Methodological Mismatch: Reinforcing Escape Behavior] Utilizing exclusionary timeout as a consequence for an escape-maintained behavior is a critical methodological error, as the timeout functionally reinforces the problem behavior by providing the desired escape from the environment.
* EXC: [IS_EXECUTIVE_FUNCTIONING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Demands During Timeout] Executive functioning tasks (e.g., following 2-step instructions, independent task initiation) represent cognitive demands. Exclusionary timeout necessitates the complete removal of demands and positive reinforcement, making simultaneous application contradictory.
* EXC: [IS_FALL_HAZARD] | [Methodological Mismatch: Reinforcing Timeout Environments] Placing a client on highly reinforcing gross motor equipment, such as a therapy ball, swing, or playground, as a timeout location violates the core premise of timeout, which requires removal from reinforcing and preferred activities.
* EXC: [IS_GI_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Timeout during GI Distress] Isolating or punishing a client via timeout for behaviors potentially driven by an underlying medical issue (GI distress) is ethically prohibited.
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Methodological Contradiction: Timeout and Instructional Target] Exclusionary timeout fundamentally requires removal from the instructional setting, making active engagement in a gross motor target physically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Ethical Violation: Tactical Break during Hand Compromise SIB] Implementing a tactical break or exclusionary timeout while a client is actively biting or scratching their hands (SIB) leaves a self-injuring client unattended.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Timeout and High Effort Punishment] Exclusionary timeout requires the total removal of activity, whereas high-effort punishment demands active physical engagement. Both cannot be applied at the same exact moment.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Ethical Violation: Tactical Break during Imminent Danger] Placing a client in an exclusionary timeout or tactical break during severe imminent danger (SIB, pica, bolting to street) abandons them during a crisis and is highly unethical.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Definitional Contradiction: Timeout vs Independent] Exclusionary timeout is a consequence for severe maladaptive behavior, which contradicts the state of being completely independent and free of maladaptive behaviors.
* EXC: [IS_INTENSIVE_TEACHING] | [Methodological Contradiction: Exclusionary Timeout and Intensive Teaching] An exclusionary timeout physically removes the individual from the teaching environment, making the simultaneous delivery of intensive teaching structurally impossible.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical Violation: Punitive Timeout During Medical Crisis] Placing an individual in an exclusionary timeout during an active medical emergency is severe medical neglect and highly unethical.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Punitive Timeout for Medical Distress] Utilizing a disciplinary exclusionary timeout when a client is experiencing active medical distress or physical illness violates ethical guidelines for compassionate and safe care.
* EXC: [IS_NET_TEACHING] | [Methodological Contradiction: Timeout During Naturalistic Teaching] Exclusionary timeout isolates the individual from ongoing activities, structurally contradicting the inclusive, environment-embedded methodology of Natural Environment Teaching.
* EXC: [IS_NON_DANGEROUS_BEHAVIOR] | [Ethical Violation: Restrictive Procedures for Mild Behavior] Utilizing an exclusionary timeout (a highly restrictive, punitive procedure) for non-dangerous behaviors (stereotypy, passive slumping) violates the ethical mandate for least restrictive behavioral interventions.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DISTRESS] | [Medical Contradiction: Unmonitored Oral Distress] Placing an individual in an isolated timeout while they are experiencing active oral distress or pooling saliva presents an ethical violation and an unmonitored choking risk.
* EXC: [IS_OUTDOOR_MATERIAL] | [Methodological Mismatch: Timeout and Environmental Engagement] An exclusionary timeout fundamentally requires the removal of access to reinforcing environments; it cannot co-occur with engaging with outdoor materials like playgrounds or water play tables.
* EXC: [IS_OUTDOOR_REINFORCER] | [Methodological Mismatch: Timeout and Reinforcement] An exclusionary timeout fundamentally requires the removal of reinforcement; it cannot methodologically co-occur with providing access to the outdoors as a reinforcer.
* EXC: [IS_PEER_OR_SIBLING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Timeout and Social Interaction] Exclusionary timeout socially isolates the individual, making it physically and methodologically impossible to simultaneously participate in cooperative or parallel play with peers or siblings.
* EXC: [IS_PEER_TARGET] | [Methodological Mismatch: Timeout and Peer Targets] Exclusionary timeout socially isolates the individual, making it physically and methodologically impossible to simultaneously target peer greetings, play, or turn-taking.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_CONTACT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Timeout and Physical Contact] Providing positive physical contact (such as deep pressure, squeezes, or tickles) during an exclusionary timeout violates the procedural withdrawal of social and sensory reinforcement.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_PROMPT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Timeout and Instructional Prompts] Exclusionary timeout dictates the withdrawal of instructional interaction. Providing physical prompts (hand-over-hand) violates the methodological definition of a timeout.
* EXC: [IS_PICA] | [Medical Contradiction: Unmonitored Pica Hazard] Placing an individual with active Pica in an isolated exclusionary timeout presents a severe, potentially fatal risk of unobserved ingestion of non-edible items.
* EXC: [IS_PROXIMITY_PAIRING] | [Physical/Methodological Contradiction: Timeout vs. Proximity Pairing] Exclusionary timeout requires removing the client from reinforcing environments and people, which physically and methodologically contradicts proximity pairing like holding hands or providing deep pressure.
