* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing a Regulated Client] Applying an exclusionary timeout to a client who is regulated and displaying no maladaptive behaviors violates the ethical principles of behavior analysis.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Unmonitored Severe Topography] Placing a client in an exclusionary timeout (removing proximity/supervision) while they engage in severe topographies (like swallowing objects or gouging eyes) is a severe medical safety hazard.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Sharp Objects in Timeout] Permitting a client to possess sharp objects (like scissors) while serving an exclusionary timeout introduces an extreme risk of unmonitored self-harm or injury.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Unmonitored SIB] Utilizing exclusionary timeout during self-injurious behavior removes the practitioner's ability to physically block the SIB, creating an acute physical safety hazard.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Ethical Violation: Timeout for Lethargy] Using exclusionary timeout as a consequence for behaviors rooted in severe lethargy or sleep deprivation is medically inappropriate and unethical.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT' forces 'IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT'. 'IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION' forces 'IS_REGULATED'. 'IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT' and 'IS_REGULATED' cannot co-occur: [Ethical and Methodological Violation] It is an ethical violation and a Skinnerian methodological impossibility to apply restrictive punishments (e.g., response cost, tactical break, overcorrection) to a client who is actively regulated, cooperative, and devoid of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_SOCIAL_PLAY] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Timeout During Cooperative Play] A client cannot be concurrently serving an exclusionary timeout (removed from the environment) and actively participating in cooperative social play with peers.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT' forces 'IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT'. 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT' and 'IS_INDEPENDENT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Punishing Independent Behavior] Applying restrictive punishment (tactical breaks, response cost) to a client who is behaving independently and appropriately without maladaptive behaviors is an ethical and methodological violation.
* EXC: [IS_TACTILE_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Timeout for Tactile Distress] Placing a client in exclusionary timeout for behaviors that are driven by physical/tactile distress (like a rash) is an unethical denial of medical/sensory care.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | [Ethical Violation: Inappropriate Seclusion Area] Utilizing a bathroom or restroom facility as a location for an exclusionary timeout poses significant safety risks and violates ethical guidelines prohibiting seclusion in potentially hazardous or unmonitored sanitary environments.
* EXC: [IS_TOXIC_HAZARD] | [Safety Violation: Hazardous Timeout Environment] Placing a client in an exclusionary timeout area that contains toxic hazards (e.g., household cleaning supplies) is a severe safety and ethical violation due to the risk of unmonitored ingestion or injury.
* EXC: [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL] | [Safety Violation: Pedestrian Seclusion] Attempting to implement an exclusionary timeout procedure in an active traffic environment (e.g., street or parking lot) is physically hazardous and represents gross clinical negligence.
* EXC: [IS_VIBRATING_ENVIRONMENT] | [Safety Violation: Seclusion in a Moving Vehicle] Enforcing an exclusionary timeout within a moving vehicle is physically impossible to execute safely, violates vehicle safety and seatbelt protocols, and risks severe injury during transit.

---

## [IS_EXECUTIVE_FUNCTIONING]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | [Methodological Mismatch: Executive Functioning During Active Crisis] Executive functioning tasks demand high cognitive load and self-regulation, which is neurologically and methodologically incompatible with a client in an active behavioral crisis requiring physical blocking.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Demands During Timeout] Executive functioning tasks (e.g., following 2-step instructions, independent task initiation) represent cognitive demands. Exclusionary timeout necessitates the complete removal of demands and positive reinforcement, making simultaneous application contradictory.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Definitional Contradiction: Executive Functioning during Overcorrection] High-effort punishment (overcorrection) is a forced, externally directed consequence, which nullifies self-directed executive functioning and independent problem-solving.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Methodological Contradiction: Executive Functioning during Imminent Danger] A client cannot utilize higher-order executive functioning or self-monitoring while simultaneously undergoing a severe behavioral crisis involving imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical Violation: Cognitive Demands During Medical Crisis] Placing advanced executive functioning demands, such as problem-solving or self-monitoring, on an individual during an active medical emergency is clinically invalid and unethical.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Cognitive Processing and Physical Intervention] Methodologically, an individual cannot perform high-level cognitive targets (independent problem-solving) while simultaneously being physically blocked for severe safety concerns or placed in a tactical break.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_PROMPT] | [Methodological Impossibility: Prompting Internal Processes] Executive functioning targets (problem-solving, self-monitoring) are internal cognitive processes that cannot be methodologically or physically prompted (e.g., hand-over-hand guidance).
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Executive Functioning and Severe Behavior] An individual requiring protective safety equipment or physical blocks for dangerous behavior is not in an emotional or regulated state to simultaneously engage in complex independent executive functioning tasks.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Executive Functioning Demands During Medical Crisis] Imposing complex executive functioning tasks on a client experiencing active respiratory distress is ethically and medically contraindicated.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_OVERLOAD] | [Cognitive Impossibility: Executive Functioning During Sensory Overload] A client experiencing acute sensory overload lacks the physiological regulation required to engage in high-level executive functioning and self-monitoring tasks.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Physical Impossibility: Executive Functioning During Severe Topography] High-level executive functioning tasks (like multi-step problem solving and self-monitoring) are cognitively and physically impossible to execute while engaging in acute severe topographies.

---

## [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Methodological Dependency: Extinction Sensitivity Requires Extinction Application] For a behavior to be deemed 'extinction sensitive' specifically to being ignored (e.g., 'screaming subsided when ignored'), the specific methodological intervention of planned ignoring (attention extinction) must have been actively applied.
* REQ: [IS_ATTENTION_MAINTAINED] | [Methodological Dependency: Extinction Sensitivity Requires Attention Maintained Function] If a behavior's frequency demonstrably subsides when attention is removed (ignored), it methodologically establishes that the behavior was functionally maintained by attention.
* REQ: [IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT] | Derived Chain (IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE -> IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION -> IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT): [Methodological Dependency: Extinction Sensitivity Requires Extinction Application] For a behavior to be deemed 'extinction sensitive' specifically to being ignored (e.g., 'screaming subsided when ignored'), the specific methodological intervention of planned ignoring (attention extinction) must have been actively applied. | [Ethical Dependency: Extinction Requires Reinforcement] Methodologically and ethically, implementing an extinction procedure such as attention extinction (planned ignoring) requires the concurrent use of a differential reinforcement procedure to teach and reinforce functionally equivalent alternative behaviors.
* REQ: [IS_NON_DANGEROUS_BEHAVIOR] | [Ethical Dependency: Extinction Suitability] Applying an extinction procedure that involves ignoring a behavior methodologically and ethically requires the behavior to be non-dangerous. It is a severe ethical and safety violation to ignore dangerous, aggressive, or self-injurious behaviors.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE' forces 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION'. 'IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Ethical Violation: Extinction during Imminent Danger] Using planned ignoring for behaviors that present an imminent danger (e.g., elopement, head-banging, gouging eyes) is a severe ethical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE' forces 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION'. 'IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Ethical Violation: Extinction during Imminent Danger] Using planned ignoring for behaviors that present an imminent danger (e.g., elopement, head-banging, gouging eyes) is a severe ethical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_ELOPEMENT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE' forces 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION'. 'IS_ELOPEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Ethical Violation: Extinction during Imminent Danger] Using planned ignoring for behaviors that present an imminent danger (e.g., elopement, head-banging, gouging eyes) is a severe ethical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE' forces 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION'. 'IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Ethical Violation: Extinction during Imminent Danger] Using planned ignoring for behaviors that present an imminent danger (e.g., elopement, head-banging, gouging eyes) is a severe ethical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE' forces 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION'. 'IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION' forces 'IS_ESCAPE_MAINTAINED'. 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' and 'IS_ESCAPE_MAINTAINED' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Attention Extinction for Escape Function] Attention extinction removes social attention, which is methodologically contraindicated for escape-maintained behavior, as the functional reinforcer is demand removal rather than attention. This often inadvertently reinforces the escape behavior.
* EXC: [IS_FRAGMENTABLE_MATERIAL] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE' forces 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION'. 'IS_FRAGMENTABLE_MATERIAL' forces 'IS_CHOKING_HAZARD'. 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' and 'IS_CHOKING_HAZARD' cannot co-occur: [Ethical Violation: Lethal Harm Prevention] Using planned ignoring when a client is actively engaging in a choking hazard (e.g., swallowing small objects) is a lethal medical and ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_GI_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction during GI Distress] Ignoring vocalizations or behavior that stem from active illness or GI distress denies the client appropriate medical care and violates ethical guidelines.
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction for SIB] Using planned ignoring procedures for self-injurious behavior such as biting or scratching one's own hands violates immediate safety protocols.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Extinction and Punishment] Planned ignoring (extinction) cannot occur concurrently with high-effort punishment (overcorrection), as overcorrection demands active physical intervention from staff.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction for Imminent Danger] Applying planned ignoring (extinction) to behaviors involving imminent danger (SIB, bolting, aggression) is a critical safety and ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Definitional Contradiction: Extinction vs Independent] Extinction requires active maladaptive behavior to be ignored, whereas independence requires the complete absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical Violation: Ignoring Distress During Medical Emergency] Applying attention extinction by ignoring vocal protests or distress during a medical emergency is inherently dangerous and constitutes medical neglect.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Attention Extinction for Medical Pain] It is an ethical violation to utilize attention extinction procedures on an individual explicitly expressing distress derived from active physical pain, illness, or medical discomfort.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE' forces 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION'. 'IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Ethical Violation: Extinction during Imminent Danger] Using planned ignoring for behaviors that present an imminent danger (e.g., elopement, head-banging, gouging eyes) is a severe ethical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE' forces 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION'. 'IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Ethical Violation: Extinction during Imminent Danger] Using planned ignoring for behaviors that present an imminent danger (e.g., elopement, head-banging, gouging eyes) is a severe ethical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_PICA] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE' forces 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION'. 'IS_PICA' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Ethical Violation: Extinction during Imminent Danger] Using planned ignoring for behaviors that present an imminent danger (e.g., elopement, head-banging, gouging eyes) is a severe ethical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Extinction vs. Active Punishment] A behavior that is managed by ignoring it (extinction sensitive) methodologically contradicts the simultaneous application of active restrictive punishment like overcorrection or response cost.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_OVERLOAD] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction for Sensory Overload] Subjecting sensory overload to extinction (planned ignoring) is medically and ethically abusive, as sensory dysregulation requires accommodation or removal of the aversive stimulus.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Ignoring Severe Topography] Ignoring (using extinction for) severe topographies like bolting into traffic or head-butting is clinically negligent and introduces severe risk of harm.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Ignoring SIB] It is an ethical and safety violation to apply extinction (ignoring) to active self-injurious behavior (SIB), as it requires immediate physical intervention.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction for Lethargy] Using extinction to ignore behaviors resulting from genuine physiological fatigue or sleep deprivation is an ethical violation of client care.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE' forces 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION'. 'IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION' forces 'IS_REGULATED'. 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' and 'IS_REGULATED' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Extinction on Regulated Client] Attention extinction (planned ignoring) targets active maladaptive behaviors. Applying it when a client is regulated, cooperative, and displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior is a fundamental methodological error.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE' forces 'IS_NON_DANGEROUS_BEHAVIOR'. 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_NON_DANGEROUS_BEHAVIOR' and 'IS_INDEPENDENT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Contradiction: Independence vs Maladaptive Behavior] A client cannot simultaneously be documented as 'displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior' while actively engaging in non-dangerous maladaptive behaviors such as dropping to the floor and crying or ignoring instructions.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Ignoring Staff Aggression] Using extinction (planned ignoring) for direct physical aggression toward staff (like punching or head-butting) places practitioners at unacceptable physical risk.
* EXC: [IS_TACTILE_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction for Tactile Distress] Ignoring a client's behaviors that are secondary to genuine tactile or medical distress (like rashes) is an ethical violation of their medical rights.

---

## [IS_FALL_HAZARD]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_CHOKING_HAZARD] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Choking Hazard During Fall Hazard] Permitting a client to interact with small objects (choking hazards) while simultaneously engaging in gross motor fall hazards (jumping, swinging) creates an extreme and preventable risk of aspiration and bodily injury.
* EXC: [IS_CONFIDENTIAL_DISCUSSION] | [Ethical Violation: Divided Attention During Fall Hazard] Engaging in a confidential discussion diverts practitioner attention, creating a severe physical safety risk when a client is simultaneously navigating gross motor fall hazards (e.g., swinging, obstacle courses).
* EXC: [IS_CONFINED] | [Physical Impossibility: Fall Hazard in Confined Space] Confined spaces (e.g., vehicles, restrooms) lack the spatial capacity and safety infrastructure required for gross motor fall hazard activities like playgrounds, swinging, or obstacle courses, making their combination physically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Fall Hazard in Dark Environment] Engaging in gross motor fall hazards (e.g., swinging, jumping, obstacle courses) in a dark environment completely removes visual spotting capabilities, constituting an extreme physical safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED] | [Safety Violation: Distance Maintenance During Fall Hazard] Maintaining a strict safety distance from a client prevents the practitioner from providing necessary physical spotting and immediate intervention, making it medically unsafe during gross motor fall hazard activities.
* EXC: [IS_EDIBLE] | [Ethical Violation: Harm Prevention - Choking Hazard] Consuming edibles while simultaneously engaging in vigorous gross motor activities (e.g., swinging, jumping, obstacle courses) presents an acute choking hazard, representing a severe physical risk to the client.
* EXC: [IS_ELOPEMENT] | [Physical Impossibility: Simultaneous Bolting and Stationary Gross Motor] It is physically impossible for a client to be actively eloping (e.g., bolting toward an exit or running away) while simultaneously positioned on and engaging with stationary gross motor equipment such as swings or therapy balls.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Reinforcing Timeout Environments] Placing a client on highly reinforcing gross motor equipment, such as a therapy ball, swing, or playground, as a timeout location violates the core premise of timeout, which requires removal from reinforcing and preferred activities.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Safety Hazard: Punishment on Fall Hazard] Forcing high-effort punishment routines while a client is stationed on a fall hazard (swing, therapy ball) presents an immense risk of injury.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Physical Hazard: Gross Motor Play During Medical Crisis] Engaging in gross motor fall hazards (e.g., swinging, jumping) during a severe medical crisis like a seizure poses an imminent risk of physical injury and is medically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DEMAND] | [Physical Contradiction: Fall Hazard and Oral Demands] Presenting oral demands (eating, brushing teeth) while a client is actively engaged with a gross motor fall hazard (jumping, swinging, therapy ball) poses a critical risk of choking or injury.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Medical Contraindication: Fall Hazards During Respiratory Distress] Engaging a client in gross motor fall hazards, such as jumping or obstacle courses, during an active asthma attack or respiratory distress is medically unsafe.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Gross Motor With Severe Topography] Prompting gross motor play or obstacle courses while a client is exhibiting severe topographies (like head banging or bolting) introduces chaotic movement that severely exacerbates the risk of physical injury.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Sharp Objects During Gross Motor] Engaging in gross motor activities (like jumping or obstacle courses) while handling sharp objects (like scissors) presents a severe risk of laceration or puncture upon falling.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Gross Motor With SIB] Engaging in dynamic gross motor activities (like swinging or jumping) while actively committing self-injurious behavior drastically increases the risk of catastrophic fall and injury.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Gross Motor During Lethargy] Permitting or prompting a fatigued, lethargic, or slumping client to engage in gross motor fall hazards (like therapy balls or jumping) poses a significant risk of physical injury due to compromised motor control.
* EXC: [IS_SLIP_HAZARD] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Slip Risk During Gross Motor] Combining gross motor jumping or running obstacle courses with slip hazard environments (such as wet bathroom floors or water play tables) drastically elevates the risk of slip-and-fall injuries.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Gross Motor With Aggression] A client engaging in active physical aggression while navigating gross motor fall hazards (like a therapy ball or obstacle course) poses a severe mutual fall and physical injury risk to both client and staff.
* EXC: [IS_VIBRATING_ENVIRONMENT] | [Safety Violation: Unsecured Gross Motor Activity] Engaging in dynamic gross motor activities (e.g., therapy ball, obstacle courses, swinging, jumping) inside a moving vehicle is physically incompatible with seatbelt safety laws and presents an acute risk of traumatic injury.

---

## [IS_FINE_MOTOR_TARGET]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Fine Motor Tools During Active Crisis] It is an extreme physical safety hazard to present fine motor tools (e.g., scissors, pencils) while a client is in an active crisis requiring physical blocking or incident reporting.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Fine Motor Target in Dark Environment] Precision fine motor tasks (e.g., cutting with scissors, tracing) require clear visual feedback and supervision, making them physically impossible and medically unsafe to perform in a dark environment.
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Physical Impossibility: Simultaneous Fine and Gross Motor Targets] Focused fine motor tasks (tracing, bead stringing) are physically incompatible with the gross body movements required for jumping with two feet or catching a ball.
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Medical/Physical Impossibility: Fine Motor Target with Hand Compromise] A client cannot engage in precise fine motor targets (pincer grasp, stringing beads) while simultaneously biting or scratching their own hands.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EXERTION] | [Physical Impossibility: Fine Motor during High Exertion] Fine motor tasks demand stability and focus and cannot be physically accomplished during high-exertion activities such as running, jumping, or pacing rapidly.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_MOBILITY_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical Impossibility: Fine Motor in High Mobility Environment] Delicate fine motor activities (using scissors, worksheets) are physically incompatible and unsafe to execute while navigating high-mobility environments like gross motor obstacle courses or playground structures.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Methodological Contradiction: Fine Motor during Imminent Danger] A client undergoing an episode of imminent danger cannot physically or clinically engage in fine motor skills instruction.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical Violation: Fine Motor Demands During Medical Crisis] Requiring purposeful fine motor skill execution (e.g., stringing beads, tying shoes) during an active medical emergency is physically impossible and ethically prohibited.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_CONTACT_WITH_THERAPIST] | [Methodological Mismatch: Fine Motor Precision and Severe Aggression] An individual cannot methodologically execute precise fine motor targets (tracing letters, stringing beads) while actively engaging in severe aggression (biting, head-butting) directed at the therapist.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Fine Motor Precision and Safety Management] An individual requiring protective safety equipment or physical blocks for severe behavior cannot simultaneously be engaged in executing fine motor precision tasks like tracing or pincer grasp exercises.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Fine Motor Demands During Medical Crisis] Forcing fine motor educational targets, such as tracing letters or worksheets, during acute respiratory distress is medically and ethically inappropriate.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_OVERLOAD] | [Methodological Mismatch: Fine Motor During Sensory Overload] It is methodologically and ethically inappropriate to require precise fine motor skill execution while a client is suffering from acute sensory overload.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Physical Impossibility: Fine Motor During Severe Topography] Executing precise fine motor targets (like pincer grasp or using a pencil) is physically impossible while the client is concurrently engaging in severe, dangerous topographies like bolting or smashing.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Fine Motor With Lethargy] Requiring a significantly lethargic or slumping client to execute fine motor tasks, particularly those involving sharp objects like scissors, introduces a high physical injury risk.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Physical Impossibility: Fine Motor During Aggression] It is physically impossible to safely and accurately execute fine motor targets (like tracing or stringing beads) while simultaneously directing active physical aggression toward staff.

---

## [IS_FOOD_PREP_AREA]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_CONFINED] | [Physical Impossibility: Confined Space vs Kitchen] A client cannot simultaneously be in a confined space (vehicle, bathroom) and a food preparation area (kitchen, dining room).
* EXC: [IS_CONTAGIOUS] | [Health Code Violation: Illness in Food Prep Area] Permitting a contagious client with active illness symptoms to be in a food preparation or dining area violates basic health, safety, and sanitation protocols.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Safety Hazard: Food Prep in the Dark] Conducting activities in a food preparation area or kitchen within a dark environment is an extreme physical safety hazard.
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Safety & Structural Mismatch: Food Preparation and Gross Motor Activities] Running gross motor skill acquisition programs (e.g., catching balls, jumping, obstacle courses) in a kitchen or food preparation area poses severe physical safety risks (slips, sharp edges) and hygiene violations.
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Medical & Sanitation Violation: Hand Compromise in Food Areas] Participating in food preparation or utilizing kitchen/dining areas with actively compromised skin (bitten hands, bleeding scratches) is a severe sanitation hazard and health code violation.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_MOBILITY_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical Environment Mismatch: Food Prep and High Mobility] Kitchens and food preparation areas are physically and functionally incompatible with high mobility environments such as outdoor playgrounds or gross motor obstacle courses.
* EXC: [IS_OUTDOOR_MATERIAL] | [Physical Impossibility: Food Prep Area and Outdoor Material] It is physically incompatible to utilize large-scale outdoor playground equipment or a gross motor obstacle course within food preparation areas such as a kitchen, dining room, or store.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | [Sanitary and Environmental Mismatch: Food Prep and Toileting] Engaging in a toileting or wiping routine within a kitchen or dining area represents a severe sanitary violation and physical contradiction.
* EXC: [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL] | [Physical Environment Contradiction: Food Prep and Traffic Hazard] A kitchen or dining room does not contain parking lots or active traffic hazards, making simultaneous occurrence physically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_VIBRATING_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical Environment Contradiction: Food Prep and Vehicle] A kitchen or dining room is a stationary room within a building, structurally contradicting the environment of a moving vehicle or transport.

---

## [IS_FRAGMENTABLE_MATERIAL]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_CHOKING_HAZARD] | [Physical/Environmental Hazard Requirement] Materials that are fragmentable fundamentally break down into smaller pieces, which inherently creates the physical condition necessary to be classified as a choking hazard.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_BIO_HAZARD] | [Safety Hazard: Ingestion Risk] Providing fragmentable materials like playdough or kinetic sand to a client exhibiting bio-hazardous behaviors like pica or mouthing non-edible items poses a severe ingestion and toxicity risk.
* EXC: [IS_CONTAGIOUS] | [Health Code Violation: Contaminated Permeable Materials] Providing highly permeable and non-sanitizable materials (playdough, kinetic sand) to a contagious individual is a severe infection control violation.
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | [Physical Impossibility: Tutoring with Fragmentable Materials] It is physically impractical to complete writing or tracing worksheets while the hands are simultaneously manipulating fragmentable materials like kinetic sand or playdough.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_FRAGMENTABLE_MATERIAL' forces 'IS_CHOKING_HAZARD'. 'IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE' forces 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION'. 'IS_CHOKING_HAZARD' and 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' cannot co-occur: [Ethical Violation: Lethal Harm Prevention] Using planned ignoring when a client is actively engaging in a choking hazard (e.g., swallowing small objects) is a lethal medical and ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Medical Risk: Infection from Fragmentable Materials] Exposing actively compromised, bitten, or scratched hands to particulate, fragmentable materials (kinetic sand, playdough) introduces a severe medical risk of infection and material embedding in open wounds.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Severe Medical Hazard: Pica and Fragmentable Materials] Providing fragmentable sensory materials like kinetic sand or playdough to a client in imminent danger due to active Pica (mouthing non-edibles, swallowing small objects) creates an immediate, severe choking and bowel obstruction hazard.
* EXC: [IS_PICA] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Fragmentable Materials and Pica] Providing fragmentable materials such as kinetic sand or playdough to a client actively engaging in Pica creates an immediate and severe swallowing and choking hazard.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | [Sanitary Contradiction: Fragmentable Materials and Toileting] Utilizing fragmentable play materials like kinetic sand or playdough during a toileting or wiping routine creates a significant sanitary and contamination risk.

---

## [IS_GI_DISTRESS]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AAC_TARGET] | [Ethical Violation: Skill Acquisition during Medical Distress] Enforcing skill acquisition and communication targets while the client is actively experiencing medical GI distress or refusing meals due to illness is an ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_ACADEMIC] | [Ethical Violation: Academic Demands during Medical Distress] Enforcing seated academic targets like patterning, tracing, or counting when the child is experiencing physical GI issues is unethical and methodologically inappropriate.
* EXC: [IS_ACADEMIC_MATERIAL] | [Ethical Violation: Academic Materials during Medical Distress] Enforcing the use of academic materials (worksheets, flashcards) when a client is experiencing physical GI distress is unethical.
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction of Medical Symptoms] Utilizing planned ignoring for distress behaviors stemming from medical GI issues (constipation, lack of appetite) violates ethical guidelines regarding necessary medical care.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_DEMAND] | [Ethical Violation: Exposure Therapy during Medical Distress] Enforcing auditory demands (tolerating loud noises) while a client is actively suffering from GI distress or physical illness is an ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | [Ethical Violation: Aversives during Medical Distress] Implementing aversive procedures like escape extinction or response cost while the client is physically suffering from GI distress is highly unethical.
* EXC: [IS_EDIBLE] | [Medical Contraindication: Edibles during GI Distress] It is medically and ethically contraindicated to present edible items as reinforcement when a client is experiencing active GI distress, constipation, or a lack of appetite.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Escape Extinction during GI Distress] Maintaining clinical demands via escape extinction when a client is sick or experiencing severe GI distress violates medical clearance and ethical care guidelines.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Ethical Violation: Timeout during GI Distress] Isolating or punishing a client via timeout for behaviors potentially driven by an underlying medical issue (GI distress) is ethically prohibited.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction during GI Distress] Ignoring vocalizations or behavior that stem from active illness or GI distress denies the client appropriate medical care and violates ethical guidelines.
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Medical & Methodological Contraindication: GI Distress and Skill Acquisition] Conducting physical/gross motor skill acquisition programs (e.g., jumping, imitation) is methodologically unsound and medically contraindicated when a learner is experiencing acute physiological pain or GI distress.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Medical Symptoms] Applying high-effort punishment procedures (e.g., Positive Practice Overcorrection) while a client is suffering from acute GI distress or constipation violates BACB ethical guidelines mandating the ruling out of medical variables.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EXERTION] | [Medical Contraindication: High Exertion during GI Distress] Encouraging or demanding high physical exertion from a client experiencing acute physiological distress, constipation, or lack of appetite is medically unsafe.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_MOBILITY_ENVIRONMENT] | [Medical Contraindication: High Mobility Demands during GI Distress] Utilizing high-mobility environments (obstacle courses, extensive playground use) is medically contraindicated when the learner is actively suffering from physiological/GI distress.
* EXC: [IS_NO_MEDICAL] | [Medical Contradiction: GI Distress and No Medical Concerns] It is a direct logical and medical contradiction to report active gastrointestinal distress or constipation while simultaneously documenting that no medical issues were reported.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DEMAND] | [Medical and Ethical Violation: Oral Demand during GI Distress] Forcing an oral demand, such as eating a variety of foods, when a client is actively refusing meals due to GI distress or constipation is medically and ethically contraindicated.
* EXC: [IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Medical Symptoms] Applying restrictive punishment to a client experiencing a medical issue such as GI distress is an ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Ethical Violation: Waiting Out Medical Symptoms] Applying an extinction or 'wait out' procedure to a suspected medical symptom, such as severe GI distress, is an ethical violation of BACB guidelines that require addressing medical needs immediately.

---

## [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AGGRESSION] | [Safety Violation: Gross Motor Targets during Aggression] Attempting structured gross motor targets like jumping or catching a ball while the client is actively engaging in aggression (punching, kicking, head-butting) is physically unfeasible and dangerous.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_DISTRESS] | [Medical Contradiction: Gross Motor Targets during Ear Infection] Implementing gross motor demands like jumping or catching a ball is medically contraindicated when a client has an ear infection, which affects vestibular balance and causes significant pain.
* EXC: [IS_CHOKING_HAZARD] | [Physical Safety: Aspiration Risk During Motor Tasks] Allowing a client to hold or mouth choking hazards (tokens, beads) while engaging in gross motor targets (jumping, obstacle courses) creates an extreme risk of aspiration and choking.
* EXC: [IS_CONFINED] | [Physical Safety: Gross Motor in Confined Space] Attempting gross motor targets like jumping, catching balls, or obstacle courses within a confined space like a vehicle or bathroom poses a severe risk of injury.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Safety Hazard: Gross Motor in the Dark] Running gross motor targets such as obstacle courses, jumping, or catching a ball in a sensory dark room creates a direct tripping and injury hazard.
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | [Physical Impossibility: Educational Tutoring and Gross Motor] It is physically impossible to complete fine-tuned educational tutoring tasks (tracing letters, completing worksheets) while simultaneously performing full-body gross motor targets (jumping, catching a ball).
* EXC: [IS_ELECTRONIC_DEVICE] | [Physical Impossibility: Electronic Device during Gross Motor] It is highly unsafe and physically incompatible to meaningfully engage in gross motor targets (catching a ball, crossing midline) while actively holding or operating an electronic device.
* EXC: [IS_ELOPEMENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Gross Motor Target during Elopement] A client actively eloping or bolting cannot simultaneously receive clinical instruction in targeted gross motor skills like catching a ball.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Timeout and Instructional Target] Exclusionary timeout fundamentally requires removal from the instructional setting, making active engagement in a gross motor target physically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_FINE_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Physical Impossibility: Simultaneous Fine and Gross Motor Targets] Focused fine motor tasks (tracing, bead stringing) are physically incompatible with the gross body movements required for jumping with two feet or catching a ball.
* EXC: [IS_FOOD_PREP_AREA] | [Safety & Structural Mismatch: Food Preparation and Gross Motor Activities] Running gross motor skill acquisition programs (e.g., catching balls, jumping, obstacle courses) in a kitchen or food preparation area poses severe physical safety risks (slips, sharp edges) and hygiene violations.
* EXC: [IS_GI_DISTRESS] | [Medical & Methodological Contraindication: GI Distress and Skill Acquisition] Conducting physical/gross motor skill acquisition programs (e.g., jumping, imitation) is methodologically unsound and medically contraindicated when a learner is experiencing acute physiological pain or GI distress.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_AROUSAL] | [Methodological Mismatch: High Arousal and Gross Motor Acquisition] Attempting to teach gross motor acquisition targets requires attending; it is methodologically impossible and ineffective to do so when the client is highly agitated, crying, or hyperactive.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Methodological & Safety Mismatch: Skill Acquisition During Crisis] It is physically dangerous and methodologically impossible to run gross motor acquisition targets while a client is in active imminent danger (aggression, SIB, elopement). Crisis management must supersede teaching.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Medical Safety Mismatch: Gross Motor Targets During Medical Crisis] It is medically unsafe and ethically prohibited to demand gross motor targets requiring physical movement when a client is experiencing a medical crisis such as an asthma attack, dehydration, or seizure.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DEMAND] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Gross Motor Targets and Oral Demands] Combining gross motor targets like jumping with two feet or catching a ball with concurrent oral demands such as eating a variety of foods poses a severe, immediate choking hazard.
* EXC: [IS_PICA] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Gross Motor Targets and Pica] Targeting dynamic gross motor skills like jumping or catching a ball while a client is actively engaging in Pica creates a critical and immediate choking hazard.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Medical Contraindication: Exertion During Respiratory Distress] Targeting gross motor skills requiring physical exertion while a client is experiencing respiratory distress (e.g., asthma) poses a severe physical and medical risk.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Methodological Mismatch: Skill Acquisition During Severe Crisis] It is a safety violation and methodologically invalid to attempt teaching gross motor targets while the client is actively engaging in severe topographies like elopement, head-banging, or severe aggression.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | [Methodological Mismatch: Skill Acquisition During SIB] Teaching gross motor targets is strictly contraindicated while the client is actively engaging in self-injurious behavior.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Methodological Mismatch: Motor Demands During Lethargy] It is methodologically and physically inappropriate to demand effortful gross motor targets (jumping, catching a ball) from a client who is lethargic, fatigued, and passively slumping.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Skill Acquisition During Aggression] Teaching gross motor targets cannot safely or methodologically occur while the client is actively engaging in staff-directed aggression.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | [Safety and Sanitary Mismatch: Gross Motor and Toileting] Executing high-movement gross motor tasks (e.g., catching a ball, jumping with two feet) while attempting to complete a toileting routine is unsafe and physically incompatible.
* EXC: [IS_VIBRATING_ENVIRONMENT] | [Safety Violation: Gross Motor in a Vehicle] Practicing large gross motor targets like jumping or catching a ball within a moving vehicle poses a severe safety hazard and violates seatbelt/restraint requirements.
* EXC: [IS_VISUAL_DEPENDENT_TASK] | [Physical Contradiction: Gross Motor vs Fine Visual Task] Performing gross motor activities such as jumping or navigating an obstacle course is physically incompatible with simultaneously executing stationary, fine-motor visual tasks like tracing letters or completing worksheets.
* EXC: [IS_VOCATIONAL] | [Physical Contradiction: Gross Motor vs Vocational Assembly] Catching a ball or practicing jumping is physically incompatible with focused, stationary vocational tasks such as sorting mail or completing fine assembly tasks.

---

## [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | Derived Chain (IS_HAND_COMPROMISE -> IS_SIB -> IS_IMMINENT_DANGER): [Clinical Classification Dependency] Behaviors that physically compromise one's own hands or arms, such as biting or scratching oneself, are inherently self-inflicted tissue damage, thereby strictly requiring classification as Self-Injurious Behavior (SIB). | [Ethical/Safety Requirement: SIB] Self-injurious behavior poses a direct, immediate physical threat to the client's health, strictly necessitating clinical and ethical handling under an imminent danger protocol.
* REQ: [IS_SIB] | [Clinical Classification Dependency] Behaviors that physically compromise one's own hands or arms, such as biting or scratching oneself, are inherently self-inflicted tissue damage, thereby strictly requiring classification as Self-Injurious Behavior (SIB).

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AAC_MATERIAL] | [Physical Contradiction: Hand Compromise limits AAC Use] Actively biting or scratching one's own hands physically precludes the simultaneous functional fine-motor use of AAC materials like an iPad or PECS book.
* EXC: [IS_AAC_TARGET] | [Physical Contradiction: Hand Compromise limits AAC Targets] Hand compromise behaviors like biting or violently scratching one's hands physically impair the fine motor capabilities required to independently use an AAC device.
* EXC: [IS_ACADEMIC] | [Physical Contradiction: Hand Compromise limits Fine Motor Academics] Biting or aggressively scratching hands physically precludes the ability to engage in academic fine motor tasks like tracing letters or writing one's name.
* EXC: [IS_ACADEMIC_MATERIAL] | [Physical Contradiction: Hand Compromise limits Academic Material Use] You cannot functionally utilize fine motor academic materials (worksheets, crayons, cards) while actively biting or violently scratching your own hands.
* EXC: [IS_ADL_TARGET] | [Physical Contradiction: Hand Compromise limits ADL Execution] Tying shoes, opening containers, or washing hands requires functional hand use, which is physically impossible while simultaneously engaging in biting or scratching one's hands.
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction for Self-Injury] Planned ignoring is ethically contraindicated for self-injurious behavior like biting own hand or scratching skin due to the significant risk of permanent tissue damage.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | [Ethical Violation: Aversives on Self-Injury] Applying extinction or aversive procedures like planned ignoring to self-injurious behavior (biting hands, scratching) poses an unethical risk of tissue damage.
* EXC: [IS_CHAINING_FORMAT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_HAND_COMPROMISE' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' forces 'IS_CHAINED'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINED' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching ADLs During Crisis] It is methodologically invalid and physically unsafe to conduct chained daily living activities (e.g., tying shoes, hygiene routines) while a client is in imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | [Physical Impossibility: Educational Tutoring with Hand Compromise] Educational tutoring tasks such as writing or tracing require functional use of the hands, which is physically impossible if the client is actively biting or scratching their own hands.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Escape Extinction during SIB] Enforcing demands and blocking escape while a client is engaging in self-injurious behavior (hand-biting/scratching) compromises client safety.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Ethical Violation: Tactical Break during Hand Compromise SIB] Implementing a tactical break or exclusionary timeout while a client is actively biting or scratching their hands (SIB) leaves a self-injuring client unattended.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction for SIB] Using planned ignoring procedures for self-injurious behavior such as biting or scratching one's own hands violates immediate safety protocols.
* EXC: [IS_FINE_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Medical/Physical Impossibility: Fine Motor Target with Hand Compromise] A client cannot engage in precise fine motor targets (pincer grasp, stringing beads) while simultaneously biting or scratching their own hands.
* EXC: [IS_FOOD_PREP_AREA] | [Medical & Sanitation Violation: Hand Compromise in Food Areas] Participating in food preparation or utilizing kitchen/dining areas with actively compromised skin (bitten hands, bleeding scratches) is a severe sanitation hazard and health code violation.
* EXC: [IS_FRAGMENTABLE_MATERIAL] | [Medical Risk: Infection from Fragmentable Materials] Exposing actively compromised, bitten, or scratched hands to particulate, fragmentable materials (kinetic sand, playdough) introduces a severe medical risk of infection and material embedding in open wounds.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Independence and SIB] Active hand compromise (a form of Self-Injurious Behavior) inherently contradicts the clinical parameters of independence, which require an absolute absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_MESSY_MATERIAL] | [Ethical and Medical Violation: Infection Risk with Messy Materials] Presenting messy materials like water play or kinetic sand to a client whose hands are compromised with open wounds (e.g., from severe scratching or biting) introduces a high risk of infection, representing a medical contraindication.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_PROMPT] | [Medical and Safety Violation: Physical Prompting on Compromised Hands] Applying physical prompts or manually guiding hands that are currently compromised by severe self-injurious behavior (e.g., actively biting or scratching) is medically unsafe and risks exacerbating tissue damage.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Self-Injury vs Regulated State] Hand compromise (e.g., biting or scratching own hands) is a form of self-injurious behavior, which fundamentally contradicts a regulated, calm state characterized by the absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Ethical Violation: Harm Prevention During Hand Compromise] A client actively engaging in hand compromise (biting or scratching own hands and arms) cannot safely handle sharp objects like scissors due to the severe risk of exacerbating the injury or causing accidental cuts.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Self-Injury vs. Regulated State] Active self-injury such as biting one's own hand is a clear indicator of distress or dysregulation, completely contradicting the fully regulated, happy, and highly motivated state defined by the Smart Chip Regulated category.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Self-Injury vs. Smooth Transition] A client actively engaging in self-injurious behaviors directed at their hands or arms is in distress, which inherently contradicts transitioning smoothly, independently, and happily to a new area.
* EXC: [IS_SOCIAL_PLAY] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Self-Injury vs. Cooperative Play] A client actively biting their own arms or engaging in self-injurious scratching cannot simultaneously sustain cooperative, positive social play and peer interaction.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_HAND_COMPROMISE' forces 'IS_SIB'. 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_SIB' and 'IS_INDEPENDENT' cannot co-occur: [Definitional Contradiction: Independence vs. SIB] Self-Injurious Behavior is maladaptive, which definitionally contradicts the 'Independent' state that strictly requires displaying an absence of maladaptive behaviors.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | [Physical Impossibility: Hand Compromise and Toileting] If a client is actively biting their own hands or scratching their arms, they are physically unable to safely or hygienically wipe independently or complete a toileting routine.
* EXC: [IS_VISUAL_DEPENDENT_TASK] | [Physical Impossibility: Hand Compromise and Visual Tasks] A client actively engaging in hand-directed self-injury (e.g., biting hands, severe scratching) cannot physically grasp writing utensils to trace letters, write their name, or complete worksheets.
* EXC: [IS_VOCATIONAL] | [Physical Impossibility: Hand Compromise and Vocational Tasks] Actively biting or scratching one's hands precludes the physical capability required to sort mail, wipe tables, or complete fine vocational assembly tasks.
* EXC: [IS_WATER_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical Impossibility: Hand Compromise and Water Routine] If a client is actively engaging in hand-biting or scratching, they cannot physically perform a thorough and hygienic hand-washing or tooth-brushing routine.

---

## [IS_HAND_HELD_ITEM]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_TRANSITION] | [Methodological Dependency] The use of a transition object conceptually and methodologically requires a transition to be actively occurring in order to be functionally relevant.

---

## [IS_HIGH_AROUSAL]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AAC_TARGET] | [Methodological Mismatch: Skill Acquisition during High Arousal] Teaching fine motor AAC targets is methodologically unfeasible when a client is highly agitated, crying, or pacing rapidly.
* EXC: [IS_ACADEMIC] | [Methodological Mismatch: Academics during High Arousal] Expected seated academic tasks (tracing, sorting) cannot be methodologically implemented while a client is highly agitated, pacing the room rapidly, or highly distracted.
* EXC: [IS_ADL_TARGET] | [Methodological Mismatch: ADLs during High Arousal] Teaching precise daily living skills (like tying shoes or proper utensil use) is methodologically unfeasible when the client is agitated, crying, or highly hyperactive.
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Methodological Mismatch: High Arousal and Gross Motor Acquisition] Attempting to teach gross motor acquisition targets requires attending; it is methodologically impossible and ineffective to do so when the client is highly agitated, crying, or hyperactive.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Independence and High Arousal] The definition of independence requires smooth fluency and an absence of maladaptive behavior, which is strictly incompatible with being highly agitated, crying, or severely distracted.
* EXC: [IS_LOW_AROUSAL] | [Physical Impossibility: Simultaneous High and Low Arousal] A client cannot physically be in a state of high arousal (e.g., rapid pacing, hyperactivity) while simultaneously exhibiting low arousal states (e.g., lethargic, passively slumping, sluggish).
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DEMAND] | [Physical Safety Hazard: High Arousal and Oral Demands] Placing oral demands like eating or brushing teeth while a client is in a state of high arousal (e.g., agitated and crying, pacing rapidly) represents a significant choking and aspiration risk.
* EXC: [IS_PEER_TARGET] | [Methodological Mismatch: Peer Targets during High Arousal] It is methodologically impossible and clinically inappropriate to target complex peer interactions, such as conversational turn-taking, while a client is in an active state of high arousal (agitated, crying, hyperactive).
* EXC: [IS_PICA] | [Physical Safety Hazard: High Arousal and Pica] Engaging in high arousal behaviors, such as agitated crying or running rapidly, while attempting to swallow small non-edible objects (Pica) poses a critical aspiration and choking hazard.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Behavioral Contradiction: High Arousal vs Regulated State] A client demonstrating high arousal (agitated, crying, hyperactive) cannot simultaneously be in a regulated state (calm, relaxed, neutral affect).
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Physiological Impossibility: High Arousal vs. Lethargy] High arousal states (high rate of movement, pacing rapidly, hyperactivity) are physiologically incompatible with sleep lethargy (slowed response times, passively slumping, severe fatigue).
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | [Physical Mismatch: High Arousal and Toileting] Rapidly pacing the room or demonstrating a high rate of movement is physically and safely incompatible with sitting on a toilet to complete a routine.
* EXC: [IS_VISUAL_DEPENDENT_TASK] | [Physical Mismatch: High Arousal and Visual Tasks] A client who is highly agitated, pacing rapidly, or hyperactive cannot physically sustain the stationary visual attention and fine motor control required for tracing letters or focusing on worksheets.
* EXC: [IS_VOCAL_VERBAL_TARGET] | [Methodological Mismatch: High Arousal and Verbal Targets] It is methodologically contradictory to attempt teaching complex conversational turn-taking or structured verbal intraverbals while a client is highly agitated, crying, or pacing rapidly.
* EXC: [IS_VOCATIONAL] | [Methodological Mismatch: High Arousal and Vocational Tasks] Rapid pacing or severe agitation physically and methodologically prevents the sustained attention required to sort mail or assemble items.

---

## [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AAC_TARGET] | [Methodological Contradiction: Skill Targeting during Punishment] Targeting functional communication skills during high-effort positive practice overcorrection is methodologically incorrect, as punishment suppresses behavior while skill acquisition requires reinforcement.
* EXC: [IS_ACADEMIC] | [Methodological Contradiction: Academics during Overcorrection] Conducting academic readiness and skill acquisition tasks simultaneously with positive practice overcorrection is methodologically contradictory.
* EXC: [IS_AGGRESSION] | [Safety Mismatch: High Effort Punishment during Active Aggression] Utilizing high-effort physical punishment like overcorrection while a client is actively engaging in aggression (punching, kicking) is a severe safety risk that will escalate dangerous behavior.
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Methodological Contradiction: Simultaneous Extinction and Punishment] Planned ignoring (withholding attention) and positive practice overcorrection (which requires physical guidance and interaction) are methodologically contradictory and cannot be implemented simultaneously.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Punishment during Medical Vulnerability] Implementing overcorrection or high-effort punishment on a client actively suffering from the pain and imbalance of an ear infection is an ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_CONTAGIOUS] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Illness] Implementing high-effort punishment (like overcorrection or positive practice) while a client is suffering from contagious illness symptoms is medically contraindicated and highly unethical.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Punitive Solitary Confinement] Enforcing high-effort punishment procedures (overcorrection) inside a dark room mirrors abusive solitary confinement practices and violates BACB ethical standards.
* EXC: [IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED] | [Physical Contradiction: Distance vs Physical Guidance] Overcorrection typically requires close physical proximity or physical guidance, which directly contradicts a safety mandate requiring a safety distance between the clinician and client.
* EXC: [IS_EDIBLE] | [Methodological Contradiction: Reinforcement during Punishment] Providing edible reinforcement simultaneously with high-effort punishment (overcorrection) violates behavioral principles by confounding reinforcement and punishment contingencies.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_REINFORCER] | [Methodological Contradiction: Escape Reinforcer and High Effort Punishment] Delivering an escape reinforcer (break) completely contradicts implementing high-effort punishment (positive practice/overcorrection) at the same time.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Timeout and High Effort Punishment] Exclusionary timeout requires the total removal of activity, whereas high-effort punishment demands active physical engagement. Both cannot be applied at the same exact moment.
* EXC: [IS_EXECUTIVE_FUNCTIONING] | [Definitional Contradiction: Executive Functioning during Overcorrection] High-effort punishment (overcorrection) is a forced, externally directed consequence, which nullifies self-directed executive functioning and independent problem-solving.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | [Methodological Contradiction: Extinction and Punishment] Planned ignoring (extinction) cannot occur concurrently with high-effort punishment (overcorrection), as overcorrection demands active physical intervention from staff.
* EXC: [IS_FALL_HAZARD] | [Safety Hazard: Punishment on Fall Hazard] Forcing high-effort punishment routines while a client is stationed on a fall hazard (swing, therapy ball) presents an immense risk of injury.
* EXC: [IS_GI_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Medical Symptoms] Applying high-effort punishment procedures (e.g., Positive Practice Overcorrection) while a client is suffering from acute GI distress or constipation violates BACB ethical guidelines mandating the ruling out of medical variables.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Ethical & Methodological Violation: Punishing Independence] Applying high-effort punishment procedures like Positive Practice Overcorrection is ethically prohibited and methodologically contradictory when the client is displaying mastery and an absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_LOW_AROUSAL] | [Ethical Violation: High Effort Punishment for Fatigued Client] Implementing high-effort punishment procedures, such as positive practice or overcorrection, on a client who is physically fatigued, sluggish, or lethargic is an ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical and Medical Violation: Punishment During Medical Crisis] Administering high-effort punishment (like overcorrection) to a client actively experiencing a medical crisis (such as a seizure or asthma attack) is medically unsafe and a severe ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Punishment During Medical Distress] Implementing high-effort punishment procedures on a client who is experiencing obvious medical distress, pain, or illness symptoms violates ethical mandates for client dignity and safety.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: High-Effort Punishment during Oral Distress] Implementing physically demanding punishment procedures (like overcorrection) on a client actively experiencing oral distress or dental pain is ethically abusive and violates BACB harm prevention guidelines.
* EXC: [IS_PEER_TARGET] | [Methodological Mismatch: Overcorrection and Peer Targets] It is methodologically impossible and ethically inappropriate to simultaneously implement a high-effort punishment procedure (like overcorrection) while actively prompting cooperative skill acquisition targets such as peer play or turn-taking.
* EXC: [IS_PREMACK_CONTINGENCY] | [Methodological Violation: Overcorrection as Premack Demand] It is a methodological violation of Skinnerian principles to frame a high-effort punishment procedure (overcorrection) as the 'First' demand in a Premack contingency (First/Then).
* EXC: [IS_PROXIMITY_PAIRING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Reinforcing Punishment] Providing proximity pairing (e.g., tickles, deep pressure, pairing with toys) concurrently with high effort punishment (e.g., overcorrection) fundamentally violates behavioral methodology by reinforcing a punishing consequence.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Methodological Mismatch: Punishing a Regulated State] Implementing high effort punishment (overcorrection) on a client who is regulated and demonstrating an absence of maladaptive behavior is a methodological and ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_REMOTE] | [Physical Impossibility: Remote Physical Guidance] High effort punishment like positive practice overcorrection requires physical guidance or immediate proximity to enforce, which is physically impossible to conduct via remote telehealth.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Medical Contraindication: High Effort Punishment During Respiratory Distress] Enforcing high effort punishment (overcorrection) while a client is in respiratory distress (e.g., asthma attack) is medically dangerous and unethical.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_DISTRESS] | [Medical/Ethical Contraindication: High Effort Punishment During Sensory Distress] Forcing high effort physical punishment on a client experiencing sensory distress such as a severe headache is ethically and medically contraindicated.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_OVERLOAD] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Sensory Distress] Applying high-effort punishment (e.g., positive practice overcorrection) to a client experiencing sensory overload violates ethical guidelines regarding trauma-informed care and harm prevention.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Physical Exhaustion] Mandating high-effort punishment such as overcorrection when a client is lethargic or physically exhausted is medically contraindicated and abusive.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Methodological Mismatch: Punishing Regulated Behavior] Applying positive practice overcorrection is methodologically incompatible with a state of being fully regulated, happy, cooperative, and highly motivated.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Punishing Smooth Transitions] It is methodologically contradictory to apply high-effort punishment during a smooth, independent, and happy transition, as there is no maladaptive behavior occurring to warrant a corrective procedure.
* EXC: [IS_SOCIAL_PLAY] | [Methodological Mismatch: Punishing Prosocial Behavior] Applying high-effort punishment is methodologically incompatible with the client actively engaging in positive, cooperative social play and sharing items with peers.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | [Methodological Mismatch: Punishing Spontaneous Skills] It is methodologically inappropriate and contradictory to apply high-effort punishment immediately surrounding a spontaneous demonstration of a mastered, appropriate skill.
* EXC: [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL] | [Safety/Ethical Violation: Punishment in Traffic Hazard] Administering high-effort punishment (e.g., positive practice overcorrection) in a high-danger traffic environment like a parking lot or street is extremely unsafe and an ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Active Punishment vs Wait Out] Overcorrection is an active, high-effort consequence requiring physical prompting, whereas a 'wait out' procedure relies on extinction/ignoring. Simultaneously applying both is methodologically impossible.

---

## [IS_HIGH_EXERTION]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AAC_MATERIAL] | [Physical Contradiction: High Exertion limits AAC Material] Utilizing a fine-motor AAC device, tablet, or PECS book is physically unfeasible while actively engaging in high-exertion activities like jumping, running an obstacle course, or catching a ball.
* EXC: [IS_AAC_TARGET] | [Physical Contradiction: AAC Targets during High Exertion] The precise fine motor and visual attention required for AAC communication targeting is physically incompatible with simultaneous high physical exertion such as running or jumping.
* EXC: [IS_ACADEMIC] | [Methodological Contradiction: Academics during High Exertion] Engaging in high-exertion gross motor activities precludes the physical stability and focus required for seated academic tasks like tracing or counting.
* EXC: [IS_ACADEMIC_MATERIAL] | [Physical Contradiction: Academic Materials during High Exertion] Utilizing worksheets, crayons, or dry-erase markers is physically impractical while a client is simultaneously engaging in high-exertion activities such as running, jumping, or navigating an obstacle course.
* EXC: [IS_ADL_TARGET] | [Physical Contradiction: ADLs during High Exertion] Executing precise daily living skills like brushing teeth or tying shoes is physically incompatible with engaging in high-exertion gross motor activities simultaneously.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_DISTRESS] | [Medical Contradiction: High Exertion during Ear Infection] High physical exertion (obstacle courses, running) is medically contraindicated for a client experiencing an active ear infection due to vestibular disruption and pain.
* EXC: [IS_CHOKING_HAZARD] | [Physical Safety: Aspiration Risk During Exertion] High physical exertion (running, rapid pacing) combined with access to choking hazards creates an extreme, immediate risk of accidental ingestion or aspiration.
* EXC: [IS_CONFINED] | [Physical Safety: High Exertion in Confined Space] Engaging in high-exertion behaviors (running, rapid pacing) within tightly confined spaces (vehicles, bathrooms) poses a direct physical hazard to the client and therapist.
* EXC: [IS_CONTAGIOUS] | [Medical Contraindication: Exertion While Ill] Forcing or allowing high physical exertion for a client actively exhibiting contagious illness symptoms poses an acute medical and physiological risk.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Safety Hazard: High Exertion in the Dark] Engaging in high exertion (rapid pacing, running) within a dark environment removes necessary visual feedback, creating a critical collision or tripping hazard.
* EXC: [IS_EDIBLE] | [Safety Hazard: Edibles during High Exertion] Providing edible items while a client is engaged in high physical exertion (jumping, pacing rapidly, running) presents a severe choking hazard.
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | [Physical Impossibility: Tutoring during High Exertion] Educational tutoring requires physical stability (sitting at a desk) and cannot be executed while a client is engaged in high exertion (running, pacing, jumping).
* EXC: [IS_FINE_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Physical Impossibility: Fine Motor during High Exertion] Fine motor tasks demand stability and focus and cannot be physically accomplished during high-exertion activities such as running, jumping, or pacing rapidly.
* EXC: [IS_GI_DISTRESS] | [Medical Contraindication: High Exertion during GI Distress] Encouraging or demanding high physical exertion from a client experiencing acute physiological distress, constipation, or lack of appetite is medically unsafe.
* EXC: [IS_LOW_AROUSAL] | [Physical Impossibility: High Exertion and Low Arousal] It is physically contradictory for a client to be engaged in high exertion activities (such as rapid pacing or jumping) while presenting with low arousal (e.g., lethargic, passively slumping).
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Medical Safety Mismatch: High Exertion During Medical Crisis] A client sustaining or being directed to sustain high physical exertion during a medical emergency (such as an asthma attack, dehydration, or seizure) represents a severe medical danger.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DEMAND] | [Physical Safety Hazard: High Exertion and Oral Demands] Placing oral demands, such as eating or brushing teeth, while a client is engaged in high exertion activities (e.g., jumping, running rapidly) creates a severe, immediate choking hazard.
* EXC: [IS_PICA] | [Physical Safety Hazard: High Exertion and Pica] Engaging in high exertion activities like running or jumping while actively displaying Pica (swallowing small objects) poses a critical aspiration and choking risk.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Medical Contraindication: Exertion During Respiratory Distress] High exertion activities (e.g., jumping, running, obstacle courses) are medically contraindicated when a client is experiencing respiratory distress like asthma.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_DISTRESS] | [Medical Contraindication: Exertion During Sensory Distress] Engaging in high exertion physical activities while a client is suffering from sensory distress (e.g., headache) is medically and ethically inappropriate.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Physiological Impossibility: Exertion vs. Lethargy] High exertion activities such as running, jumping, and completing obstacle courses cannot be physically performed by a client who is exhibiting sleep lethargy, fatigue, or passively slumping.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | [Safety Mismatch: High Exertion and Toileting] Engaging in high exertion activities like jumping, pacing rapidly, or navigating an obstacle course during a toileting routine poses a severe safety and sanitary hazard.
* EXC: [IS_VIBRATING_ENVIRONMENT] | [Safety Violation: High Exertion in a Vehicle] Running, jumping, or pacing rapidly while inside a vehicle represents a severe physical impossibility and acute safety hazard.
* EXC: [IS_VISUAL_DEPENDENT_TASK] | [Physical Contradiction: High Exertion vs Visual Tasks] Rapidly pacing, jumping, or navigating a gross motor obstacle course makes completing fine-motor visual tasks, such as writing and tracing, physically impossible.

---

## [IS_HIGH_MOBILITY_ENVIRONMENT]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_CONFIDENTIAL_DISCUSSION] | [Ethical Violation: HIPAA/Confidentiality Breach] Discussing confidential IEP goals, behavior incidents, or data graphs in public, high-mobility environments (outdoors, playgrounds) violates HIPAA and privacy guidelines.
* EXC: [IS_CONFINED] | [Physical Impossibility: Confined vs High Mobility] It is physically impossible for an environment to be simultaneously a tightly confined space (bathroom/vehicle) and a broad high-mobility environment (playground/outdoors).
* EXC: [IS_CONTAGIOUS] | [Ethical Violation: Public Infection Risk] Taking a contagious client with active illness symptoms into public high-mobility environments (e.g., playgrounds) poses an unethical infection risk to the broader community.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical Impossibility: Dark Sensory vs Outdoors] A setting cannot simultaneously be a controlled, indoor sensory dark room and a public, high-mobility outdoor environment like a playground.
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | [Physical Impossibility: Tutoring in High Mobility Environments] High mobility environments (gross motor obstacle courses, playground equipment) physically preclude safe and effective engagement in desk-based educational tutoring.
* EXC: [IS_FINE_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Physical Impossibility: Fine Motor in High Mobility Environment] Delicate fine motor activities (using scissors, worksheets) are physically incompatible and unsafe to execute while navigating high-mobility environments like gross motor obstacle courses or playground structures.
* EXC: [IS_FOOD_PREP_AREA] | [Physical Environment Mismatch: Food Prep and High Mobility] Kitchens and food preparation areas are physically and functionally incompatible with high mobility environments such as outdoor playgrounds or gross motor obstacle courses.
* EXC: [IS_GI_DISTRESS] | [Medical Contraindication: High Mobility Demands during GI Distress] Utilizing high-mobility environments (obstacle courses, extensive playground use) is medically contraindicated when the learner is actively suffering from physiological/GI distress.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DEMAND] | [Physical Safety Hazard: High Mobility Environment and Oral Demands] Requiring a client to chew food or fulfill oral demands while physically navigating high mobility environments like a gross motor obstacle course or playground equipment represents a significant choking hazard.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | [Sanitary and Environmental Mismatch: Playground Toileting] An outdoor playground or gross motor area does not contain private toileting facilities; conducting a toileting routine in an open playground is physically inappropriate and unethical.
* EXC: [IS_VIBRATING_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical Environment Contradiction: Playground and Vehicle] A playground or expansive gross motor obstacle course cannot physically fit or co-occur within the confined space of a moving vehicle.
* EXC: [IS_WATER_ENVIRONMENT] | [Environmental Mismatch: Playground and Bathroom Routine] A playground setting intrinsically contradicts an indoor bathroom/restroom water environment meant for brushing teeth or structured hand-washing routines.

---

## [IS_HOME_SETTING]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_CAREGIVER_UNAVAILABLE] | [Ethical Violation: Unsupervised Home Session] BACB and standard funding guidelines strictly prohibit conducting in-home therapy sessions when a responsible caregiver or adult is unavailable.
* EXC: [IS_CLINIC_SETTING] | [Physical Impossibility: Simultaneous Distinct Locations] A session or specific event cannot physically occur in a clinical facility and a home setting simultaneously.
* EXC: [IS_OUTDOOR_MATERIAL] | [Physical Impossibility: Home Setting and Outdoor Material] It is physically impossible to host large-scale outdoor playground equipment or water play tables naturally within a standard home environment like a bedroom or kitchen.
* EXC: [IS_SCHOOL_SETTING] | [Physical Impossibility: Simultaneous Distinct Locations] A client cannot physically be in a primary home setting (e.g., bedroom, home kitchen) and a primary school setting (e.g., classroom) at the same time.
* EXC: [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL] | [Physical Environment Contradiction: Home Setting and Traffic Hazard] Navigating a parking lot or a street crossing safely cannot physically occur inside the enclosed architectural setting of a bedroom, living room, or kitchen.
* EXC: [IS_VIBRATING_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical Environment Contradiction: Home Setting and Vehicle] A home setting (e.g., bedroom, kitchen, living room) is a stationary building, structurally and physically incompatible with being inside a moving vehicle.

---

## [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AAC_TARGET] | [Safety Violation: Skill Targeting during Crisis] Attempting to teach fine motor communication targets while the client is engaging in imminent danger (e.g., eloping, severe self-injury) is a severe safety and ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_ACADEMIC] | [Safety Violation: Academics during Crisis] Implementing academic demands like counting or tracing while a client is in imminent danger (bolting, severe self-injury) is a severe safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_ACADEMIC_MATERIAL] | [Safety Mismatch: Academic Materials during Imminent Danger] Utilizing worksheets, crayons, or flashcards is completely physically unmanageable and inappropriate during a crisis like bolting or severe SIB.
* EXC: [IS_ADL_TARGET] | [Safety Violation: ADL Demands during Crisis] Forcing daily living skill demands while a client is in imminent danger (eloping, severe self-injury) is medically dangerous and unethical.
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction during Imminent Danger] Using planned ignoring for behaviors that present an imminent danger (e.g., elopement, head-banging, gouging eyes) is a severe ethical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_BLOCKER] | [Safety Mismatch: Auditory Blockers during Imminent Danger] Wearing noise-canceling headphones or interacting with music players during imminent danger (running into the street, eloping) prevents the client from hearing crucial safety directives, posing a severe physical risk.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_DEMAND] | [Safety Mismatch: Auditory Demands during Crisis] Attempting to teach tolerance to loud noises while the client is engaging in imminent danger (gouging eyes, bolting) is methodologically and physically inappropriate.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | [Safety Violation: Aversives during Imminent Crisis] Applying planned ignoring or response cost during an imminent danger crisis (like bolting into the street or gouging eyes) prevents necessary physical safety interventions and is ethically prohibited.
