* EXC: [IS_BCBA_LEVEL_TASK] | [Safety Violation: Administrative Tasks during Crisis] A BCBA cannot methodologically or safely engage in administrative tasks like reviewing IEP goals or discussing strategies while the client is actively in an imminent danger state.
* EXC: [IS_CAREGIVER_ENGAGEMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Distraction During Crisis] Engaging in caregiver coaching, reviewing data, or general discussion during an imminent safety crisis (elopement, severe SIB) represents severe clinical negligence and a failure to prioritize client safety.
* EXC: [IS_CHAINED] | [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching ADLs During Crisis] It is methodologically invalid and physically unsafe to conduct chained daily living activities (e.g., tying shoes, hygiene routines) while a client is in imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_CHAINING_FORMAT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_CONFIDENTIAL_DISCUSSION] | [Ethical Violation: Neglecting Active Crisis] Attempting to conduct confidential discussions about data or schedules while the client is exhibiting behaviors that pose imminent danger is negligent and unsafe.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Safety Hazard: Unmonitored Crisis] It is physically unsafe to manage a client in an imminent danger crisis (SIB, aggression) in a dark environment where clinicians cannot see to safely block or prevent injury.
* EXC: [IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching During Crisis] Formal differential reinforcement of alternative behaviors requires the client to be receptive to teaching; it cannot be methodologically implemented during a life-threatening, imminent danger crisis where safety/blocking is the sole priority.
* EXC: [IS_DISCRETE] | [Methodological Mismatch: DTT During Crisis] Discrete trial teaching (e.g., tracing, answering worksheets, matching) is methodologically impossible and fundamentally unsafe to conduct during an imminent danger crisis.
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | [Methodological Contradiction: Tutoring during Imminent Danger] A client in imminent danger (SIB, aggression, bolting) cannot simultaneously engage in or be instructed to complete educational worksheets or tracing tasks.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Escape Extinction during Imminent Danger] Utilizing escape extinction (forcing compliance/blocking escape) while a client is engaged in severe imminent danger (e.g., SIB, eloping into a street) is a gross ethical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Ethical Violation: Tactical Break during Imminent Danger] Placing a client in an exclusionary timeout or tactical break during severe imminent danger (SIB, pica, bolting to street) abandons them during a crisis and is highly unethical.
* EXC: [IS_EXECUTIVE_FUNCTIONING] | [Methodological Contradiction: Executive Functioning during Imminent Danger] A client cannot utilize higher-order executive functioning or self-monitoring while simultaneously undergoing a severe behavioral crisis involving imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction for Imminent Danger] Applying planned ignoring (extinction) to behaviors involving imminent danger (SIB, bolting, aggression) is a critical safety and ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_FINE_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Methodological Contradiction: Fine Motor during Imminent Danger] A client undergoing an episode of imminent danger cannot physically or clinically engage in fine motor skills instruction.
* EXC: [IS_FRAGMENTABLE_MATERIAL] | [Severe Medical Hazard: Pica and Fragmentable Materials] Providing fragmentable sensory materials like kinetic sand or playdough to a client in imminent danger due to active Pica (mouthing non-edibles, swallowing small objects) creates an immediate, severe choking and bowel obstruction hazard.
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Methodological & Safety Mismatch: Skill Acquisition During Crisis] It is physically dangerous and methodologically impossible to run gross motor acquisition targets while a client is in active imminent danger (aggression, SIB, elopement). Crisis management must supersede teaching.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Independence] Active states of imminent danger (severe aggression, SIB, elopement) strictly contradict the definition of independence, which mandates safe navigation and a complete absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_INTENSIVE_TEACHING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Intensive Teaching During Imminent Danger] Intensive teaching strategies like DTT or Errorless Teaching cannot be methodologically or safely conducted while a client is engaging in life-threatening imminent danger, such as severe aggression or elopement.
* EXC: [IS_NCR] | [Methodological and Ethical Violation: NCR During Imminent Danger] Delivering Noncontingent Reinforcement (NCR) while a client is actively engaged in life-threatening behavior risks adventitious reinforcement of the dangerous behavior, violating safe methodological practice.
* EXC: [IS_NET_TEACHING] | [Methodological Mismatch: NET During Imminent Danger] Natural Environment Teaching (NET) or Incidental Teaching requires an appropriate learning environment and cannot be safely or methodologically implemented while a client is in imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DEMAND] | [Crisis Mismanagement: Oral Demands during Imminent Danger] Presenting an oral demand like eating or brushing teeth while a client is in imminent danger (such as bolting or severe aggression) is a severe choking hazard and reflects a dangerous lack of crisis prioritization.
* EXC: [IS_OUTDOOR_REINFORCER] | [Methodological Violation: Reinforcing Imminent Danger] Providing access to the outdoors as a reinforcer while a client is actively engaging in life-threatening imminent danger behaviors (e.g., running into the street, elopement) is a severe methodological violation.
* EXC: [IS_PEER_TARGET] | [Ethical and Safety Violation: Peer Targets during Imminent Danger] Prompting peer interaction targets (e.g., conversational turn-taking, initiating play) while a client is actively displaying imminent danger (aggression, SIB) exposes peers to direct harm, violating ethical safety guidelines.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_PROMPT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Physical Prompting during Imminent Danger] Attempting to use physical prompts for skill acquisition (e.g., partial physical prompts) while a client is in an active state of imminent danger is a serious methodological mismatch; physical safety management must take precedent.
* EXC: [IS_PREMACK_CONTINGENCY] | [Crisis Mismanagement: Premack Contingency during Imminent Danger] Attempting to negotiate with a Premack Principle (First/Then) during a state of imminent danger (e.g., elopement, severe SIB) is methodologically contraindicated and violates basic crisis management protocols.
* EXC: [IS_PROXIMITY_PAIRING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Reinforcing Imminent Danger] Engaging in proximity pairing (tickles, pairing with toys, physical touch) while a client is in imminent danger (aggression, SIB, elopement) dangerously reinforces life-threatening behaviors.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Regulated State] A client actively engaging in imminent danger (aggression, elopement, SIB) cannot be described as calm, regulated, or displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_REMOTE] | [Physical Impossibility: Staff-Directed Aggression via Telehealth] Imminent danger behaviors specifically directed at staff (biting therapist, dodging staff) are physically impossible during a remote telehealth session where staff are not physically present.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DEMAND] | [Physical/Methodological Impossibility: Deep Breathing During Severe Crisis] A client cannot physically or methodologically engage in stationary respiratory demands (e.g., deep breathing, blowing bubbles) while simultaneously actively bolting, punching, or engaging in severe SIB.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Physiological Impossibility: Active Crisis vs. Lethargy] Imminent danger topographies (running, punching, bolting, thrashing) require a high level of kinetic energy and are physiologically incompatible with passively slumping in a chair and severe lethargy.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Crisis vs. Motivation] A client cannot simultaneously present imminent danger (e.g., elopement, severe aggression, self-injury) while being 'Highly Motivated', 'Happy', and demonstrating 'Quick Control'.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Crisis vs. Smooth Transition] Imminent danger behaviors such as bolting into parking lots, severe aggression, or self-injury completely disrupt and physically preclude a smooth, independent transition.
* EXC: [IS_SOCIAL_PLAY] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Crisis vs. Social Play] Imminent danger behaviors (such as punching, biting, running into the street) preclude the ability to safely engage in cooperative social play and turn-taking with peers.
* EXC: [IS_VISUAL_DEPENDENT_TASK] | [Physical Mismatch: Imminent Danger and Visual Tasks] A client engaging in imminent danger such as elopement, severe head banging, or bolting cannot safely or physically complete worksheets, tracing, or visually dependent fine-motor tasks.
* EXC: [IS_VOCAL_VERBAL_TARGET] | [Methodological Mismatch: Imminent Danger and Verbal Targets] Attempting to teach conversational turn-taking or complex tacting while a client is actively banging their head, bolting, or gouging eyes is methodologically invalid and ignores immediate life-safety priorities.
* EXC: [IS_VOCATIONAL] | [Methodological Mismatch: Imminent Danger and Vocational Tasks] It is a severe safety and methodological violation to prompt structured vocational tasks like sorting mail or wiping tables while a learner is in a state of imminent danger (e.g., severe aggression, bolting).
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Ethical Violation: Waiting Out Imminent Danger] Applying an extinction or 'wait out' procedure to behaviors that involve imminent danger (e.g., self-injurious behavior, elopement) violates BACB ethical codes mandating immediate intervention to protect the client from harm.

---

## [IS_INAPPROPRIATE_LANGUAGE]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Inappropriate Language vs Independence] Utilizing explicit, inappropriate, or derogatory language constitutes a maladaptive behavior, which directly contradicts the clinical requirement for the absence of maladaptive behaviors under the independence category.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DEMAND] | [Physical Impossibility: Inappropriate Language and Oral Demands] It is physically incompatible for a client to effectively articulate complex verbalizations like using profanity or making sexually explicit comments while simultaneously fulfilling an oral demand such as brushing teeth or chewing foods.
* EXC: [IS_PICA] | [Physical Impossibility: Inappropriate Language and Pica] It is physically impossible to clearly articulate complex, inappropriate verbalizations while actively engaging in Pica (e.g., swallowing small objects or mouthing non-edible items).
* EXC: [IS_PROXIMITY_PAIRING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Reinforcing Inappropriate Language] Providing proximity pairing (tickles, physical touch) while a client is using inappropriate language (profanity, verbal threats) is a methodological error that reinforces the maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Inappropriate Language vs Regulated State] Using profanity, derogatory names, or verbal threats fundamentally contradicts a regulated state characterized by calmness and the absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_VOCAL_VERBAL_TARGET] | [Ethical Violation: Teaching Inappropriate Language] Targeting the acquisition of peer greetings or conversational turn-taking using profanity, threats, or derogatory language is clinically inappropriate and an ethical violation.

---

## [IS_INDEPENDENT]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AGGRESSION] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Aggression vs Independence] A client cannot simultaneously engage in severe aggression (punching, biting others) while displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior and transitioning smoothly and independently.
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Methodological Contradiction: Extinction on Independence] Planned ignoring is an extinction procedure for maladaptive behavior, which methodologically contradicts a state of independence where the client is displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_SENSITIVITY] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Sensitivities vs Independence] Being triggered by loud noises implies distress and reactivity, which behaviorally contradicts transitioning smoothly and independently with an absence of maladaptive behaviors.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | [Methodological Contradiction: Punishing Independence] Applying aversive procedures (punishment, extinction) to a client who is behaving independently and displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior is methodologically and ethically prohibited.
* EXC: [IS_BIO_HAZARD] | [Contradiction: Maladaptive Behavior vs Independence] A client exhibiting severe bio-hazardous behaviors (pica, scatolia, disrobing) cannot simultaneously be demonstrating an absence of maladaptive behavior or transitioning smoothly and independently.
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | [Contradiction: Crisis vs Independent Functioning] A client cannot simultaneously trigger a crisis indicator (requiring physical blocks or incident reports) while demonstrating an absence of maladaptive behavior and independent functioning.
* EXC: [IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED] | [Contradiction: Required Distance vs Independence] Mandating a required safety distance (used for managing severe behaviors) contradicts documentation stating the client showed an absence of maladaptive behavior and independent functioning.
* EXC: [IS_DYSREGULATED] | [Definitional Contradiction: Dysregulated vs Independent] A client cannot be simultaneously dysregulated (e.g., crying, whining, visibly frustrated) and independent (demonstrating an absence of maladaptive behavior, transitioning smoothly and happily).
* EXC: [IS_ELOPEMENT] | [Definitional Contradiction: Elopement vs Independent] Elopement (bolting, escaping the area) directly contradicts the definition of being independent (exhibiting an absence of maladaptive behaviors, transitioning smoothly).
* EXC: [IS_ERROR_OR_STRUGGLE] | [Definitional Contradiction: Error vs Independent] Demonstrating an error or struggle (prompt dependence, failure to generalize) contradicts achieving independent mastery of a skill.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | [Definitional Contradiction: Escape Extinction vs Independent] Escape extinction requires the active presence of maladaptive escape behavior, which contradicts the client being fully independent and free of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_MAINTAINED] | [Definitional Contradiction: Escape Maintained vs Independent] Engaging in active escape-maintained maladaptive behavior directly contradicts demonstrating an independent, smooth transition.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Definitional Contradiction: Timeout vs Independent] Exclusionary timeout is a consequence for severe maladaptive behavior, which contradicts the state of being completely independent and free of maladaptive behaviors.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | [Definitional Contradiction: Extinction vs Independent] Extinction requires active maladaptive behavior to be ignored, whereas independence requires the complete absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Methodological Contradiction: Independence and SIB] Active hand compromise (a form of Self-Injurious Behavior) inherently contradicts the clinical parameters of independence, which require an absolute absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_AROUSAL] | [Methodological Contradiction: Independence and High Arousal] The definition of independence requires smooth fluency and an absence of maladaptive behavior, which is strictly incompatible with being highly agitated, crying, or severely distracted.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical & Methodological Violation: Punishing Independence] Applying high-effort punishment procedures like Positive Practice Overcorrection is ethically prohibited and methodologically contradictory when the client is displaying mastery and an absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Methodological Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Independence] Active states of imminent danger (severe aggression, SIB, elopement) strictly contradict the definition of independence, which mandates safe navigation and a complete absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_INAPPROPRIATE_LANGUAGE] | [Methodological Contradiction: Inappropriate Language vs Independence] Utilizing explicit, inappropriate, or derogatory language constitutes a maladaptive behavior, which directly contradicts the clinical requirement for the absence of maladaptive behaviors under the independence category.
* EXC: [IS_LESSER_PROMPT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Independent Skill and Lesser Prompts] A response or skill demonstration cannot be logically or methodologically classified as 'Independent' or 'Spontaneous' while simultaneously requiring lesser prompts such as verbal or partial physical assistance.
* EXC: [IS_NON_DANGEROUS_BEHAVIOR] | [Methodological Contradiction: Independence vs Maladaptive Behavior] A client cannot simultaneously be documented as 'displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior' while actively engaging in non-dangerous maladaptive behaviors such as dropping to the floor and crying or ignoring instructions.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_CONTACT_WITH_THERAPIST] | [Methodological Contradiction: Independence vs Aggressive Contact] A client cannot logically be displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior or transitioning happily while actively engaging in aggressive physical contact with a therapist, such as biting, spitting, or head-butting.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION] | [Methodological Contradiction: Independence vs Physical Interventions] Documenting a client as displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior and navigating smoothly directly contradicts the documented need for physical blocking or tactical breaks.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_PROMPT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Independence vs Physical Prompts] A behavior cannot methodologically be defined as independent, smooth, or mastered while simultaneously requiring physical prompts or guidance from a therapist.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Independence vs Safety Management] A client transitioning safely and independently cannot concurrently require physical safety management interventions, such as blocking with safety pads to prevent an exit.
* EXC: [IS_PICA] | [Methodological Contradiction: Independence vs Pica] A client actively engaging in Pica contradicts the explicit definition of independence in this ontology, which requires displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_PROJECTILE_BEHAVIOR] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Independence vs Projectile Behavior] A client displaying independent, mastered behavior with an absence of maladaptive behavior cannot simultaneously engage in projectile behavior (throwing materials).
* EXC: [IS_PROPERTY_DESTRUCTION] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Independence vs Property Destruction] A client demonstrating independent, smooth, and appropriate behavior cannot be actively engaging in property destruction.
* EXC: [IS_PROXIMITY_PROMPT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Independence vs Prompting] A skill or behavior cannot be documented as fully independent (spontaneous, unprompted) while simultaneously receiving proximity, model, or gestural prompts.
* EXC: [IS_RESISTANT_TRANSITION] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Independent vs Resistant Transition] A client cannot transition smoothly and independently while simultaneously exhibiting a resistant transition (requiring physical guidance, whining, protesting).
* EXC: [IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Punishing Independent Behavior] Applying restrictive punishment (tactical breaks, response cost) to a client who is behaving independently and appropriately without maladaptive behaviors is an ethical and methodological violation.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Definitional Contradiction: Independence vs. Severe Topography] The 'Independent' category explicitly defines the client as displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior, which inherently contradicts engaging in severe topography behaviors like banging their head or running into the street.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | [Definitional Contradiction: Independence vs. SIB] Self-Injurious Behavior is maladaptive, which definitionally contradicts the 'Independent' state that strictly requires displaying an absence of maladaptive behaviors.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Physiological Impossibility: Independence vs. Sleep Lethargy] Independence that involves fluency, transitioning smoothly, and happily running to designated areas is physically and methodologically precluded by lethargy, slowed responses, and passively slumping.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_DYSREGULATED] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Independence vs. Dysregulation] A client who is actively dysregulated (e.g., crying, visibly triggered by loud noises or routine changes) is not displaying the smooth, independent, and happily engaged state defined by the Independent category.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Definitional Contradiction: Independence vs. Aggression] Staff-directed aggression is a maladaptive behavior, fundamentally contradicting the 'Independent' state requiring an absence of maladaptive behaviors.
* EXC: [IS_TACTILE_DISTRESS] | [Definitional Contradiction: Independence vs. Tactile SIB] Tactile distress includes maladaptive topographies such as repeated skin scratching and skin picking, which definitionally contradicts the 'Independent' category's requirement for an absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_VOCAL_COMPROMISE] | [Contradictory Behavior States: Independence vs Vocal Compromise] A learner displaying an 'absence of maladaptive behavior' and transitioning 'smoothly and independently' cannot simultaneously be engaging in loud, sustained protests or high-pitch screaming.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Independence vs Wait Out Procedure] A wait-out procedure is implemented for noncompliance or behavioral refusal, which directly contradicts a behavioral state defined by an absence of maladaptive behavior and smooth, independent transitions.

---

## [IS_INDOOR_SETTING]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_OUTDOOR_MATERIAL] | [Physical Impossibility: Indoor Setting and Outdoor Material] Large-scale outdoor playground structures and gross motor obstacle courses are physically incompatible with typical indoor environments like a therapy room or bathroom.
* EXC: [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL] | [Physical Environment Contradiction: Indoor Setting and Traffic Hazard] An indoor environment like a bathroom, living room, therapy room, or classroom cannot physically host a parking lot navigation or street crossing scenario.
* EXC: [IS_VIBRATING_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical Environment Contradiction: Indoor Setting and Vehicle] An indoor structural setting (e.g., therapy room, bedroom, hallway) is physically contradictory to being contained within a moving vehicle.

---

## [IS_INFANT_TOY]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_TOXIC_HAZARD] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Toxins with Infant Toys] Combining toxic household cleaning supplies with infant items like cause-and-effect toys or bubbles presents a critical chemical ingestion and safety risk for early learners.
* EXC: [IS_VOCATIONAL] | [Ethical Violation: Age-Inappropriate Materials] Infant toys such as bubbles and cause-and-effect toys are developmentally inappropriate and violate the dignity of older learners engaged in vocational tasks like clocking in or completing assembly tasks.

---

## [IS_INTENSIVE_TEACHING]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AUTOMATIC_MAINTAINED] | [Methodological Contradiction: Unstructured Time vs. Intensive Demands] Unstructured time (allowing for automatic/free-operant behaviors) inherently lacks external demands, which directly contradicts the methodology of Intensive Teaching (DTT, Fluency), characterized by continuous, rapid-paced, highly structured demands.
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | [Methodological Mismatch: Intensive Teaching during Behavioral Crisis] Continuing highly structured, demand-heavy Intensive Teaching (such as max prompting or DTT) while a client is in an escalated behavioral crisis requiring physical blocks or tactical breaks is a methodological and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Intensive Teaching in Dark Environment] Intensive Teaching (DTT) relies on the rapid and clear presentation of visual and auditory discriminative stimuli, which is methodologically contradictory to the sensory-reduction purpose of a dark environment.
* EXC: [IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED] | [Physical Impossibility: Safety Distance during Intensive Teaching] Intensive teaching techniques, particularly errorless learning and max prompting, require close physical proximity to the client to provide physical guidance, which directly contradicts maintaining a behavioral safety distance.
* EXC: [IS_ELOPEMENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Elopement During Intensive Structured Teaching] Active elopement, such as bolting or leaving the instructional area, physically precludes the simultaneous administration of seated, highly structured discrete trial training.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Exclusionary Timeout and Intensive Teaching] An exclusionary timeout physically removes the individual from the teaching environment, making the simultaneous delivery of intensive teaching structurally impossible.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Methodological Mismatch: Intensive Teaching During Imminent Danger] Intensive teaching strategies like DTT or Errorless Teaching cannot be methodologically or safely conducted while a client is engaging in life-threatening imminent danger, such as severe aggression or elopement.
* EXC: [IS_LOW_AROUSAL] | [Methodological Contradiction: Arousal State vs. Response Fluency] Intensive teaching, particularly Fluency-Based Instruction, requires rapid, energetic, and highly accurate responding. Attempting to execute this instructional methodology with a client who is physically fatigued, lethargic, passively slumping, and exhibiting slowed response times is methodologically impossible and ignores the establishing operations for rest.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical Violation: Harm Prevention During Medical Emergency] Conducting high-demand intensive teaching trials (e.g., DTT, Fluency) while a client is experiencing an acute medical crisis (seizure, dehydration, severe asthma) is abusive, clinically dangerous, and violates core BACB ethical guidelines prioritizing client health and safety.
* EXC: [IS_NET_TEACHING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Opposing Teaching Paradigms] Intensive teaching (DTT, Fluency, Max Prompting) involves highly structured, contrived, and therapist-directed learning trials. NET (Natural Environment Teaching/Incidental Teaching) is intrinsically child-led, lightly structured, and occurs within natural contexts. These two instructional paradigms are methodologically contradictory and cannot be executed simultaneously for the same target.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Intensive Teaching during Respiratory Distress] Implementing highly demanding, intensive teaching methodologies (like DTT or fluency-based instruction) while a client is in respiratory distress is an ethical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_OVERLOAD] | [Ethical Violation: Teaching During Sensory Overload] It is an ethical violation to implement intensive structured teaching (e.g., DTT, Max Prompting) while a client is actively experiencing sensory overload.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Ethical Violation: Teaching During Severe Topography] Intensive teaching demands must be suspended when a client is actively engaging in severe, dangerous topographies such as bolting, eye gouging, or head banging.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | [Ethical Violation: Teaching During SIB] Intensive teaching and maximum prompting must be ethically paused during episodes of severe self-injurious behavior.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Ethical Violation: Teaching a Sleeping/Lethargic Client] It is ethically and methodologically impossible to conduct intensive structured teaching (DTT/Fluency) on a client who is sleeping, passively slumping, or extremely lethargic.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | [Methodological Contradiction: Prompted vs Spontaneous] Intensive teaching relies on maximum prompting and errorless teaching, which directly contradicts the definition of a spontaneous, unprompted skill demonstration.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Ethical Violation: Teaching During Aggression] Intensive teaching must be suspended if a client is actively engaging in staff-directed aggression.
* EXC: [IS_TOXIC_HAZARD] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Intensive Teaching with Toxins] Utilizing toxic household cleaning supplies as instructional materials during rapid-paced, highly repetitive Intensive Teaching (e.g., DTT) poses an unacceptable risk of chemical exposure or ingestion.
* EXC: [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL] | [Methodological Mismatch: DTT in Traffic Environments] Intensive teaching formats such as Discrete Trial Training (DTT) and Fluency Based Instruction require highly controlled instructional settings and cannot be safely or effectively conducted while navigating a parking lot or crossing a street.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Intensive Demands vs Wait Out] Intensive teaching strategies like DTT necessitate a high rate of instructional demands, directly contradicting a wait-out procedure, which strictly requires the temporary cessation of all therapist demands and interaction.

---

## [IS_INTERNAL_STATE_TARGET]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical Violation: Exploiting Medical Crises for Skill Acquisition] Targeting internal states (e.g., 'Tolerating task demands', 'Accepting delayed reinforcement') during a life-threatening or severe medical crisis is clinically abusive and medically negligent. An active seizure or asthma attack is not a safe or appropriate antecedent for teaching coping or tolerance skills.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Methodological Mismatch: Coping vs Severe Topography] A client actively engaging in severe problem behaviors (e.g., bolting, eye gouging) is fundamentally failing to cope or tolerate, making the successful demonstration of internal state targets impossible.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | [Methodological Mismatch: Coping vs SIB] A client actively engaging in severe self-injurious behavior is failing to successfully cope, accept 'no', or tolerate demands.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Physical Impossibility: Active Coping While Asleep] A client who is sleeping or highly lethargic cannot actively demonstrate internal state targets such as accepting changes, using deep breathing, or tolerating loud environments.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Coping vs Aggression] A client actively assaulting staff is fundamentally failing to demonstrate coping skills or the ability to accept 'no'.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Active Coping Coaching vs Wait Out] Actively teaching and prompting internal state targets, such as using deep breathing or asking for a break, is fundamentally incompatible with a wait-out procedure that demands zero interaction or prompting.

---

## [IS_LESSER_INTERVENTION]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Clinical Mismatch: Inappropriate Behavioral Intervention for Medical Crises] Applying lesser behavioral interventions (e.g., visual schedules, stimulus fading, behavioral redirection) during an acute medical emergency (seizure, severe asthma) is a gross clinical mismatch. Medical emergencies demand immediate first aid and healthcare protocols, not behavioral accommodation.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Methodological Mismatch: Lesser Intervention for Severe Crisis] Severe topographies like bolting or eye gouging require immediate physical crisis management, making lesser interventions like visual schedules or redirection methodologically invalid as a primary response.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | [Methodological Mismatch: Lesser Intervention for SIB] Severe self-injurious behavior requires immediate physical blocking, not lesser interventions like stimulus fading or visual schedules.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Lesser Intervention for Aggression] Active aggression against staff requires immediate physical crisis management, making lesser interventions inappropriate and mismatched.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Redirection vs Wait Out] Lesser interventions such as redirection or presenting a visual schedule require active therapist engagement, which violates the parameters of a wait-out procedure.

---

## [IS_LESSER_PROMPT]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Independent Skill and Lesser Prompts] A response or skill demonstration cannot be logically or methodologically classified as 'Independent' or 'Spontaneous' while simultaneously requiring lesser prompts such as verbal or partial physical assistance.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical Violation: Unsafe Prompting During Medical Events] Delivering physical, verbal, or gestural prompts to compel compliance or task completion while a client is suffering from an active medical crisis (such as a seizure or breathing distress) places the client at risk of physical harm and is medically contraindicated.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting During Severe Behavior] Severe topographies require physical safety blocks, not lesser prompts like verbal or visual prompting.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting During SIB] Severe self-injury requires physical safety blocks, not lesser prompts like verbal or visual prompting.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | [Methodological Contradiction: Prompted and Spontaneous] A single behavior or response cannot be simultaneously prompted (via lesser prompts) and spontaneously demonstrated.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting During Aggression] Active staff-directed aggression requires physical safety blocks, not lesser prompts.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting During Wait Out] The delivery of lesser prompts (verbal, gestural, or visual) constitutes an instructional demand, fundamentally contradicting the withholding of interaction required during a wait-out procedure.

---

## [IS_LOWER_BODY_DISROBING]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_PROXIMITY_PAIRING] | [Ethical Violation: Boundaries and Physical Touch] Engaging in physical touch, tickling, or proximity pairing while a client is engaged in lower-body disrobing constitutes a severe ethical boundary violation and a risk of abuse allegations.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Methodological Contradiction: Disrobing vs Regulated State] Lower body disrobing is an acute maladaptive behavior, which is physically and behaviorally incompatible with displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior or being fully regulated.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Safety Hazard: Sharp Objects While Disrobed] It is a severe physical safety risk and ethical violation to provide sharp objects (e.g., scissors) to a client who is completely disrobed or in the process of stripping.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Disrobing During Smooth Transition] Stripping naked or removing pants is indicative of either an accident, problem behavior, or a toileting routine, which contradicts the definition of happily and smoothly transitioning between general areas.
* EXC: [IS_SOCIAL_PLAY] | [Ethical Violation: Disrobing During Peer Play] It is an ethical and social violation to allow a client to strip completely naked while engaged in cooperative social play with peers.
* EXC: [IS_TOXIC_HAZARD] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Chemical Exposure on Bare Skin] Handling toxic household cleaning supplies while stripping completely naked or removing lower body clothing creates a severe risk of chemical burns or hazardous exposure to bare skin.
* EXC: [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Public Disrobing in Traffic] Engaging in lower body disrobing or stripping completely naked while actively navigating a parking lot or street crossing presents an extreme physical safety risk and profound legal/public exposure concerns.
* EXC: [IS_VOCATIONAL] | [Ethical Violation: Dignity in Vocational Settings] Removing pants or stripping completely naked during vocational training (e.g., clocking in, sorting mail) severely violates community workplace norms and basic client dignity.

---

## [IS_LOW_AROUSAL]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AGGRESSION] | [Physical Impossibility: High-Energy Aggression vs. Lethargy] Directed, high-energy aggressive acts (punching, kicking, head-butting) are physically contradictory to a state of extreme low arousal, characterized by passive slumping, sluggishness, and severe lethargy.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_AROUSAL] | [Physical Impossibility: Simultaneous High and Low Arousal] A client cannot physically be in a state of high arousal (e.g., rapid pacing, hyperactivity) while simultaneously exhibiting low arousal states (e.g., lethargic, passively slumping, sluggish).
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical Violation: High Effort Punishment for Fatigued Client] Implementing high-effort punishment procedures, such as positive practice or overcorrection, on a client who is physically fatigued, sluggish, or lethargic is an ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EXERTION] | [Physical Impossibility: High Exertion and Low Arousal] It is physically contradictory for a client to be engaged in high exertion activities (such as rapid pacing or jumping) while presenting with low arousal (e.g., lethargic, passively slumping).
* EXC: [IS_INTENSIVE_TEACHING] | [Methodological Contradiction: Arousal State vs. Response Fluency] Intensive teaching, particularly Fluency-Based Instruction, requires rapid, energetic, and highly accurate responding. Attempting to execute this instructional methodology with a client who is physically fatigued, lethargic, passively slumping, and exhibiting slowed response times is methodologically impossible and ignores the establishing operations for rest.
* EXC: [IS_NO_MEDICAL] | [Logical Contradiction: Arousal/Fatigue vs No Medical] IS_LOW_AROUSAL includes symptoms like 'Lethargy / Fatigue' and 'Poor sleep' which represent physiological/medical distress states. This directly contradicts the absolute declaration of IS_NO_MEDICAL ('None reported').
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Physiological Incompatibility: Low Arousal vs Energetic Regulation] A state of low arousal (lethargy, passive slumping, fatigue) directly contradicts the physiological definition of being regulated (smiling, energetic, highly motivated).
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Physical Impossibility: Low Arousal vs High-Energy Topography] A client cannot be physically fatigued and passively slumping while simultaneously engaging in high-energy severe topographies like bolting or kicking holes in walls.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Safety Hazard: Sharp Objects and Low Arousal] It is physically and ethically unsafe to provide sharp objects to a client who is lethargic, passively slumping, or severely fatigued.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Clinical Contradiction: Regulated vs Low Arousal] A client who is withdrawn, fatigued, and passively slumping contradicts the operational definition of being highly motivated, happy, and regulated.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Physical Impossibility: Smooth Transition While Slumping] A client who is passively slumping, sluggish, and physically fatigued cannot simultaneously be happily running or transitioning smoothly without resistance.
* EXC: [IS_SOCIAL_PLAY] | [Physical Impossibility: Social Play While Withdrawn] A client who is passively slumping, lethargic, and withdrawn cannot actively initiate and sustain cooperative peer play.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | [Physical Impossibility: Spontaneous Action While Lethargic] A lethargic, slumping, and physically fatigued client cannot simultaneously make spontaneous and unprompted skill demonstrations.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Physical Impossibility: Low Arousal vs Active Aggression] A client cannot be physically fatigued and passively slumping while simultaneously engaging in high-energy active aggression toward staff.
* EXC: [IS_TOXIC_HAZARD] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Handling Toxins While Lethargic] Lethargy and sluggishness severely impair cognitive and motor functioning, making it profoundly unsafe for a client to handle toxic household cleaning supplies.
* EXC: [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Low Motor Readiness in Traffic] A client exhibiting low arousal (e.g., sluggish, highly fatigued, lethargic) lacks the necessary motor readiness, coordination, and reaction time to safely practice traffic hazard skills like navigating a parking lot.
* EXC: [IS_VISUAL_DEPENDENT_TASK] | [Physical Impossibility: Visual Attention During Low Arousal] A client who is passively slumping, lethargic, or sluggish physically lacks the physiological readiness required to actively sustain visual attention for complex tracing or worksheet tasks.
* EXC: [IS_VOCAL_COMPROMISE] | [Physical Impossibility: Arousal State Contradiction] A client physically cannot be in a state of extreme low arousal (passively slumping, lethargic) while simultaneously exhibiting high-arousal vocal compromise (screaming at a high pitch, loud sustained protests).

---

## [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AAC_TARGET] | [Physical Impossibility: AAC Target During Medical Emergency] It is physically and medically impossible to meaningfully prompt or expect a client to utilize an AAC device to communicate while they are actively experiencing an incapacitating medical crisis, such as a tonic-clonic seizure or severe asthma attack.
* EXC: [IS_ACADEMIC] | [Ethical/Physical Violation: Academics During Medical Emergency] Continuing or presenting academic demands (e.g., counting, identifying shapes) while a client is experiencing a life-threatening medical crisis is both physically impossible and constitutes severe medical neglect and abuse.
* EXC: [IS_ADL_TARGET] | [Ethical/Physical Violation: ADLs During Medical Emergency] Targeting daily living skills such as shoe tying, packing a backpack, or washing hands cannot physically or ethically occur while a client is in the throes of a medical emergency like a seizure or respiratory crisis.
* EXC: [IS_AGGRESSION] | [Physical Impossibility: Aggression During Incapacitating Crisis] A client actively experiencing an incapacitating medical crisis (e.g., a seizure) cannot simultaneously engage in intentional, directed high-energy aggressive acts like punching or hair-pulling.
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction During Medical Crisis] Utilizing planned ignoring while a client is experiencing an acute medical emergency (seizure, severe dehydration) constitutes severe medical neglect and violates all duty-of-care guidelines.
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_MAINTAINED] | [Methodological Mismatch: Medical Crisis vs. Attention Function] A medical crisis (e.g., seizure, acute asthma) is an involuntary physiological event, not an operant behavior maintained by social attention.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_DEMAND] | [Ethical Violation: Sensory Demands During Medical Emergency] Subjecting a client to loud noises or demanding tolerance of a loud environment during an active medical emergency is physically unsafe and abusive.
* EXC: [IS_AUTOMATIC_MAINTAINED] | [Ethical Mismatch: Unstructured Time During Emergency Protocol] A medical crisis requires immediate, highly structured, emergency intervention and protocol adherence, making it fundamentally incompatible with coding 'unstructured free time.'
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | [Ethical Violation: Aversives During Medical Crisis] Applying aversive behavioral procedures (such as overcorrection, response cost, or escape extinction) while a client is suffering a physiological emergency is highly abusive and medically dangerous.
* EXC: [IS_BCBA_LEVEL_TASK] | [Ethical/Methodological Violation: Administrative Tasks During Crisis] Routine administrative or pedagogical BCBA tasks (e.g., reviewing IEP goals, discussing theoretical generalization strategies) must be suspended during an active medical crisis, which demands immediate, emergency health and safety interventions.
* EXC: [IS_BIO_HAZARD] | [Physical Impossibility: Voluntary Biohazard during Incapacitating Medical Crisis] Active, voluntary biohazard behaviors, such as intentionally stripping completely naked, pica, or smearing feces, are physically impossible for a client to execute while simultaneously incapacitated by a severe medical event like a seizure.
* EXC: [IS_CAREGIVER_ENGAGEMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Caregiver Engagement during Medical Crisis] Engaging caregivers in routine program reviews, discussing mastered skills, or analyzing data graphs while the client is experiencing an active, life-threatening medical emergency is an extreme safety and ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_CHAINED] | [Safety Violation: Chained ADLs during Medical Crisis] Prompting a client to perform multi-step activities of daily living (such as brushing teeth, dressing, or packing a bag) during an active seizure or respiratory crisis is physically impossible and highly unsafe.
* EXC: [IS_CHAINING_FORMAT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Chaining Formats during Medical Crisis] It is methodologically, physically, and ethically impossible to conduct shaping or chaining instructional formats during a life-threatening medical crisis such as a seizure or severe asthma attack.
* EXC: [IS_CHOKING_HAZARD] | [Safety Violation: Choking Hazard during Medical Crisis] Introducing or failing to remove choking hazards near a client who is actively experiencing an incapacitating medical event, such as a seizure or respiratory distress, is a critical and life-threatening safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_CONFIDENTIAL_DISCUSSION] | [Ethical Violation: Confidential Discussion during Medical Crisis] Engaging in confidential administrative discussions, such as reviewing data logs or IEP goals, while the client is suffering from an active, life-threatening medical emergency is an extreme ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | [Ethical Violation: Behavioral Restraint during Medical Crisis] Applying physical behavioral restraints (such as safety pads or physical blocking) during an involuntary neurological event like a seizure is contraindicated and represents an extreme medical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Differential Reinforcement during Medical Crisis] Attempting to apply behavioral reinforcement schedules while a client is actively experiencing a severe medical emergency (like a seizure or respiratory failure) is physically and ethically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_DISCRETE] | [Safety Violation: Discrete Trials during Medical Crisis] Running academic or discrete trial demands (such as answering WH-questions or matching items) during an active medical emergency is physically impossible and a severe safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED] | [Safety Violation: Safety Distance during Medical Crisis] Maintaining a behavioral 'safety distance' during a medical emergency prevents the caregiver from rendering critical, life-saving medical aid, constituting a severe ethical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_EDIBLE] | [Ethical Violation: Edibles During Medical Emergency] Administering edible items during a medical crisis such as an active asthma attack or seizure poses a severe choking risk and is physically hazardous.
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | [Ethical Violation: Academic Demands During Medical Crisis] Conducting structured educational tutoring or academic tasks during an incapacitating medical event is an ethical violation and medically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_ELECTRONIC_DEVICE] | [Physical Impossibility: Electronic Device Usage During Medical Crisis] Operating electronic devices requires motor and cognitive control that is physically precluded by an active medical crisis such as a seizure.
* EXC: [IS_ELOPEMENT] | [Physical Impossibility: Operant Elopement During Medical Incapacitation] An individual experiencing a severe, incapacitating medical crisis like a seizure cannot simultaneously engage in active, goal-directed operant elopement.
* EXC: [IS_ERROR_OR_STRUGGLE] | [Methodological Mismatch: Skill Assessment During Medical Emergency] It is medically invalid and ethically inappropriate to record skill acquisition errors or struggles during an active medical crisis.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Escape Extinction During Medical Crisis] Utilizing escape extinction to force compliance and block escape during a life-threatening medical crisis is dangerous and violates BACB ethical guidelines regarding physiological needs.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_MAINTAINED] | [Methodological Mismatch: Operant Function for Involuntary Medical Event] A physiological medical crisis such as a seizure is an involuntary, biological event and cannot be categorized as an operant behavior maintained by escape.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_REINFORCER] | [Methodological Mismatch: Escape Reinforcement for Medical Emergency] Escape functions and operant reinforcement contingencies cannot be applied to involuntary, non-operant medical emergencies.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Ethical Violation: Punitive Timeout During Medical Crisis] Placing an individual in an exclusionary timeout during an active medical emergency is severe medical neglect and highly unethical.
* EXC: [IS_EXECUTIVE_FUNCTIONING] | [Ethical Violation: Cognitive Demands During Medical Crisis] Placing advanced executive functioning demands, such as problem-solving or self-monitoring, on an individual during an active medical emergency is clinically invalid and unethical.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | [Ethical Violation: Ignoring Distress During Medical Emergency] Applying attention extinction by ignoring vocal protests or distress during a medical emergency is inherently dangerous and constitutes medical neglect.
* EXC: [IS_FALL_HAZARD] | [Physical Hazard: Gross Motor Play During Medical Crisis] Engaging in gross motor fall hazards (e.g., swinging, jumping) during a severe medical crisis like a seizure poses an imminent risk of physical injury and is medically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_FINE_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Ethical Violation: Fine Motor Demands During Medical Crisis] Requiring purposeful fine motor skill execution (e.g., stringing beads, tying shoes) during an active medical emergency is physically impossible and ethically prohibited.
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Medical Safety Mismatch: Gross Motor Targets During Medical Crisis] It is medically unsafe and ethically prohibited to demand gross motor targets requiring physical movement when a client is experiencing a medical crisis such as an asthma attack, dehydration, or seizure.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical and Medical Violation: Punishment During Medical Crisis] Administering high-effort punishment (like overcorrection) to a client actively experiencing a medical crisis (such as a seizure or asthma attack) is medically unsafe and a severe ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EXERTION] | [Medical Safety Mismatch: High Exertion During Medical Crisis] A client sustaining or being directed to sustain high physical exertion during a medical emergency (such as an asthma attack, dehydration, or seizure) represents a severe medical danger.
* EXC: [IS_INTENSIVE_TEACHING] | [Ethical Violation: Harm Prevention During Medical Emergency] Conducting high-demand intensive teaching trials (e.g., DTT, Fluency) while a client is experiencing an acute medical crisis (seizure, dehydration, severe asthma) is abusive, clinically dangerous, and violates core BACB ethical guidelines prioritizing client health and safety.
* EXC: [IS_INTERNAL_STATE_TARGET] | [Ethical Violation: Exploiting Medical Crises for Skill Acquisition] Targeting internal states (e.g., 'Tolerating task demands', 'Accepting delayed reinforcement') during a life-threatening or severe medical crisis is clinically abusive and medically negligent. An active seizure or asthma attack is not a safe or appropriate antecedent for teaching coping or tolerance skills.
* EXC: [IS_LESSER_INTERVENTION] | [Clinical Mismatch: Inappropriate Behavioral Intervention for Medical Crises] Applying lesser behavioral interventions (e.g., visual schedules, stimulus fading, behavioral redirection) during an acute medical emergency (seizure, severe asthma) is a gross clinical mismatch. Medical emergencies demand immediate first aid and healthcare protocols, not behavioral accommodation.
* EXC: [IS_LESSER_PROMPT] | [Ethical Violation: Unsafe Prompting During Medical Events] Delivering physical, verbal, or gestural prompts to compel compliance or task completion while a client is suffering from an active medical crisis (such as a seizure or breathing distress) places the client at risk of physical harm and is medically contraindicated.
* EXC: [IS_NCR] | [Methodological/Ethical Mismatch: NCR During Emergency Health Events] Delivering noncontingent reinforcement (scheduled delivery of toys, edibles, or attention) to a client actively suffering a medical crisis is methodologically nonsensical and medically negligent. The primary and sole response must be medical intervention, not reinforcer delivery.
* EXC: [IS_NET_TEACHING] | [Ethical Violation: Naturalistic Demands During Health Crises] Attempting to utilize the environment for Incidental Teaching or NET while a client is physically experiencing a medical crisis (e.g., prompting a tact of 'water' during dehydration/asthma or while actively seizing) constitutes severe medical neglect and client abuse.
* EXC: [IS_NO_MEDICAL] | [Logical Contradiction: Medical Crisis vs No Medical] IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS explicitly documents severe medical emergencies (e.g., Asthma, Seizures). It is logically and methodologically impossible to document a medical crisis while simultaneously declaring IS_NO_MEDICAL ('None reported').
* EXC: [IS_PROXIMITY_PAIRING] | [Ethical/Safety Violation: Crisis vs Proximity Pairing] Engaging in proximity pairing (such as tickling, applying deep pressure, or playing with toys) during an acute medical crisis ignores emergency protocols and constitutes a severe safety risk.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Physiological Incompatibility: Crisis vs Regulated] A client actively experiencing a medical crisis (e.g., active seizures, asthma) cannot simultaneously be physiologically regulated (happy, energetic, absence of medical/behavioral issues).
* EXC: [IS_RESISTANT_TRANSITION] | [Methodological/Safety Mismatch: Crisis vs Transition Demands] A medical crisis requires immediate stabilization; attempting to enforce or measure structured transition demands (leading to resistant transitions) during a crisis is medically contraindicated.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DEMAND] | [Physiological Impossibility/Safety: Crisis vs Respiratory Demand] Enforcing respiratory demands (like blowing bubbles or structured deep breathing) during a medical crisis (like an active seizure or severe dehydration) is physically inappropriate and unsafe.
* EXC: [IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Medical Crisis] Applying restrictive punishment (e.g., overcorrection, tactical breaks, response cost) during a medical crisis (seizures, asthma attack, dehydration) is a severe ethical and health safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Medical vs Operant Mismatch: Crisis and Severe Topography] A medical crisis (e.g., seizure, asthma attack) is an involuntary physiological event, which cannot be categorized alongside operant problem behaviors like bolting or smearing feces.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Safety Hazard: Sharp Objects During Medical Crisis] It is ethically and physically unsafe to have sharp objects present or actively used during an involuntary medical emergency such as a seizure.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | [Medical vs Operant Mismatch: Crisis and SIB] A medical crisis such as a seizure involves involuntary movements that cannot be categorized as operant self-injurious behavior.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Clinical Contradiction: Regulated During Medical Crisis] A client experiencing a medical emergency (e.g., seizure, severe dehydration) is in physiological distress and cannot be categorized as highly motivated, happy, or regulated.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Physical Impossibility: Smooth Transition During Medical Crisis] An active medical crisis (seizure, asthma attack) prevents any form of smooth or happy physical transition to a new area.
* EXC: [IS_SOCIAL_PLAY] | [Physical Impossibility: Social Play During Medical Crisis] A client experiencing an active medical emergency cannot engage in cooperative social peer play.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | [Physical Impossibility: Spontaneous Demonstration During Crisis] An active medical emergency completely precludes the ability to spontaneously and voluntarily demonstrate clinical skills.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Medical vs Operant Mismatch: Crisis and Aggression] A client experiencing an involuntary medical emergency (e.g., seizure) cannot simultaneously engage in operant, staff-directed aggression.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | [Medical Exclusivity: Emergency vs ADL Demands] A medical crisis such as a seizure or severe asthma attack requires immediate life-saving intervention, completely precluding the ability to complete a toileting routine.
* EXC: [IS_TOKEN_ECONOMY] | [Ethical Violation: Operant Conditioning During Medical Crisis] Attempting to run a token economy during a physiological emergency like a seizure or severe asthma attack is methodologically invalid and constitutes gross medical neglect.
* EXC: [IS_TOKEN_MATERIAL] | [Ethical Violation: Materials During Medical Crisis] Managing token materials during a medical crisis like a seizure is clinically inappropriate as all focus must be on emergency medical response.
* EXC: [IS_TOXIC_HAZARD] | [Physical Impossibility: Handling Toxins During Medical Emergency] A client cannot safely or functionally handle toxic household cleaning supplies while experiencing an active medical emergency such as a seizure.
* EXC: [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Medical Emergency in Traffic] Experiencing a medical crisis such as a seizure while practicing safety crossing or navigating a parking lot represents an extreme physical safety and life-threatening hazard.
* EXC: [IS_TRANSITION] | [Physical Impossibility: Transition During Medical Emergency] Transitioning back to a standard class routine or transitioning to a teacher is physically and medically impossible during an acute crisis like a seizure; emergency stabilization is required.
* EXC: [IS_VISUAL_DEPENDENT_TASK] | [Physical Impossibility: Visual Tasks During Medical Emergency] A client cannot physiologically sustain visual attention or complete worksheets while experiencing a medical crisis such as a seizure, dehydration, or respiratory distress.
* EXC: [IS_VOCAL_COMPROMISE] | [Physical Impossibility: Volitional Vocal Protests During Seizure] A client actively experiencing a tonic-clonic seizure physically cannot engage in volitional vocal compromise behaviors such as repetitive vocal scripting or sustained loud protests.
* EXC: [IS_VOCAL_VERBAL_TARGET] | [Physical Impossibility: Vocal Production During Respiratory/Neurological Crisis] A client actively experiencing a tonic-clonic seizure or an acute asthma attack is physically incapable of producing controlled vocal-verbal targets such as answering WH questions or tacting objects.
* EXC: [IS_VOCATIONAL] | [Ethical Violation: Vocational Demands During Medical Crisis] Imposing vocational expectations (e.g., clocking in, assembly tasks) during a severe medical crisis such as a seizure or asthma attack is highly unethical and medically neglectful.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Ethical Violation: Withholding Medical Care] Utilizing a wait-out behavioral procedure during a medical crisis (e.g., seizure, acute asthma) constitutes life-threatening medical neglect.

---

## [IS_MEDICAL_DISTRESS]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction of Pain/Distress Communication] Implementing planned ignoring when a client is communicating or exhibiting signs of active medical distress (e.g., ear infection, dental pain, illness) is an ethical violation. Medical and physiological needs must be addressed rather than placed on extinction.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_DEMAND] | [Ethical Violation: Sensory Demands During Medical Distress] Requiring a client to tolerate loud environments when they are actively suffering from a headache, ear infection, or other acute pain is physically agonizing and an ethical violation of client dignity and care.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | [Ethical Violation: Aversives During Pain and Distress] Implementing aversives when a client is in active medical distress (dental pain, ear infection, illness) directly violates the BACB ethical mandate to rule out and address medical/biological variables before applying behavioral reduction procedures.
* EXC: [IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Differential Reinforcement of Medical Distress] Applying behavioral differential reinforcement schedules to suppress or treat behaviors that are direct manifestations of active medical distress (e.g., dental pain, ear infections) is an ethical violation. Medical needs must be treated medically, not behaviorally.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Forcing Compliance During Medical Illness] Applying escape extinction to demand compliance when a client is experiencing active medical distress (e.g., severe pain, illness) is highly unethical and contradicts clinical safety guidelines.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Ethical Violation: Punitive Timeout for Medical Distress] Utilizing a disciplinary exclusionary timeout when a client is experiencing active medical distress or physical illness violates ethical guidelines for compassionate and safe care.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | [Ethical Violation: Attention Extinction for Medical Pain] It is an ethical violation to utilize attention extinction procedures on an individual explicitly expressing distress derived from active physical pain, illness, or medical discomfort.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Punishment During Medical Distress] Implementing high-effort punishment procedures on a client who is experiencing obvious medical distress, pain, or illness symptoms violates ethical mandates for client dignity and safety.
* EXC: [IS_NO_MEDICAL] | [Logical Contradiction: Medical Distress vs No Medical] IS_MEDICAL_DISTRESS documents active physiological and medical symptoms (e.g., Allergies, Dental pain, Rash). This is fundamentally incompatible with the IS_NO_MEDICAL category ('None reported').
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Physiological Incompatibility: Distress vs Regulated] Active medical distress (illness, dental pain, ear infection) is physiologically incompatible with a fully regulated state (absence of issues, calm, happy, and energetic).
* EXC: [IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Medical Distress] It is a violation of behavioral ethics to apply restrictive punishment procedures to a client actively experiencing medical distress, illness, or pain.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Ethical Violation: Ignoring Medical Distress] Waiting out symptoms of genuine medical distress (e.g., extreme pain, ear infections, seizure activity) as if they were strictly operant behaviors is medically neglectful and violates ethical guidelines.

---

## [IS_MEDICAL_VARIABLE]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_NO_MEDICAL] | [Logical Contradiction: Medical Variable vs No Medical] IS_MEDICAL_VARIABLE identifies underlying physiological or pharmacological conditions (e.g., Medication change, Missed meal). Documenting these physiological variables directly invalidates the absolute claim of IS_NO_MEDICAL ('None reported').

---

## [IS_MESSY_MATERIAL]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Ethical and Medical Violation: Infection Risk with Messy Materials] Presenting messy materials like water play or kinetic sand to a client whose hands are compromised with open wounds (e.g., from severe scratching or biting) introduces a high risk of infection, representing a medical contraindication.
* EXC: [IS_TOXIC_HAZARD] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Toxins in Messy Play] Combining messy play elements like kinetic sand or a water play table with toxic household cleaning supplies introduces a severe risk of inadvertent chemical mixing and ingestion.
* EXC: [IS_VOCATIONAL] | [Dignity Violation: Messy Play in Vocational Training] Utilizing sensory messy materials such as a water play table or kinetic sand during structured adult vocational tasks (e.g., clocking in, sorting mail) violates age-appropriateness and professional workplace norms.

---

## [IS_NCR]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Methodological and Ethical Violation: NCR During Imminent Danger] Delivering Noncontingent Reinforcement (NCR) while a client is actively engaged in life-threatening behavior risks adventitious reinforcement of the dangerous behavior, violating safe methodological practice.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Methodological/Ethical Mismatch: NCR During Emergency Health Events] Delivering noncontingent reinforcement (scheduled delivery of toys, edibles, or attention) to a client actively suffering a medical crisis is methodologically nonsensical and medically negligent. The primary and sole response must be medical intervention, not reinforcer delivery.

---

## [IS_NET_TEACHING]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | [Methodological Mismatch: Natural Environment Teaching during Behavioral Crisis] Natural Environment Teaching requires a calm, engaged, and child-led state. Attempting NET while a client is in an active behavioral crisis that necessitates physical restraints or tactical breaks is methodologically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Structured Academics in Naturalistic Teaching] Formal educational tutoring involving worksheets and tracing letters fundamentally contradicts the child-led, embedded nature of Natural Environment Teaching (NET).
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Timeout During Naturalistic Teaching] Exclusionary timeout isolates the individual from ongoing activities, structurally contradicting the inclusive, environment-embedded methodology of Natural Environment Teaching.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Methodological Mismatch: NET During Imminent Danger] Natural Environment Teaching (NET) or Incidental Teaching requires an appropriate learning environment and cannot be safely or methodologically implemented while a client is in imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_INTENSIVE_TEACHING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Opposing Teaching Paradigms] Intensive teaching (DTT, Fluency, Max Prompting) involves highly structured, contrived, and therapist-directed learning trials. NET (Natural Environment Teaching/Incidental Teaching) is intrinsically child-led, lightly structured, and occurs within natural contexts. These two instructional paradigms are methodologically contradictory and cannot be executed simultaneously for the same target.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical Violation: Naturalistic Demands During Health Crises] Attempting to utilize the environment for Incidental Teaching or NET while a client is physically experiencing a medical crisis (e.g., prompting a tact of 'water' during dehydration/asthma or while actively seizing) constitutes severe medical neglect and client abuse.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: NET during Respiratory Distress] Continuing instructional demands via Natural Environment Teaching (NET) while a client is experiencing acute respiratory distress compromises client safety and violates ethical care standards.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_OVERLOAD] | [Ethical Violation: NET During Sensory Overload] Natural Environment Teaching demands should not be placed on a client actively suffering from sensory overload.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Ethical Violation: NET During Severe Topography] Natural Environment Teaching must be paused when a client is engaging in severe problem behaviors like bolting or eye gouging.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | [Ethical Violation: NET During SIB] Natural Environment Teaching must be suspended when a client is engaging in severe self-injurious behavior.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Ethical Violation: NET on Sleeping Client] It is practically and ethically impossible to conduct Natural Environment Teaching on a client who is actively sleeping or heavily lethargic.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Ethical Violation: NET During Aggression] Natural Environment Teaching must be completely suspended during episodes of active aggression directed at staff.
* EXC: [IS_VOCAL_COMPROMISE] | [Methodological Mismatch: NET During Vocal Compromise] Natural Environment Teaching (NET) relies on the learner's intrinsic motivation and functional play; if the learner is screaming at a high pitch or engaged in loud sustained protests, the conditions for NET are methodologically destroyed.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: NET vs Wait Out] Natural Environment Teaching (NET) requires active engagement to capture learner motivation, rendering it methodologically incompatible with a wait-out procedure that demands the removal of all interaction.

---

## [IS_NON_CLINIC]

---

## [IS_NON_DANGEROUS_BEHAVIOR]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Ethical Violation: Restrictive Procedures for Mild Behavior] Utilizing an exclusionary timeout (a highly restrictive, punitive procedure) for non-dangerous behaviors (stereotypy, passive slumping) violates the ethical mandate for least restrictive behavioral interventions.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Independence vs Maladaptive Behavior] A client cannot simultaneously be documented as 'displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior' while actively engaging in non-dangerous maladaptive behaviors such as dropping to the floor and crying or ignoring instructions.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION] | [Ethical Violation: Least Restrictive Alternative] Implementing restrictive physical interventions (e.g., blocking, tactical breaks) to manage non-dangerous behaviors (e.g., stereotypy, vocal protests, non-compliance) violates BACB ethical guidelines requiring the use of least restrictive procedures.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Least Restrictive Alternative] Using physical safety management techniques (e.g., physical blocking to prevent exit, safety pads) for purely non-dangerous behaviors is an ethical violation of the mandate for least restrictive behavior management.

---

## [IS_NO_MEDICAL]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_DISTRESS] | [Medical Contradiction: Auditory Distress vs No Medical] Reporting an active ear infection (auditory distress) fundamentally contradicts reporting that no medical conditions are present.
* EXC: [IS_CONTAGIOUS] | [Logical Contradiction: Medical Status] It is logically contradictory to document that a client has active contagious illness symptoms while simultaneously reporting that there are no medical issues or symptoms present.
* EXC: [IS_GI_DISTRESS] | [Medical Contradiction: GI Distress and No Medical Concerns] It is a direct logical and medical contradiction to report active gastrointestinal distress or constipation while simultaneously documenting that no medical issues were reported.
* EXC: [IS_LOW_AROUSAL] | [Logical Contradiction: Arousal/Fatigue vs No Medical] IS_LOW_AROUSAL includes symptoms like 'Lethargy / Fatigue' and 'Poor sleep' which represent physiological/medical distress states. This directly contradicts the absolute declaration of IS_NO_MEDICAL ('None reported').
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Logical Contradiction: Medical Crisis vs No Medical] IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS explicitly documents severe medical emergencies (e.g., Asthma, Seizures). It is logically and methodologically impossible to document a medical crisis while simultaneously declaring IS_NO_MEDICAL ('None reported').
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_DISTRESS] | [Logical Contradiction: Medical Distress vs No Medical] IS_MEDICAL_DISTRESS documents active physiological and medical symptoms (e.g., Allergies, Dental pain, Rash). This is fundamentally incompatible with the IS_NO_MEDICAL category ('None reported').
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_VARIABLE] | [Logical Contradiction: Medical Variable vs No Medical] IS_MEDICAL_VARIABLE identifies underlying physiological or pharmacological conditions (e.g., Medication change, Missed meal). Documenting these physiological variables directly invalidates the absolute claim of IS_NO_MEDICAL ('None reported').
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DISTRESS] | [Medical Contradiction: Symptom Reporting] Reporting no medical issues ('None reported') logically contradicts the presence of active oral distress, such as 'Dental pain'.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Medical Contradiction: Health Status] Reporting that a client has 'None reported' for medical issues directly contradicts the documentation of 'Asthma / Respiratory issues' within the same session.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_DISTRESS] | [Medical Contradiction: Health Status] Reporting that a client has 'None reported' for medical issues directly contradicts the documentation of sensory distress such as a 'Headache'.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Medical Status Contradiction: Sleep / Physiological] Lethargy due to poor sleep represents a physiological/medical setting event. Claiming "None reported" for medical status contradicts the presence of this sleep-related physiological issue.
* EXC: [IS_TACTILE_DISTRESS] | [Medical Status Contradiction: Dermatological] "Rash / Skin irritation" is a dermatological medical condition. It is logically contradictory to report this while simultaneously claiming "None reported" for medical issues.

---

## [IS_ORAL_DEMAND]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_DYSREGULATED] | [Medical Contradiction: Aspiration Risk] Placing an oral demand (eating, brushing teeth) on an individual who is highly dysregulated (crying, screaming) poses a severe choking and aspiration hazard.
* EXC: [IS_ELOPEMENT] | [Physical Contradiction: Elopement and Oral Demands] Attempting to feed or brush the teeth of an individual while they are actively eloping (running away, bolting) creates an acute choking and physical injury hazard.
* EXC: [IS_FALL_HAZARD] | [Physical Contradiction: Fall Hazard and Oral Demands] Presenting oral demands (eating, brushing teeth) while a client is actively engaged with a gross motor fall hazard (jumping, swinging, therapy ball) poses a critical risk of choking or injury.
* EXC: [IS_GI_DISTRESS] | [Medical and Ethical Violation: Oral Demand during GI Distress] Forcing an oral demand, such as eating a variety of foods, when a client is actively refusing meals due to GI distress or constipation is medically and ethically contraindicated.
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Gross Motor Targets and Oral Demands] Combining gross motor targets like jumping with two feet or catching a ball with concurrent oral demands such as eating a variety of foods poses a severe, immediate choking hazard.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_AROUSAL] | [Physical Safety Hazard: High Arousal and Oral Demands] Placing oral demands like eating or brushing teeth while a client is in a state of high arousal (e.g., agitated and crying, pacing rapidly) represents a significant choking and aspiration risk.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EXERTION] | [Physical Safety Hazard: High Exertion and Oral Demands] Placing oral demands, such as eating or brushing teeth, while a client is engaged in high exertion activities (e.g., jumping, running rapidly) creates a severe, immediate choking hazard.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_MOBILITY_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical Safety Hazard: High Mobility Environment and Oral Demands] Requiring a client to chew food or fulfill oral demands while physically navigating high mobility environments like a gross motor obstacle course or playground equipment represents a significant choking hazard.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Crisis Mismanagement: Oral Demands during Imminent Danger] Presenting an oral demand like eating or brushing teeth while a client is in imminent danger (such as bolting or severe aggression) is a severe choking hazard and reflects a dangerous lack of crisis prioritization.
* EXC: [IS_INAPPROPRIATE_LANGUAGE] | [Physical Impossibility: Inappropriate Language and Oral Demands] It is physically incompatible for a client to effectively articulate complex verbalizations like using profanity or making sexually explicit comments while simultaneously fulfilling an oral demand such as brushing teeth or chewing foods.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Harm Prevention] Placing oral demands (e.g., eating, brushing teeth) on a client actively experiencing oral distress or dental pain is ethically contraindicated, as it forces compliance and interaction through pain.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Safety Hazard: Oral Demand vs. Severe Topography] Placing oral demands (like feeding or brushing teeth) during episodes of severe behavioral topography (e.g., head banging, punching, kicking) creates an acute choking hazard and physical injury risk.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Safety Hazard: Oral Demand vs. Sharp Objects] Conducting oral hygiene or feeding demands while a client is simultaneously holding or using sharp objects (e.g., scissors) poses an extreme physical hazard for accidental facial laceration or puncture.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | [Safety Hazard: Oral Demand vs. SIB] Delivering oral demands (e.g., feeding or brushing teeth) while a client is actively engaging in self-injurious behavior (such as head banging or biting themselves) presents an acute aspiration, choking, and traumatic injury hazard.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Safety Hazard: Oral Demand vs. Sleep Lethargy] Presenting oral demands, such as eating a variety of foods or brushing teeth, to a client who is severely lethargic, yawning, or passively slumping presents a critical risk of aspiration and choking.
