* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Safety Hazard: Oral Demand vs. Staff Directed Aggression] Administering oral demands such as feeding or toothbrushing while a client is actively directing aggression at the therapist (biting, swiping) poses an immediate physical safety risk and severe choking hazard.
* EXC: [IS_TOXIC_HAZARD] | [Safety/Poisoning Hazard: Oral Demand with Toxic Materials] It is a severe poisoning risk and physically dangerous to concurrently mandate oral demands (e.g., eating, brushing teeth) while a client is handling or exposed to toxic hazards like household cleaning supplies.
* EXC: [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL] | [Safety/Choking Risk: Oral Demand during Traffic Navigation] Placing oral demands (e.g., eating a variety of foods, brushing teeth) while a client is actively navigating a parking lot or practicing safety crossing in traffic poses a severe choking hazard and unsafe visual distraction.

---

## [IS_ORAL_DISTRESS]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Ignoring Medical Distress] Utilizing planned ignoring (attention extinction) for behaviors that are stemming from or accompanied by active oral distress (like dental pain) is an ethical violation of client care and safety.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing a Medical Symptom] Applying aversive procedures, such as escape extinction or overcorrection, to a client suffering from dental pain (oral distress) violates BACB ethical guidelines that mandate addressing underlying medical or physiological pain before implementing behavioral reductive procedures.
* EXC: [IS_EDIBLE] | [Medical Contradiction: Feeding During Oral Distress] Administering edible reinforcers to an individual experiencing active oral distress, dental pain, or pooling saliva presents a direct choking hazard and can exacerbate physical pain.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Forcing Compliance Through Pain] Implementing escape extinction (forcing demand compliance) when a client is escaping due to acute medical or dental pain violates ethical guidelines regarding harm prevention.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Medical Contradiction: Unmonitored Oral Distress] Placing an individual in an isolated timeout while they are experiencing active oral distress or pooling saliva presents an ethical violation and an unmonitored choking risk.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical Violation: High-Effort Punishment during Oral Distress] Implementing physically demanding punishment procedures (like overcorrection) on a client actively experiencing oral distress or dental pain is ethically abusive and violates BACB harm prevention guidelines.
* EXC: [IS_NO_MEDICAL] | [Medical Contradiction: Symptom Reporting] Reporting no medical issues ('None reported') logically contradicts the presence of active oral distress, such as 'Dental pain'.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DEMAND] | [Ethical Violation: Harm Prevention] Placing oral demands (e.g., eating, brushing teeth) on a client actively experiencing oral distress or dental pain is ethically contraindicated, as it forces compliance and interaction through pain.
* EXC: [IS_VOCAL_VERBAL_TARGET] | [Medical Contraindication: Vocal Demands during Oral Distress] It is ethically and medically contraindicated to mandate vocal-verbal targets (e.g., conversational turn-taking, echoics, manding) when a client is experiencing acute oral distress, such as dental pain or severe tissue damage from biting their lips.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Medical Neglect: Waiting Out Oral Distress] Implementing a passive wait-out procedure is medically neglectful and dangerous when a client is experiencing acute oral distress, dental pain, or tissue damage, which requires immediate medical/clinical intervention rather than behavioral extinction.

---

## [IS_OUTDOOR_MATERIAL]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Timeout and Environmental Engagement] An exclusionary timeout fundamentally requires the removal of access to reinforcing environments; it cannot co-occur with engaging with outdoor materials like playgrounds or water play tables.
* EXC: [IS_FOOD_PREP_AREA] | [Physical Impossibility: Food Prep Area and Outdoor Material] It is physically incompatible to utilize large-scale outdoor playground equipment or a gross motor obstacle course within food preparation areas such as a kitchen, dining room, or store.
* EXC: [IS_HOME_SETTING] | [Physical Impossibility: Home Setting and Outdoor Material] It is physically impossible to host large-scale outdoor playground equipment or water play tables naturally within a standard home environment like a bedroom or kitchen.
* EXC: [IS_INDOOR_SETTING] | [Physical Impossibility: Indoor Setting and Outdoor Material] Large-scale outdoor playground structures and gross motor obstacle courses are physically incompatible with typical indoor environments like a therapy room or bathroom.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Safety Hazard: Gross Motor Obstacles vs. Sharp Objects] Permitting a client to navigate gross motor obstacle courses or playground equipment while possessing sharp objects like scissors poses an extreme risk of severe puncture injuries or lacerations in the event of a fall.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Safety Hazard: Gross Motor Obstacles vs. Sleep Lethargy] Directing a client to navigate gross motor obstacle courses or playground equipment while they are passively slumping or severely lethargic poses a severe fall and physical injury risk.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | [Physical Impossibility: Outdoor Materials in Toileting Facilities] It is physically and conceptually nonsensical to deploy large outdoor gross motor materials (e.g., playground equipment) within a private indoor bathroom setting during toileting ADLs.
* EXC: [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL] | [Physical Safety Risk: Outdoor Play Items in Traffic] It is physically dangerous and functionally contradictory to utilize outdoor gross motor play materials (e.g., playground equipment, large obstacle courses) within active traffic hazard zones such as streets or parking lots.
* EXC: [IS_VIBRATING_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical Impossibility: Outdoor Gross Motor in Transport] It is physically impossible to construct or utilize large outdoor materials, such as playground equipment or gross motor obstacle courses, inside a standard vibrating environment like a moving vehicle or transport.

---

## [IS_OUTDOOR_REINFORCER]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_HIGH_MOBILITY_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical/Environmental Requirement] Delivering 'Access to Outdoors' as an outdoor reinforcer fundamentally requires the physical presence of an environment with outdoor or high-mobility access to be provided.
* REQ: [IS_NON_CLINIC] | Derived Chain (IS_OUTDOOR_REINFORCER -> IS_PUBLIC -> IS_NON_CLINIC): [Physical/Environmental Requirement] Providing access to the outdoors as a reinforcer inherently requires the physical environment of being outdoors, which is categorized under public or community settings. | [Physical/Environmental Requirement: Public Settings] Any service delivery, intervention, or observation occurring in a public setting (such as a community area, store, or playground) physically dictates that the session is taking place outside of a dedicated private facility, inherently requiring a non-clinic environment.
* REQ: [IS_PUBLIC] | [Physical/Environmental Requirement] Providing access to the outdoors as a reinforcer inherently requires the physical environment of being outdoors, which is categorized under public or community settings.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Timeout and Reinforcement] An exclusionary timeout fundamentally requires the removal of reinforcement; it cannot methodologically co-occur with providing access to the outdoors as a reinforcer.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Methodological Violation: Reinforcing Imminent Danger] Providing access to the outdoors as a reinforcer while a client is actively engaging in life-threatening imminent danger behaviors (e.g., running into the street, elopement) is a severe methodological violation.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_OUTDOOR_REINFORCER' forces 'IS_HIGH_MOBILITY_ENVIRONMENT'. 'IS_TOILETING_ADL' forces 'IS_WATER_ENVIRONMENT'. 'IS_HIGH_MOBILITY_ENVIRONMENT' and 'IS_WATER_ENVIRONMENT' cannot co-occur: [Environmental Mismatch: Playground and Bathroom Routine] A playground setting intrinsically contradicts an indoor bathroom/restroom water environment meant for brushing teeth or structured hand-washing routines.

---

## [IS_PEER_OR_SIBLING]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ELOPEMENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Elopement and Cooperative Social Play] It is physically and methodologically impossible for an individual to engage in sustained cooperative play, joint attention, or sibling play while actively bolting or eloping from the area.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Timeout and Social Interaction] Exclusionary timeout socially isolates the individual, making it physically and methodologically impossible to simultaneously participate in cooperative or parallel play with peers or siblings.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | [Privacy Violation: Peer/Sibling Involvement in Toileting] It is a severe ethical and dignity violation of BACB guidelines to involve peers or siblings in a client's highly private toileting and hygiene ADL routines.

---

## [IS_PEER_TARGET]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_PEER_OR_SIBLING] | [Physical/Environmental Requirement] Executing, practicing, and measuring a peer-mediated skill target (such as peer greetings, initiating play, or turn-taking) fundamentally requires the physical presence and active participation of a peer or sibling in the clinical environment.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_CONTAGIOUS] | [Ethical Violation: Infection Control] Facilitating or targeting interactions with non-sibling peers (e.g., sharing items, cooperative play, turn-taking) when a client is actively exhibiting contagious illness symptoms is a direct violation of standard health, safety, and infection control policies.
* EXC: [IS_DYSREGULATED] | [Methodological Mismatch: Dysregulation and Social Play] An individual cannot methodologically sustain cooperative peer play or conversational turn-taking while in a state of severe dysregulation (agitated, crying, screaming).
* EXC: [IS_ELOPEMENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Elopement and Peer Targets] It is physically and methodologically impossible for an individual to execute targeted cooperative peer play or turn-taking while actively bolting or eloping from the instructional area.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Timeout and Peer Targets] Exclusionary timeout socially isolates the individual, making it physically and methodologically impossible to simultaneously target peer greetings, play, or turn-taking.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_AROUSAL] | [Methodological Mismatch: Peer Targets during High Arousal] It is methodologically impossible and clinically inappropriate to target complex peer interactions, such as conversational turn-taking, while a client is in an active state of high arousal (agitated, crying, hyperactive).
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Overcorrection and Peer Targets] It is methodologically impossible and ethically inappropriate to simultaneously implement a high-effort punishment procedure (like overcorrection) while actively prompting cooperative skill acquisition targets such as peer play or turn-taking.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Ethical and Safety Violation: Peer Targets during Imminent Danger] Prompting peer interaction targets (e.g., conversational turn-taking, initiating play) while a client is actively displaying imminent danger (aggression, SIB) exposes peers to direct harm, violating ethical safety guidelines.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | [Privacy Violation: Peer Targets during Toileting] Targeting peer interaction or collaborative play skills during a client's private toileting routines represents a fundamental breach of client dignity, privacy, and clinical ethics.

---

## [IS_PHYSICAL_CONTACT]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Timeout and Physical Contact] Providing positive physical contact (such as deep pressure, squeezes, or tickles) during an exclusionary timeout violates the procedural withdrawal of social and sensory reinforcement.
* EXC: [IS_REMOTE] | [Physical Impossibility: Remote Therapy Constraints] Telehealth sessions occur remotely, making physical contact (e.g., holding hands, deep pressure) between the therapist and the client physically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Safety Hazard: Physical Contact vs. Sharp Objects] Engaging in close physical contact, such as physical prompting, tickling, or deep pressure, while a client handles sharp objects like scissors creates an acute, immediate risk of accidental puncture or laceration.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Physical Contact during Wait Out] A wait-out procedure necessitates minimizing interaction and physical contact; providing affirmative physical touch, deep pressure, or tickles completely contradicts the fundamental behavioral methodology of waiting.

---

## [IS_PHYSICAL_CONTACT_WITH_THERAPIST]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Physical Impossibility: Ignoring Direct Physical Attacks] It is methodologically and physically impossible to safely utilize planned ignoring (attention extinction) while a client is actively making direct physical contact with the therapist (e.g., biting or head-butting the therapist). Active physical intervention is required for practitioner safety.
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Academic Tutoring and Severe Aggression] A therapist cannot safely or methodologically administer educational tutoring demands (worksheets, writing) while the client is actively directing severe physical aggression (biting, head-butting) at them.
* EXC: [IS_FINE_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Methodological Mismatch: Fine Motor Precision and Severe Aggression] An individual cannot methodologically execute precise fine motor targets (tracing letters, stringing beads) while actively engaging in severe aggression (biting, head-butting) directed at the therapist.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Independence vs Aggressive Contact] A client cannot logically be displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior or transitioning happily while actively engaging in aggressive physical contact with a therapist, such as biting, spitting, or head-butting.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_PROMPT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting During Aggression] Applying physical guidance or prompts while a client is actively engaging in dangerous physical contact or aggression toward the therapist (e.g., biting, head-butting, spitting) is methodologically contraindicated and exponentially increases the risk of injury.
* EXC: [IS_REMOTE] | [Physical Impossibility: Remote Therapy Constraints] Physical or aggressive contact directed specifically at the therapist (e.g., biting, swiping) is physically impossible when the therapist is providing remote/telehealth services.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Clinical State Contradiction: Aggression vs. Regulated Status] Active physical aggression towards a therapist (e.g., head-butting, biting) is a clear indicator of distress and dysregulation, contradicting clinical states of being highly motivated, regulated, or having a happy transition.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Physical Impossibility: Therapist Directed Aggression vs. Smooth Transition] Directing aggression at a therapist (e.g., biting, head-butting, swiping) indicates significant resistance and distress, rendering a smooth, happy, and independent transition physically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Safety Violation: Waiting Out Therapist-Directed Aggression] It is a severe safety violation to employ a passive wait-out procedure when a client is actively engaging in dangerous physical aggression directed at the therapist (e.g., biting, head-butting, swiping), which firmly mandates active physical safety management.

---

## [IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Ethical Methodological Dependency] The application of physical interventions to restrict or physically block a patient ethically requires a documented threat of imminent danger (such as aggression, elopement, or SIB) to ensure patient rights and safety protocols are maintained.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ADL_TARGET] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_ADL_TARGET' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' forces 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching During Crisis] Formal differential reinforcement of alternative behaviors requires the client to be receptive to teaching; it cannot be methodologically implemented during a life-threatening, imminent danger crisis where safety/blocking is the sole priority.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_AVERSIVE' forces 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching During Crisis] Formal differential reinforcement of alternative behaviors requires the client to be receptive to teaching; it cannot be methodologically implemented during a life-threatening, imminent danger crisis where safety/blocking is the sole priority.
* EXC: [IS_CHAINING_FORMAT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' forces 'IS_CHAINED'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINED' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching ADLs During Crisis] It is methodologically invalid and physically unsafe to conduct chained daily living activities (e.g., tying shoes, hygiene routines) while a client is in imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_EDIBLE] | [Physical Contradiction: Swallowing Intervention] It is physically and methodologically contradictory to administer an edible item while simultaneously executing physical interventions (e.g., blocking an attempt to swallow or tactical breaks).
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING' forces 'IS_ACADEMIC'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_ACADEMIC' cannot co-occur: [Safety Violation: Academics during Crisis] Implementing academic demands like counting or tracing while a client is in imminent danger (bolting, severe self-injury) is a severe safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_EXECUTIVE_FUNCTIONING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Cognitive Processing and Physical Intervention] Methodologically, an individual cannot perform high-level cognitive targets (independent problem-solving) while simultaneously being physically blocked for severe safety concerns or placed in a tactical break.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE' forces 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' cannot co-occur: [Ethical Violation: Extinction during Imminent Danger] Using planned ignoring for behaviors that present an imminent danger (e.g., elopement, head-banging, gouging eyes) is a severe ethical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Independence vs Physical Interventions] Documenting a client as displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior and navigating smoothly directly contradicts the documented need for physical blocking or tactical breaks.
* EXC: [IS_NON_DANGEROUS_BEHAVIOR] | [Ethical Violation: Least Restrictive Alternative] Implementing restrictive physical interventions (e.g., blocking, tactical breaks) to manage non-dangerous behaviors (e.g., stereotypy, vocal protests, non-compliance) violates BACB ethical guidelines requiring the use of least restrictive procedures.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_PROMPT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Skill Acquisition vs. Crisis Management] Applying physical prompts for skill acquisition simultaneously with physical interventions (e.g., tactical breaks, safety blocking) is physically contradictory, methodologically impossible, and poses a severe safety risk.
* EXC: [IS_REMOTE] | [Physical/Methodological Impossibility: Remote Crisis Management] Therapists cannot physically execute interventions, blocking, or tactical breaks remotely over Telehealth.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Medical/Ethical Violation: Restraint and Respiratory Compromise] Applying physical interventions, blocking, or tactical breaks on a client actively experiencing respiratory distress or asthma is extremely dangerous and medically contraindicated due to the high risk of positional asphyxiation.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Clinical State Contradiction: Physical Intervention vs. Regulated Status] Implementing a tactical break or physical blocking addresses severe behavioral escalation and danger, which contradicts a regulated state characterized by being highly motivated or transitioning happily.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Physical Impossibility: Physical Intervention vs. Smooth Transition] Requiring a tactical break or physical blocking implies severe behavioral resistance, which directly contradicts transitioning smoothly and independently without resistance.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_INDEPENDENT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Independence] Active states of imminent danger (severe aggression, SIB, elopement) strictly contradict the definition of independence, which mandates safe navigation and a complete absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_TOILETING_ADL' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Active Physical Intervention vs Wait Out] Employing active physical interventions (e.g., physically blocking an exit, tactical breaks) is methodologically mutually exclusive with a wait-out procedure, which requires passive non-intervention and withholding of contact.

---

## [IS_PHYSICAL_PROMPT]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Timeout and Instructional Prompts] Exclusionary timeout dictates the withdrawal of instructional interaction. Providing physical prompts (hand-over-hand) violates the methodological definition of a timeout.
* EXC: [IS_EXECUTIVE_FUNCTIONING] | [Methodological Impossibility: Prompting Internal Processes] Executive functioning targets (problem-solving, self-monitoring) are internal cognitive processes that cannot be methodologically or physically prompted (e.g., hand-over-hand guidance).
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Medical and Safety Violation: Physical Prompting on Compromised Hands] Applying physical prompts or manually guiding hands that are currently compromised by severe self-injurious behavior (e.g., actively biting or scratching) is medically unsafe and risks exacerbating tissue damage.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Methodological Mismatch: Physical Prompting during Imminent Danger] Attempting to use physical prompts for skill acquisition (e.g., partial physical prompts) while a client is in an active state of imminent danger is a serious methodological mismatch; physical safety management must take precedent.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Independence vs Physical Prompts] A behavior cannot methodologically be defined as independent, smooth, or mastered while simultaneously requiring physical prompts or guidance from a therapist.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_CONTACT_WITH_THERAPIST] | [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting During Aggression] Applying physical guidance or prompts while a client is actively engaging in dangerous physical contact or aggression toward the therapist (e.g., biting, head-butting, spitting) is methodologically contraindicated and exponentially increases the risk of injury.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Skill Acquisition vs. Crisis Management] Applying physical prompts for skill acquisition simultaneously with physical interventions (e.g., tactical breaks, safety blocking) is physically contradictory, methodologically impossible, and poses a severe safety risk.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Safety Management Conflict] Physical prompts (e.g., hand-over-hand guidance) cannot be safely or effectively implemented while a client requires active physical safety management (e.g., blocking, safety pads) for crisis behavior.
* EXC: [IS_REMOTE] | [Physical Impossibility: Remote Therapy Constraints] Physical prompting by the therapist (e.g., partial or full physical guidance) is physically impossible in a remote Telehealth session.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | [Methodological Contradiction: Prompting vs. Spontaneity] A spontaneous behavior is defined by the absence of prompts. It is methodologically impossible for a target response to be both physically prompted and spontaneously demonstrated.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Physical Prompting during Wait Out] Administering partial or full physical prompts completely negates the methodology of a wait-out procedure, which relies on strategically withholding active guidance until compliance occurs independently.

---

## [IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Ethical Safety Requirement] Ethically and procedurally, implementing physical safety management (such as protective equipment or blocking) fundamentally requires the presence of an imminent danger behavior to justify its use.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ADL_TARGET] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_ADL_TARGET' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' forces 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching During Crisis] Formal differential reinforcement of alternative behaviors requires the client to be receptive to teaching; it cannot be methodologically implemented during a life-threatening, imminent danger crisis where safety/blocking is the sole priority.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_AVERSIVE' forces 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching During Crisis] Formal differential reinforcement of alternative behaviors requires the client to be receptive to teaching; it cannot be methodologically implemented during a life-threatening, imminent danger crisis where safety/blocking is the sole priority.
* EXC: [IS_CHAINING_FORMAT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' forces 'IS_CHAINED'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINED' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching ADLs During Crisis] It is methodologically invalid and physically unsafe to conduct chained daily living activities (e.g., tying shoes, hygiene routines) while a client is in imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Academic Tutoring and Safety Management] Administering academic/worksheet demands while an individual is in a state requiring protective safety pads or physical blocking is methodologically and physically incompatible.
* EXC: [IS_EXECUTIVE_FUNCTIONING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Executive Functioning and Severe Behavior] An individual requiring protective safety equipment or physical blocks for dangerous behavior is not in an emotional or regulated state to simultaneously engage in complex independent executive functioning tasks.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE' forces 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' cannot co-occur: [Ethical Violation: Extinction during Imminent Danger] Using planned ignoring for behaviors that present an imminent danger (e.g., elopement, head-banging, gouging eyes) is a severe ethical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_FINE_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Methodological Mismatch: Fine Motor Precision and Safety Management] An individual requiring protective safety equipment or physical blocks for severe behavior cannot simultaneously be engaged in executing fine motor precision tasks like tracing or pincer grasp exercises.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Independence vs Safety Management] A client transitioning safely and independently cannot concurrently require physical safety management interventions, such as blocking with safety pads to prevent an exit.
* EXC: [IS_NON_DANGEROUS_BEHAVIOR] | [Ethical Violation: Least Restrictive Alternative] Using physical safety management techniques (e.g., physical blocking to prevent exit, safety pads) for purely non-dangerous behaviors is an ethical violation of the mandate for least restrictive behavior management.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_PROMPT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Safety Management Conflict] Physical prompts (e.g., hand-over-hand guidance) cannot be safely or effectively implemented while a client requires active physical safety management (e.g., blocking, safety pads) for crisis behavior.
* EXC: [IS_REMOTE] | [Physical/Methodological Impossibility: Remote Crisis Management] Physical safety management procedures, such as physically blocking a swallow or an exit, cannot be performed by a remote therapist via Telehealth.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Medical/Ethical Violation: Restraint and Respiratory Compromise] Physically blocking or holding a client for safety management while they are experiencing asthma or respiratory distress is medically contraindicated due to asphyxiation risks.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Clinical State Contradiction: Safety Management vs. Regulated Status] The necessity of physical safety management (e.g., blocking an exit, using safety pads) indicates an acute behavioral crisis, which is mutually exclusive with a state of being behaviorally regulated, highly motivated, and engaged in a happy transition.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Physical Impossibility: Safety Management vs. Smooth Transition] A transition characterized as smooth, independent, and without resistance cannot physically co-occur with an acute behavioral crisis requiring physical safety management, such as blocking an exit or using safety pads.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_INDEPENDENT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Independence] Active states of imminent danger (severe aggression, SIB, elopement) strictly contradict the definition of independence, which mandates safe navigation and a complete absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_TOILETING_ADL' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Safety Contradiction: Passive Wait Out vs Active Safety Management] A wait-out procedure cannot be safely or methodologically implemented when a client concurrently requires active physical safety management (e.g., using safety pads, blocking swallow attempts) to prevent immediate physical harm.

---

## [IS_PICA]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION] | [Ethical/Safety Requirement: Pica Management] Ethically and safely managing a client who engages in Pica strictly requires environmental modifications, such as sweeping and clearing the area of hazards, to prevent the ingestion of dangerous non-edible items.
* REQ: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | Derived Chain (IS_PICA -> IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION -> IS_IMMINENT_DANGER): [Ethical/Safety Requirement: Pica Management] Ethically and safely managing a client who engages in Pica strictly requires environmental modifications, such as sweeping and clearing the area of hazards, to prevent the ingestion of dangerous non-edible items. | [Crisis Management Dependency] Environmental modification, such as sweeping an area for hazards or clearing a room, is a crisis management strategy that fundamentally depends on the presence or immediate risk of imminent danger to ensure physical safety.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ADL_TARGET] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PICA' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_ADL_TARGET' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Harm Prevention in Pica] Using planned ignoring (attention extinction) is an ethical and safety violation when a client is engaging in Pica (swallowing small objects), as active intervention is required to prevent choking or internal injury.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PICA' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_AVERSIVE' forces 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_DIFFERENTIAL_REINFORCEMENT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching During Crisis] Formal differential reinforcement of alternative behaviors requires the client to be receptive to teaching; it cannot be methodologically implemented during a life-threatening, imminent danger crisis where safety/blocking is the sole priority.
* EXC: [IS_CHAINING_FORMAT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PICA' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' forces 'IS_CHAINED'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINED' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching ADLs During Crisis] It is methodologically invalid and physically unsafe to conduct chained daily living activities (e.g., tying shoes, hygiene routines) while a client is in imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PICA' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING' forces 'IS_ACADEMIC'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_ACADEMIC' cannot co-occur: [Safety Violation: Academics during Crisis] Implementing academic demands like counting or tracing while a client is in imminent danger (bolting, severe self-injury) is a severe safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Medical Contradiction: Unmonitored Pica Hazard] Placing an individual with active Pica in an isolated exclusionary timeout presents a severe, potentially fatal risk of unobserved ingestion of non-edible items.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PICA' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE' forces 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' cannot co-occur: [Ethical Violation: Extinction during Imminent Danger] Using planned ignoring for behaviors that present an imminent danger (e.g., elopement, head-banging, gouging eyes) is a severe ethical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_FRAGMENTABLE_MATERIAL] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Fragmentable Materials and Pica] Providing fragmentable materials such as kinetic sand or playdough to a client actively engaging in Pica creates an immediate and severe swallowing and choking hazard.
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Gross Motor Targets and Pica] Targeting dynamic gross motor skills like jumping or catching a ball while a client is actively engaging in Pica creates a critical and immediate choking hazard.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_AROUSAL] | [Physical Safety Hazard: High Arousal and Pica] Engaging in high arousal behaviors, such as agitated crying or running rapidly, while attempting to swallow small non-edible objects (Pica) poses a critical aspiration and choking hazard.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EXERTION] | [Physical Safety Hazard: High Exertion and Pica] Engaging in high exertion activities like running or jumping while actively displaying Pica (swallowing small objects) poses a critical aspiration and choking risk.
* EXC: [IS_INAPPROPRIATE_LANGUAGE] | [Physical Impossibility: Inappropriate Language and Pica] It is physically impossible to clearly articulate complex, inappropriate verbalizations while actively engaging in Pica (e.g., swallowing small objects or mouthing non-edible items).
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Independence vs Pica] A client actively engaging in Pica contradicts the explicit definition of independence in this ontology, which requires displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Safety Hazard: Pica vs. Sharp Objects] Allowing access to sharp objects (like scissors) for a client actively engaging in pica (mouthing or attempting to swallow non-edible items) creates a catastrophic risk of internal laceration and severe injury.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PICA' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION' forces 'IS_REGULATED'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_REGULATED' cannot co-occur: [Behavioral Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Regulated State] A client actively engaging in imminent danger (aggression, elopement, SIB) cannot be described as calm, regulated, or displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PICA' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_INDEPENDENT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Independence] Active states of imminent danger (severe aggression, SIB, elopement) strictly contradict the definition of independence, which mandates safe navigation and a complete absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PICA' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_TOILETING_ADL' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_TOXIC_HAZARD] | [Severe Poisoning Risk: Pica with Toxic Hazards] Exposing a client who actively engages in pica (mouthing, licking, or swallowing non-edible items) to toxic hazards such as household cleaning supplies constitutes an extreme, life-threatening medical safety risk.

---

## [IS_PREMACK_CONTINGENCY]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Methodological Violation: Overcorrection as Premack Demand] It is a methodological violation of Skinnerian principles to frame a high-effort punishment procedure (overcorrection) as the 'First' demand in a Premack contingency (First/Then).
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Crisis Mismanagement: Premack Contingency during Imminent Danger] Attempting to negotiate with a Premack Principle (First/Then) during a state of imminent danger (e.g., elopement, severe SIB) is methodologically contraindicated and violates basic crisis management protocols.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | [Methodological Contradiction: Premack vs. Spontaneity] A behavior occasioned by a Premack contingency (First/Then prompt) is explicitly teacher-directed. It is methodologically contradictory to label a response as spontaneous if it was contingent on a Premack prompt.

---

## [IS_PROJECTILE_BEHAVIOR]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_DYSREGULATED] | [Methodological Dependency] Projectile behaviors, such as throwing instructional materials or sweeping items off a desk, are maladaptive responses that clinically indicate a state of behavioral dysregulation.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Independence vs Projectile Behavior] A client displaying independent, mastered behavior with an absence of maladaptive behavior cannot simultaneously engage in projectile behavior (throwing materials).
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Behavioral State Contradiction] A clinical note cannot logically claim the client is completely regulated (e.g., displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior, calm and cooperative) while simultaneously documenting active projectile behaviors such as throwing or sweeping instructional materials.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Methodological Mismatch: Projectile Behavior and Regulated State] Throwing instructional materials is a maladaptive behavior that directly contradicts being highly regulated, compliant, and displaying an absence of problem behavior.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Projectile Behavior and Smooth Transition] A smooth, happy, and independent transition is physically and methodologically incompatible with the disruption of throwing items and sweeping materials off a desk.
* EXC: [IS_SOCIAL_PLAY] | [Methodological Mismatch: Projectile Behavior and Social Play] Throwing instructional materials disrupts the environment and contradicts the sustained cooperative engagement and turn-taking required for functional social play.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PROJECTILE_BEHAVIOR' forces 'IS_DYSREGULATED'. 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_DYSREGULATED' and 'IS_INDEPENDENT' cannot co-occur: [Definitional Contradiction: Dysregulated vs Independent] A client cannot be simultaneously dysregulated (e.g., crying, whining, visibly frustrated) and independent (demonstrating an absence of maladaptive behavior, transitioning smoothly and happily).

---

## [IS_PROPERTY_DESTRUCTION]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_DYSREGULATED] | [Methodological Dependency] Property destruction is a severe maladaptive behavior that inherently signifies behavioral dysregulation; a learner cannot engage in destructive aggression while remaining fully regulated.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Independence vs Property Destruction] A client demonstrating independent, smooth, and appropriate behavior cannot be actively engaging in property destruction.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Behavioral State Contradiction] It is a logical and behavioral contradiction to state a client is actively regulated, cooperative, and displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior while also documenting property destruction such as breaking toys or ripping data sheets.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Physical Impossibility: Property Destruction and Lethargy] High-energy destructive behaviors, such as kicking holes in walls or overturning tables, physically contradict a physiological state of lethargy, yawning, and passively slumping.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Definitional Contradiction: Property Destruction and Regulated State] Property destruction is a severe maladaptive behavior that is mutually exclusive with a highly motivated, happy, and regulated state.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Definitional Contradiction: Property Destruction and Smooth Transition] Successfully and smoothly transitioning to a new area cannot co-occur with violent resistance such as breaking toys or slamming doors.
* EXC: [IS_SOCIAL_PLAY] | [Definitional Contradiction: Property Destruction and Social Play] Engaging in property destruction, such as breaking toys, is incompatible with the positive peer interaction and sharing required for social play.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_PROPERTY_DESTRUCTION' forces 'IS_DYSREGULATED'. 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_DYSREGULATED' and 'IS_INDEPENDENT' cannot co-occur: [Definitional Contradiction: Dysregulated vs Independent] A client cannot be simultaneously dysregulated (e.g., crying, whining, visibly frustrated) and independent (demonstrating an absence of maladaptive behavior, transitioning smoothly and happily).

---

## [IS_PROXIMITY_PAIRING]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AGGRESSION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Pairing during Aggression] Initiating physical proximity pairing (e.g., tickles, deep pressure) during active aggression (punching, biting) is physically unsafe and methodologically invalid, as it provides direct positive reinforcement for aggressive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Extinction and Proximity Pairing] Planned ignoring requires the strict withholding of attention, which physically and methodologically cannot co-occur with proximity pairing actions like tickles or physical touch.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | [Methodological Mismatch: Aversives and Proximity Pairing] Simultaneously applying aversive interventions (e.g., response cost, overcorrection) and providing positive proximity pairing (e.g., tickles, deep pressure) presents a direct Skinnerian contradiction of concurrent punishment and reinforcement.
* EXC: [IS_BIO_HAZARD] | [Infection Control: Bio-Hazard and Proximity Pairing] Engaging in proximity pairing activities (such as tickles, holding hands, or deep pressure) during active bio-hazardous behaviors (like spitting, fecal smearing, or pica) poses a severe hygiene and infection control risk.
* EXC: [IS_CONTAGIOUS] | [Medical Safety: Infection Control during Pairing] Engaging in close physical proximity pairing (e.g., holding hands, physical touch) with an actively contagious client violates medical infection control standards.
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | [Methodological Contradiction: Crisis vs Physical Pairing] Attempting physical proximity pairing (such as tickles or hand-holding) during an acute crisis requiring safety blocks or environmental clearing is unsafe and methodologically invalid.
* EXC: [IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED] | [Physical Impossibility: Safety Distance vs Proximity Touch] Mandating a required safety distance physically contradicts engaging in close proximity pairing actions like holding hands or deep pressure touch.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Physical/Methodological Contradiction: Timeout vs. Proximity Pairing] Exclusionary timeout requires removing the client from reinforcing environments and people, which physically and methodologically contradicts proximity pairing like holding hands or providing deep pressure.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Reinforcing Punishment] Providing proximity pairing (e.g., tickles, deep pressure, pairing with toys) concurrently with high effort punishment (e.g., overcorrection) fundamentally violates behavioral methodology by reinforcing a punishing consequence.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Methodological Mismatch: Reinforcing Imminent Danger] Engaging in proximity pairing (tickles, pairing with toys, physical touch) while a client is in imminent danger (aggression, SIB, elopement) dangerously reinforces life-threatening behaviors.
* EXC: [IS_INAPPROPRIATE_LANGUAGE] | [Methodological Mismatch: Reinforcing Inappropriate Language] Providing proximity pairing (tickles, physical touch) while a client is using inappropriate language (profanity, verbal threats) is a methodological error that reinforces the maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_LOWER_BODY_DISROBING] | [Ethical Violation: Boundaries and Physical Touch] Engaging in physical touch, tickling, or proximity pairing while a client is engaged in lower-body disrobing constitutes a severe ethical boundary violation and a risk of abuse allegations.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical/Safety Violation: Crisis vs Proximity Pairing] Engaging in proximity pairing (such as tickling, applying deep pressure, or playing with toys) during an acute medical crisis ignores emergency protocols and constitutes a severe safety risk.
* EXC: [IS_REMOTE] | [Physical Impossibility] Proximity pairing techniques that strictly require direct physical contact (e.g., holding the therapist's hand, physical touch, deep pressure squeezes, tickles) are physically impossible for a clinician to execute when delivering services remotely via telehealth.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Safety Hazard: Proximity Pairing during Severe Topography] Attempting physical touch, tickles, or close proximity pairing while a client is engaging in severe topographies (e.g., bolting, smearing feces) is a severe safety hazard and methodologically incorrect.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Safety Hazard: Proximity Pairing with Sharp Objects] Providing physical touch, deep pressure, or tickles while a client is utilizing sharp objects like scissors presents an unacceptable physical safety hazard.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | [Safety Hazard: Proximity Pairing during SIB] Utilizing proximity pairing (e.g., tickles, hand-holding) while a client is actively engaging in dangerous SIB (e.g., eye gouging) is unsafe and would inadvertently reinforce the self-injury.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Safety Hazard: Proximity Pairing during Staff Aggression] Attempting to pair physically (tickles, deep pressure) while a client is actively biting or punching staff is both physically impossible to execute safely and methodologically contraindicated.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | [Ethical Violation: Dignity and Boundary Breach] Applying deep pressure, tickles, or physical pairing during toileting ADLs violates client dignity, physical boundaries, and ethical guidelines regarding private personal care.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Reinforcement vs Extinction] Proximity pairing involves active rapport building and reinforcement (e.g., tickles, toy pairing), which conceptually contradicts the passive withholding of attention and demands required in a wait-out procedure.

---

## [IS_PROXIMITY_PROMPT]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Independence vs Prompting] A skill or behavior cannot be documented as fully independent (spontaneous, unprompted) while simultaneously receiving proximity, model, or gestural prompts.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Active Prompting vs Wait Out] Using active instructional prompts (gestural, positional, model) violates the core methodology of a wait-out procedure, which requires the temporary removal of demands.

---

## [IS_PUBLIC]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_NON_CLINIC] | [Physical/Environmental Requirement: Public Settings] Any service delivery, intervention, or observation occurring in a public setting (such as a community area, store, or playground) physically dictates that the session is taking place outside of a dedicated private facility, inherently requiring a non-clinic environment.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_CLINIC_SETTING] | [Physical Impossibility: Clinic vs Public Setting] A specialized clinical facility is an indoor, controlled environment, making it physically impossible to simultaneously occur in an open, public community setting like a store or playground.
* EXC: [IS_CONFIDENTIAL_DISCUSSION] | [Ethical Violation: HIPAA Privacy in Public Spaces] Openly discussing confidential client data, IEP goals, or behavior incidents in public community spaces (such as stores or playgrounds) violates HIPAA and BACB confidentiality mandates.
* EXC: [IS_CONTAGIOUS] | [Medical Safety: Public Health Risk] Taking a client actively presenting contagious illness symptoms into a public community setting poses a severe public health and transmission risk.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical Impossibility: Sensory Room in Public Space] A controlled sensory break requiring a dark, quiet room physically cannot be conducted in open, public areas such as playgrounds or stores.

---

## [IS_REGULATED]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AGGRESSION] | [Behavioral Mismatch: Aggression vs Regulated State] Active physical aggression (e.g., punching, biting, swiping) inherently involves high levels of maladaptive behavior, which contradicts a regulated state defined by calmness, cooperation, and an absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Extinction on Regulated Client] Attention extinction (planned ignoring) targets active maladaptive behaviors. Applying it when a client is regulated, cooperative, and displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior is a fundamental methodological error.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_SENSITIVITY] | [Behavioral Mismatch: Auditory Sensitivity vs Regulated State] A client being actively triggered by loud noises (auditory sensitivity) is in a state of neurological/sensory distress, which physically and behaviorally precludes them from simultaneously being calm, relaxed, and regulated.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Regulated Behavior] Applying aversive or punishing procedures (e.g., overcorrection, response cost, escape extinction) to a regulated, cooperative client represents a strict ethical and methodological violation.
* EXC: [IS_BIO_HAZARD] | [Methodological Contradiction: Severe Maladaptive vs Regulated State] Bio-hazardous behaviors represent severe, active maladaptive occurrences, which methodologically contradicts the definition of a regulated state (characterized by calm behavior and the absence of maladaptive behaviors).
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | [Methodological Contradiction: Crisis vs Regulated State] A client actively presenting crisis indicators (e.g., agitated, requiring physical blocking) cannot simultaneously be in a calm, regulated state devoid of maladaptive behaviors.
* EXC: [IS_DYSREGULATED] | [Contradictory Clinical States: Dysregulation vs. Regulation] A client cannot simultaneously be documented as dysregulated (e.g., agitated, crying) and regulated (e.g., calm, displaying an absence of maladaptive behaviors).
* EXC: [IS_ELOPEMENT] | [Contradictory Clinical States: Elopement vs. Regulation] Active elopement is a maladaptive behavior, which inherently contradicts the documentation of a regulated client displaying an absence of maladaptive behaviors.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing a Regulated Client] Applying an exclusionary timeout to a client who is regulated and displaying no maladaptive behaviors violates the ethical principles of behavior analysis.
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Self-Injury vs Regulated State] Hand compromise (e.g., biting or scratching own hands) is a form of self-injurious behavior, which fundamentally contradicts a regulated, calm state characterized by the absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_AROUSAL] | [Behavioral Contradiction: High Arousal vs Regulated State] A client demonstrating high arousal (agitated, crying, hyperactive) cannot simultaneously be in a regulated state (calm, relaxed, neutral affect).
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Punishing a Regulated State] Implementing high effort punishment (overcorrection) on a client who is regulated and demonstrating an absence of maladaptive behavior is a methodological and ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Regulated State] A client actively engaging in imminent danger (aggression, elopement, SIB) cannot be described as calm, regulated, or displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_INAPPROPRIATE_LANGUAGE] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Inappropriate Language vs Regulated State] Using profanity, derogatory names, or verbal threats fundamentally contradicts a regulated state characterized by calmness and the absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_LOWER_BODY_DISROBING] | [Methodological Contradiction: Disrobing vs Regulated State] Lower body disrobing is an acute maladaptive behavior, which is physically and behaviorally incompatible with displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior or being fully regulated.
* EXC: [IS_LOW_AROUSAL] | [Physiological Incompatibility: Low Arousal vs Energetic Regulation] A state of low arousal (lethargy, passive slumping, fatigue) directly contradicts the physiological definition of being regulated (smiling, energetic, highly motivated).
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Physiological Incompatibility: Crisis vs Regulated] A client actively experiencing a medical crisis (e.g., active seizures, asthma) cannot simultaneously be physiologically regulated (happy, energetic, absence of medical/behavioral issues).
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_DISTRESS] | [Physiological Incompatibility: Distress vs Regulated] Active medical distress (illness, dental pain, ear infection) is physiologically incompatible with a fully regulated state (absence of issues, calm, happy, and energetic).
* EXC: [IS_PROJECTILE_BEHAVIOR] | [Behavioral State Contradiction] A clinical note cannot logically claim the client is completely regulated (e.g., displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior, calm and cooperative) while simultaneously documenting active projectile behaviors such as throwing or sweeping instructional materials.
* EXC: [IS_PROPERTY_DESTRUCTION] | [Behavioral State Contradiction] It is a logical and behavioral contradiction to state a client is actively regulated, cooperative, and displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior while also documenting property destruction such as breaking toys or ripping data sheets.
* EXC: [IS_RESISTANT_TRANSITION] | [Behavioral State Contradiction] A client cannot be accurately described as fully regulated (readily responding to instructions, happy and interactive) while simultaneously exhibiting resistant transitions such as dropping to the floor, whining, or attempting to elope.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Medical and Behavioral Contradiction] It is medically and behaviorally contradictory for a client to be completely regulated (calm, displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior) while actively experiencing acute respiratory distress or emitting high-pitched screaming due to medical issues.
* EXC: [IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical and Methodological Violation] It is an ethical violation and a Skinnerian methodological impossibility to apply restrictive punishments (e.g., response cost, tactical break, overcorrection) to a client who is actively regulated, cooperative, and devoid of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_OVERLOAD] | [Definitional Contradiction: Regulated State and Sensory Overload] A client cannot simultaneously experience acute sensory overload and be documented as completely calm, relaxed, and engaged.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Definitional Contradiction: Regulated State and Severe Topography] An absence of maladaptive behavior, which defines a regulated state, cannot logically co-occur with severe problem behaviors like head-butting or stripping completely naked.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | [Definitional Contradiction: Regulated State and SIB] Being fully regulated, calm, and cooperative contradicts the distress and active maladaptive engagement of self-injurious behavior.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Definitional Contradiction: Regulated State and Lethargy] A regulated state characterized as smiling, energetic, and highly interactive physically contradicts the lethargic presentation of slowed responses and passive slumping.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_DYSREGULATED] | [Definitional Contradiction: Regulated State and Dysregulation] A client cannot simultaneously be coded as presenting with dysregulation (crying, triggered) and exhibiting a calm, cooperative, regulated state.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Definitional Contradiction: Regulated State and Staff Aggression] Actively engaging in open-handed hitting or biting directed at a therapist strictly precludes the client from being documented as relaxed and regulated.
* EXC: [IS_TANGIBLE_MAINTAINED] | [Definitional Contradiction: Regulated State and Tangible Maintained Problem Behavior] A behavior identified as tangible-maintained (reacting to denied access) implies a maladaptive response occurred, contradicting the specific criteria for being regulated (absence of problem behavior).
* EXC: [IS_VOCAL_COMPROMISE] | [Physiological Impossibility: Regulated Affect vs Vocal Escalation] It is physiologically and definitionally impossible for a client to be calm, cooperative, and displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior while simultaneously screaming at a high pitch.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Extinction During Regulated State] A wait-out procedure involves withholding reinforcement/demands during noncompliance or escalation. It is methodologically invalid to apply this when a client is fully regulated, happy, and readily responding.

---

## [IS_REMOTE]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_ELECTRONIC_DEVICE] | [Physical/Environmental Requirement] Remote service delivery (e.g., Telehealth) fundamentally depends on an electronic device to transmit audio and video, making it a physical and environmental necessity.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_CAREGIVER_UNAVAILABLE] | [Ethical Violation: Telehealth Supervision Requirements] Conducting a remote telehealth session without a responsible adult or caregiver available for a minor or dependent client is a significant clinical safety and ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_CLINIC_SETTING] | [Physical Impossibility: Clinic vs Telehealth] An in-person clinical setting is physically and methodologically mutually exclusive from a remote telehealth session.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Physical Impossibility: Remote Physical Guidance] High effort punishment like positive practice overcorrection requires physical guidance or immediate proximity to enforce, which is physically impossible to conduct via remote telehealth.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Physical Impossibility: Staff-Directed Aggression via Telehealth] Imminent danger behaviors specifically directed at staff (biting therapist, dodging staff) are physically impossible during a remote telehealth session where staff are not physically present.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_CONTACT] | [Physical Impossibility: Remote Therapy Constraints] Telehealth sessions occur remotely, making physical contact (e.g., holding hands, deep pressure) between the therapist and the client physically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_CONTACT_WITH_THERAPIST] | [Physical Impossibility: Remote Therapy Constraints] Physical or aggressive contact directed specifically at the therapist (e.g., biting, swiping) is physically impossible when the therapist is providing remote/telehealth services.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION] | [Physical/Methodological Impossibility: Remote Crisis Management] Therapists cannot physically execute interventions, blocking, or tactical breaks remotely over Telehealth.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_PROMPT] | [Physical Impossibility: Remote Therapy Constraints] Physical prompting by the therapist (e.g., partial or full physical guidance) is physically impossible in a remote Telehealth session.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT] | [Physical/Methodological Impossibility: Remote Crisis Management] Physical safety management procedures, such as physically blocking a swallow or an exit, cannot be performed by a remote therapist via Telehealth.
* EXC: [IS_PROXIMITY_PAIRING] | [Physical Impossibility] Proximity pairing techniques that strictly require direct physical contact (e.g., holding the therapist's hand, physical touch, deep pressure squeezes, tickles) are physically impossible for a clinician to execute when delivering services remotely via telehealth.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Physical Impossibility: Remote Delivery and Physical Blocks] Remote telehealth therapy physically precludes the practitioner from directly implementing hands-on safety pads or physical blocking.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Physical Impossibility: Remote Delivery and Direct Staff Aggression] A client cannot successfully head-butt, bite, or swipe at a remote therapist over a telehealth screen.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | [Ethical Violation: Telehealth Privacy Breach] Observing or conducting toileting routines via a telehealth camera constitutes a severe violation of client privacy, dignity, and HIPAA/ethical guidelines.

---

## [IS_RESISTANT_TRANSITION]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_DYSREGULATED] | [Methodological Dependency] Resistant transitions involving vocal protests, dropping to the floor, or precursor behaviors methodologically represent a state of dysregulation in response to a demand.
* REQ: [IS_TRANSITION] | [Methodological Dependency: Transition Resistance] Resistant transition is a behavioral manifestation defined entirely by its occurrence during the process of transitioning. Therefore, it fundamentally and methodologically requires a transition event to take place.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Independent vs Resistant Transition] A client cannot transition smoothly and independently while simultaneously exhibiting a resistant transition (requiring physical guidance, whining, protesting).
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Methodological/Safety Mismatch: Crisis vs Transition Demands] A medical crisis requires immediate stabilization; attempting to enforce or measure structured transition demands (leading to resistant transitions) during a crisis is medically contraindicated.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Behavioral State Contradiction] A client cannot be accurately described as fully regulated (readily responding to instructions, happy and interactive) while simultaneously exhibiting resistant transitions such as dropping to the floor, whining, or attempting to elope.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Definitional Contradiction: Resistant Transition and Regulated State] Engaging in whining, dropping to the floor, or resisting transitions fundamentally contradicts the operational definition of a motivated, highly regulated transition.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Definitional Contradiction: Resistant Transition and Smooth Transition] A transition logically cannot be simultaneously completely smooth and cooperative while also being resistant, prolonged, and protested.
* EXC: [IS_SOCIAL_PLAY] | [Definitional Contradiction: Resistant Transition and Social Play] Attempting to elope back to a previous area or protesting transitions is incompatible with functionally sustaining cooperative play and turn-taking.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_RESISTANT_TRANSITION' forces 'IS_DYSREGULATED'. 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_DYSREGULATED' and 'IS_INDEPENDENT' cannot co-occur: [Definitional Contradiction: Dysregulated vs Independent] A client cannot be simultaneously dysregulated (e.g., crying, whining, visibly frustrated) and independent (demonstrating an absence of maladaptive behavior, transitioning smoothly and happily).

---

## [IS_RESPIRATORY_DEMAND]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AGGRESSION] | [Physical Mismatch: Aggression and Respiratory Demands] A client actively engaged in severe physical aggression (punching, kicking, biting) cannot concurrently engage in structured respiratory compliance demands such as slow, deep breathing.
* EXC: [IS_EDIBLE] | [Physical Safety: Choking Hazard with Respiratory Demands] Administering edibles while simultaneously requiring respiratory demands like deep breathing or blowing bubbles presents a high risk of aspiration and choking.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Physical/Methodological Impossibility: Deep Breathing During Severe Crisis] A client cannot physically or methodologically engage in stationary respiratory demands (e.g., deep breathing, blowing bubbles) while simultaneously actively bolting, punching, or engaging in severe SIB.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Physiological Impossibility/Safety: Crisis vs Respiratory Demand] Enforcing respiratory demands (like blowing bubbles or structured deep breathing) during a medical crisis (like an active seizure or severe dehydration) is physically inappropriate and unsafe.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Medical Harm Prevention] It is medically contraindicated and physically dangerous to impose respiratory demands (such as deep breathing exercises or blowing bubbles) on a client who is actively experiencing acute respiratory distress or an asthma attack.
* EXC: [IS_VOCAL_COMPROMISE] | [Physiological Impossibility: Deep Breathing While Screaming] A client cannot physiologically perform deep breathing exercises or blow bubbles while simultaneously engaging in high-pitched screaming or loud, sustained vocal protests.

---

## [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ACADEMIC] | [Ethical Violation: Academic Demands during Respiratory Crisis] Presenting structured academic demands (e.g., worksheets, counting) to a client in active respiratory distress is physically dangerous, medically contraindicated, and ethically prohibited.
* EXC: [IS_ADL_TARGET] | [Ethical Violation: ADL Demands during Respiratory Crisis] Mandating activities of daily living (such as brushing teeth or eating) during an active asthma attack or respiratory distress is physically hazardous and ethically forbidden.
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction during Respiratory Crisis] Implementing planned ignoring while a client is experiencing a life-threatening or acute medical crisis (asthma, severe respiratory distress) is physically dangerous and severely violates BACB ethical codes regarding client welfare.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_DEMAND] | [Ethical Violation: Auditory Demand during Respiratory Crisis] Placing clinical demands on a client to tolerate loud noises while they are experiencing an acute asthma or respiratory crisis is medically contraindicated and physically abusive.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | [Ethical Violation: Aversives during Respiratory Crisis] Applying aversive interventions (e.g., overcorrection, escape extinction) during a respiratory emergency is medically hazardous, physically dangerous, and strictly unethical.
* EXC: [IS_CHOKING_HAZARD] | [Medical Safety: Aspiration Risk] Exposing a client to choking hazards (such as small objects or kinetic sand) while they are experiencing active respiratory distress (e.g., asthma attacks) presents an acute medical risk of aspiration.
* EXC: [IS_EDIBLE] | [Physical Safety: Choking Hazard during Respiratory Distress] Administering edibles to a client experiencing acute respiratory distress such as an asthma attack presents a severe choking and aspiration hazard.
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | [Ethical Violation: Academic Demands During Medical Crisis] Placing educational or academic tutoring demands on a client actively experiencing a medical emergency like an asthma attack is an ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Escape Extinction During Medical Crisis] Utilizing escape extinction to block escape or force compliance during an active medical event like respiratory distress is a severe ethical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_EXECUTIVE_FUNCTIONING] | [Ethical Violation: Executive Functioning Demands During Medical Crisis] Imposing complex executive functioning tasks on a client experiencing active respiratory distress is ethically and medically contraindicated.
* EXC: [IS_FALL_HAZARD] | [Medical Contraindication: Fall Hazards During Respiratory Distress] Engaging a client in gross motor fall hazards, such as jumping or obstacle courses, during an active asthma attack or respiratory distress is medically unsafe.
* EXC: [IS_FINE_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Ethical Violation: Fine Motor Demands During Medical Crisis] Forcing fine motor educational targets, such as tracing letters or worksheets, during acute respiratory distress is medically and ethically inappropriate.
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Medical Contraindication: Exertion During Respiratory Distress] Targeting gross motor skills requiring physical exertion while a client is experiencing respiratory distress (e.g., asthma) poses a severe physical and medical risk.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Medical Contraindication: High Effort Punishment During Respiratory Distress] Enforcing high effort punishment (overcorrection) while a client is in respiratory distress (e.g., asthma attack) is medically dangerous and unethical.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EXERTION] | [Medical Contraindication: Exertion During Respiratory Distress] High exertion activities (e.g., jumping, running, obstacle courses) are medically contraindicated when a client is experiencing respiratory distress like asthma.
* EXC: [IS_INTENSIVE_TEACHING] | [Ethical Violation: Intensive Teaching during Respiratory Distress] Implementing highly demanding, intensive teaching methodologies (like DTT or fluency-based instruction) while a client is in respiratory distress is an ethical and safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_NET_TEACHING] | [Ethical Violation: NET during Respiratory Distress] Continuing instructional demands via Natural Environment Teaching (NET) while a client is experiencing acute respiratory distress compromises client safety and violates ethical care standards.
* EXC: [IS_NO_MEDICAL] | [Medical Contradiction: Health Status] Reporting that a client has 'None reported' for medical issues directly contradicts the documentation of 'Asthma / Respiratory issues' within the same session.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION] | [Medical/Ethical Violation: Restraint and Respiratory Compromise] Applying physical interventions, blocking, or tactical breaks on a client actively experiencing respiratory distress or asthma is extremely dangerous and medically contraindicated due to the high risk of positional asphyxiation.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT] | [Medical/Ethical Violation: Restraint and Respiratory Compromise] Physically blocking or holding a client for safety management while they are experiencing asthma or respiratory distress is medically contraindicated due to asphyxiation risks.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Medical and Behavioral Contradiction] It is medically and behaviorally contradictory for a client to be completely regulated (calm, displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior) while actively experiencing acute respiratory distress or emitting high-pitched screaming due to medical issues.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DEMAND] | [Medical Harm Prevention] It is medically contraindicated and physically dangerous to impose respiratory demands (such as deep breathing exercises or blowing bubbles) on a client who is actively experiencing acute respiratory distress or an asthma attack.
* EXC: [IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Harm Prevention] It is a severe ethical and medical violation to apply restrictive punishments (such as overcorrection or tactical breaks) to a client who is suffering from a medical emergency like asthma or active respiratory distress. Medical needs unequivocally supersede behavioral contingencies.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Medical Impossibility: Respiratory Distress and Regulated State] An acute respiratory event like an asthma attack or screaming at a high pitch is medically and behaviorally incompatible with being calm, relaxed, and energetic.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Medical Impossibility: Respiratory Distress and Smooth Transition] A client actively screaming at a high pitch or experiencing asthma-related respiratory distress cannot simultaneously execute a happy, smooth, unassisted transition.
* EXC: [IS_SOCIAL_PLAY] | [Medical Impossibility: Respiratory Distress and Social Play] Screaming at a high pitch or struggling with respiratory capacity physically prevents a client from appropriately engaging in turn-taking and sustained social interaction.
* EXC: [IS_VOCAL_VERBAL_TARGET] | [Medical Hazard: Forced Vocalizations During Respiratory Distress] Placing vocal demands (e.g., echoics, conversational turn-taking) on a client experiencing an asthma attack or respiratory distress directly exacerbates physical harm and restricts oxygen.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Medical Neglect: Withholding Intervention for Respiratory Distress] Using a wait-out procedure (withholding attention or intervention) during active respiratory distress such as an asthma attack constitutes medical neglect and presents a severe safety hazard.

---

## [IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Restrictive Punishment during Auditory Distress] Applying restrictive punishers or requiring prolonged tactical breaks for a client suffering from acute ear infection pain is abusive and violates medical/ethical boundaries.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Seclusion Risk] Implementing restrictive punishments (such as a tactical break or overcorrection) in a confined dark room constitutes seclusion, which is a severe ethical and legal violation under BACB guidelines.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_MAINTAINED] | [Methodological Mismatch: Timeout for Escape-Maintained Behavior] Applying a tactical break (exclusionary timeout) for an escape-maintained behavior is a methodological mismatch, as the timeout inadvertently provides the reinforcing escape.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_REINFORCER] | [Methodological Mismatch: Timeout as an Escape Reinforcer] When escape is the primary reinforcer, applying a restrictive punishment such as a tactical break provides access to escape, creating a methodological contradiction.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | [Methodological Contradiction: Extinction vs. Active Punishment] A behavior that is managed by ignoring it (extinction sensitive) methodologically contradicts the simultaneous application of active restrictive punishment like overcorrection or response cost.
