* EXC: [IS_GI_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Medical Symptoms] Applying restrictive punishment to a client experiencing a medical issue such as GI distress is an ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Punishing Independent Behavior] Applying restrictive punishment (tactical breaks, response cost) to a client who is behaving independently and appropriately without maladaptive behaviors is an ethical and methodological violation.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Medical Crisis] Applying restrictive punishment (e.g., overcorrection, tactical breaks, response cost) during a medical crisis (seizures, asthma attack, dehydration) is a severe ethical and health safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Medical Distress] It is a violation of behavioral ethics to apply restrictive punishment procedures to a client actively experiencing medical distress, illness, or pain.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Ethical and Methodological Violation] It is an ethical violation and a Skinnerian methodological impossibility to apply restrictive punishments (e.g., response cost, tactical break, overcorrection) to a client who is actively regulated, cooperative, and devoid of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Harm Prevention] It is a severe ethical and medical violation to apply restrictive punishments (such as overcorrection or tactical breaks) to a client who is suffering from a medical emergency like asthma or active respiratory distress. Medical needs unequivocally supersede behavioral contingencies.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Medical Distress] Applying restrictive punishments (like a tactical break or response cost) while a client is suffering from sensory or medical distress (such as a headache) is clinically abusive and ethically prohibited.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_OVERLOAD] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Sensory Overload] Applying restrictive punishments such as response cost or tactical breaks for an involuntary sensory overload is an ethical violation against compassionate care standards.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Physiological Lethargy] Using restrictive punishment to target yawning, fatigue, or biological symptoms of poor sleep violates medical and ethical safety protocols.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Regulated Behavior] Implementing overcorrection or tactical breaks when a client is highly motivated, making happy transitions, and demonstrating quick control violates basic Skinnerian behavioral principles and ethics.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Smooth Transitions] Applying punishment procedures immediately concurrent with independent, smooth, and happy transitions is ethically abusive and methodologically incorrect.
* EXC: [IS_SOCIAL_PLAY] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Social Play] Punishing a client while they are successfully initiating peer play or sharing items contradicts the fundamental goal of applied behavior analysis to reinforce prosocial skills.
* EXC: [IS_TACTILE_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Tactile Distress] Utilizing restrictive punishments like tactical breaks for uncontrollable medical/tactile issues such as rashes or skin irritation is a clear ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Biological Functions] Applying restrictive punishments such as overcorrection or response cost to toileting routines or accidents is developmentally inappropriate and violates ethical standards of compassionate care.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Active Punishment vs Passive Withholding] Implementing active restrictive punishments (like overcorrection) is methodologically incompatible with the simultaneous execution of a passive wait-out procedure.

---

## [IS_SCHOOL_SETTING]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_NON_CLINIC] | [Physical/Environmental Requirement: Educational Settings] Conducting therapy or tracking behaviors in an educational or school-based classroom fundamentally necessitates that the physical service delivery model is occurring in a non-clinic environment.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_CLINIC_SETTING] | [Physical Impossibility: Clinic vs School Setting] A specialized clinic facility is physically distinct from and mutually exclusive to a school or classroom setting.
* EXC: [IS_CONTAGIOUS] | [Medical Safety: School Transmission Risk] Bringing a contagious client into a school setting introduces a direct disease transmission risk to peers and staff, violating basic medical safety protocols.
* EXC: [IS_HOME_SETTING] | [Physical Impossibility: Simultaneous Distinct Locations] A client cannot physically be in a primary home setting (e.g., bedroom, home kitchen) and a primary school setting (e.g., classroom) at the same time.

---

## [IS_SENSORY_DISTRESS]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction during Sensory Distress] Using planned ignoring to address behaviors stemming directly from acute medical/physiological pain (such as a headache) violates ethical guidelines concerning the management of physiological needs.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_DEMAND] | [Ethical Violation: Auditory Demand during Sensory Distress] Forcing a client to tolerate loud environments while they are actively suffering from a headache exacerbates physiological pain, violating core ethical guidelines around medical care.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | [Ethical Violation: Aversives during Sensory Distress] Subjecting a client to aversive punishment procedures while they are actively suffering from medical pain (e.g., headache) is a severe ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Escape Extinction During Sensory Distress] Enforcing escape extinction (forcing compliance) during acute sensory distress, such as a severe headache, violates ethical guidelines regarding the humane treatment of medical symptoms.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Medical/Ethical Contraindication: High Effort Punishment During Sensory Distress] Forcing high effort physical punishment on a client experiencing sensory distress such as a severe headache is ethically and medically contraindicated.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EXERTION] | [Medical Contraindication: Exertion During Sensory Distress] Engaging in high exertion physical activities while a client is suffering from sensory distress (e.g., headache) is medically and ethically inappropriate.
* EXC: [IS_NO_MEDICAL] | [Medical Contradiction: Health Status] Reporting that a client has 'None reported' for medical issues directly contradicts the documentation of sensory distress such as a 'Headache'.
* EXC: [IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Medical Distress] Applying restrictive punishments (like a tactical break or response cost) while a client is suffering from sensory or medical distress (such as a headache) is clinically abusive and ethically prohibited.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Medical Neglect: Withholding Intervention for Sensory/Pain Distress] Applying a wait-out behavioral extinction procedure to a physiological medical symptom like a severe headache is medically negligent and ethically prohibited.

---

## [IS_SENSORY_OVERLOAD]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Ignoring Sensory Overload] Utilizing planned ignoring when a client is experiencing physiological sensory overload is ethically and medically contraindicated.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_DEMAND] | [Ethical Violation: Demands during Overload] Placing demands for a client to tolerate loud noises or environments during an active sensory overload violates ethical guidelines for harm prevention.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | [Ethical Violation: Aversives during Overload] Applying aversive procedures during active sensory overload is ethically abusive, as it penalizes physiological dysregulation.
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | [Ethical Violation: Tutoring During Sensory Overload] It is methodologically and ethically inappropriate to force structured academic tutoring tasks while a client is in a state of acute sensory overload.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Escape Extinction During Sensory Overload] Using escape extinction (forcing compliance) when a client is escaping due to genuine sensory overload is an ethical violation of neurodivergent affirming care.
* EXC: [IS_EXECUTIVE_FUNCTIONING] | [Cognitive Impossibility: Executive Functioning During Sensory Overload] A client experiencing acute sensory overload lacks the physiological regulation required to engage in high-level executive functioning and self-monitoring tasks.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction for Sensory Overload] Subjecting sensory overload to extinction (planned ignoring) is medically and ethically abusive, as sensory dysregulation requires accommodation or removal of the aversive stimulus.
* EXC: [IS_FINE_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Methodological Mismatch: Fine Motor During Sensory Overload] It is methodologically and ethically inappropriate to require precise fine motor skill execution while a client is suffering from acute sensory overload.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Sensory Distress] Applying high-effort punishment (e.g., positive practice overcorrection) to a client experiencing sensory overload violates ethical guidelines regarding trauma-informed care and harm prevention.
* EXC: [IS_INTENSIVE_TEACHING] | [Ethical Violation: Teaching During Sensory Overload] It is an ethical violation to implement intensive structured teaching (e.g., DTT, Max Prompting) while a client is actively experiencing sensory overload.
* EXC: [IS_NET_TEACHING] | [Ethical Violation: NET During Sensory Overload] Natural Environment Teaching demands should not be placed on a client actively suffering from sensory overload.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Definitional Contradiction: Regulated State and Sensory Overload] A client cannot simultaneously experience acute sensory overload and be documented as completely calm, relaxed, and engaged.
* EXC: [IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Sensory Overload] Applying restrictive punishments such as response cost or tactical breaks for an involuntary sensory overload is an ethical violation against compassionate care standards.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Safety Hazard / Physical Impossibility: Sharp Objects vs Sensory Overload] Sensory overload severely compromises attention and motor control, making the simultaneous use of sharp objects a major safety violation and methodological contradiction.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Contradictory States: Regulated vs Sensory Overload] Sensory overload represents a state of being neurologically overwhelmed, which prevents the calm, motivated engagement required for a regulated state.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Contradictory States: Smooth Transition vs Sensory Overload] The neurological distress of sensory overload physically and methodologically prevents a client from happily transitioning without resistance.

---

## [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Ignoring Severe Behavior] Ignoring severe topographies such as bolting, head-butting, or eye-gouging poses a direct threat to the client's physical safety and violates ethical mandates.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | [Ethical Violation: Aversives during Severe Topography] Implementing aversive procedures such as planned ignoring or escape extinction during severe topography (e.g., eye-gouging, bolting) is a severe safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_EDIBLE] | [Medical Safety Hazard: Edibles During Severe Topography] Administering food or edible reinforcers while a client is in the midst of severe topography (like bolting, head banging, or severe aggression) poses an acute aspiration and choking hazard.
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | [Physical Impossibility: Tutoring During Severe Topography] It is physically impossible to complete structured academic fine-motor tasks (like tracing or worksheets) while simultaneously engaging in severe topographies such as bolting, head-banging, or gouging eyes.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Extinction Burst in Severe Topography] Implementing escape extinction during severe, dangerous topographies (like bolting into the street or severe aggression) is clinically contraindicated due to the high risk of catastrophic harm during extinction bursts.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Unmonitored Severe Topography] Placing a client in an exclusionary timeout (removing proximity/supervision) while they engage in severe topographies (like swallowing objects or gouging eyes) is a severe medical safety hazard.
* EXC: [IS_EXECUTIVE_FUNCTIONING] | [Physical Impossibility: Executive Functioning During Severe Topography] High-level executive functioning tasks (like multi-step problem solving and self-monitoring) are cognitively and physically impossible to execute while engaging in acute severe topographies.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Ignoring Severe Topography] Ignoring (using extinction for) severe topographies like bolting into traffic or head-butting is clinically negligent and introduces severe risk of harm.
* EXC: [IS_FALL_HAZARD] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Gross Motor With Severe Topography] Prompting gross motor play or obstacle courses while a client is exhibiting severe topographies (like head banging or bolting) introduces chaotic movement that severely exacerbates the risk of physical injury.
* EXC: [IS_FINE_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Physical Impossibility: Fine Motor During Severe Topography] Executing precise fine motor targets (like pincer grasp or using a pencil) is physically impossible while the client is concurrently engaging in severe, dangerous topographies like bolting or smashing.
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Methodological Mismatch: Skill Acquisition During Severe Crisis] It is a safety violation and methodologically invalid to attempt teaching gross motor targets while the client is actively engaging in severe topographies like elopement, head-banging, or severe aggression.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Definitional Contradiction: Independence vs. Severe Topography] The 'Independent' category explicitly defines the client as displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior, which inherently contradicts engaging in severe topography behaviors like banging their head or running into the street.
* EXC: [IS_INTENSIVE_TEACHING] | [Ethical Violation: Teaching During Severe Topography] Intensive teaching demands must be suspended when a client is actively engaging in severe, dangerous topographies such as bolting, eye gouging, or head banging.
* EXC: [IS_INTERNAL_STATE_TARGET] | [Methodological Mismatch: Coping vs Severe Topography] A client actively engaging in severe problem behaviors (e.g., bolting, eye gouging) is fundamentally failing to cope or tolerate, making the successful demonstration of internal state targets impossible.
* EXC: [IS_LESSER_INTERVENTION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Lesser Intervention for Severe Crisis] Severe topographies like bolting or eye gouging require immediate physical crisis management, making lesser interventions like visual schedules or redirection methodologically invalid as a primary response.
* EXC: [IS_LESSER_PROMPT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting During Severe Behavior] Severe topographies require physical safety blocks, not lesser prompts like verbal or visual prompting.
* EXC: [IS_LOW_AROUSAL] | [Physical Impossibility: Low Arousal vs High-Energy Topography] A client cannot be physically fatigued and passively slumping while simultaneously engaging in high-energy severe topographies like bolting or kicking holes in walls.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Medical vs Operant Mismatch: Crisis and Severe Topography] A medical crisis (e.g., seizure, asthma attack) is an involuntary physiological event, which cannot be categorized alongside operant problem behaviors like bolting or smearing feces.
* EXC: [IS_NET_TEACHING] | [Ethical Violation: NET During Severe Topography] Natural Environment Teaching must be paused when a client is engaging in severe problem behaviors like bolting or eye gouging.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DEMAND] | [Safety Hazard: Oral Demand vs. Severe Topography] Placing oral demands (like feeding or brushing teeth) during episodes of severe behavioral topography (e.g., head banging, punching, kicking) creates an acute choking hazard and physical injury risk.
* EXC: [IS_PROXIMITY_PAIRING] | [Safety Hazard: Proximity Pairing during Severe Topography] Attempting physical touch, tickles, or close proximity pairing while a client is engaging in severe topographies (e.g., bolting, smearing feces) is a severe safety hazard and methodologically incorrect.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Definitional Contradiction: Regulated State and Severe Topography] An absence of maladaptive behavior, which defines a regulated state, cannot logically co-occur with severe problem behaviors like head-butting or stripping completely naked.
* EXC: [IS_REMOTE] | [Physical Impossibility: Remote Delivery and Physical Blocks] Remote telehealth therapy physically precludes the practitioner from directly implementing hands-on safety pads or physical blocking.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Safety Hazard / Physical Impossibility: Sharp Objects vs Severe Topography] It is both physically contradictory and a severe ethical/safety violation to expect a client to perform fine-motor tasks with scissors while they are actively exhibiting severe topographies such as bolting or physical destruction.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Physical Impossibility: Lethargy vs Severe Topography] The passive, low-energy state of lethargy (e.g., slumping in a chair) is physically incompatible with the explosive, high-energy output required for severe topographies like bolting or kicking holes in walls.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Contradictory States: Regulated vs Severe Topography] Being in a calm, quick-to-control, and happy state is physically and methodologically incompatible with exhibiting severe topographies such as bolting, kicking holes in walls, or smearing feces.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Contradictory Motor Execution: Smooth Transition vs Severe Topography] A client cannot happily and independently transition to a new area while concurrently bolting away, kicking furniture, or engaging in severe topographical resistance.
* EXC: [IS_SOCIAL_PLAY] | [Contradictory Contexts: Social Play vs Severe Topography] A client cannot sustain appropriate cooperative peer play while concurrently engaging in severe maladaptive behaviors such as bolting into the street or kicking holes in walls.
* EXC: [IS_VOCATIONAL] | [Methodological Mismatch: Vocational Tasks During Severe Topography] It is physically and methodologically impossible to safely and correctly implement structured vocational demands (e.g., assembly tasks, clocking in) while a client is actively engaged in severe, dangerous topography.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Ethical Violation: Wait Out Procedure During Severe Topography] Implementing a 'wait out' procedure for severe, dangerous topography (e.g., bolting, gouging eyes, smearing feces) is an ethical violation of the harm prevention code, requiring immediate active intervention rather than waiting.

---

## [IS_SHARP_OBJECT]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AGGRESSION] | [Safety Violation: Aggression and Sharp Objects] Permitting access to sharp objects like scissors while a client is actively aggressive poses an immediate, severe physical safety hazard.
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | [Safety Hazard: Acute Crisis Escalation] It is a profound safety and ethical violation to provide a client with sharp objects (e.g., scissors) while they are exhibiting active crisis indicators (e.g., agitated crying, requiring physical blocking), as this creates an immediate, high risk of severe self-injury or harm to others.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Safety Hazard: Visual Impairment] It is a critical physical safety hazard to provide or allow the use of sharp objects (e.g., scissors) in a darkened environment designed for sensory breaks, as reduced visibility drastically increases the risk of severe lacerations.
* EXC: [IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED] | [Safety Hazard: Weaponization Risk] If a client's behavior mandates a safety distance protocol to protect staff or peers, providing them with a sharp instructional object (such as scissors) fundamentally violates safety guidelines by potentially arming a dangerous individual.
* EXC: [IS_ELOPEMENT] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Elopement With Sharp Objects] A client actively eloping, bolting, or running away while holding a sharp object (such as scissors) presents a severe physical safety and laceration hazard.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Sharp Objects in Timeout] Permitting a client to possess sharp objects (like scissors) while serving an exclusionary timeout introduces an extreme risk of unmonitored self-harm or injury.
* EXC: [IS_FALL_HAZARD] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Sharp Objects During Gross Motor] Engaging in gross motor activities (like jumping or obstacle courses) while handling sharp objects (like scissors) presents a severe risk of laceration or puncture upon falling.
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Ethical Violation: Harm Prevention During Hand Compromise] A client actively engaging in hand compromise (biting or scratching own hands and arms) cannot safely handle sharp objects like scissors due to the severe risk of exacerbating the injury or causing accidental cuts.
* EXC: [IS_LOWER_BODY_DISROBING] | [Safety Hazard: Sharp Objects While Disrobed] It is a severe physical safety risk and ethical violation to provide sharp objects (e.g., scissors) to a client who is completely disrobed or in the process of stripping.
* EXC: [IS_LOW_AROUSAL] | [Safety Hazard: Sharp Objects and Low Arousal] It is physically and ethically unsafe to provide sharp objects to a client who is lethargic, passively slumping, or severely fatigued.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Safety Hazard: Sharp Objects During Medical Crisis] It is ethically and physically unsafe to have sharp objects present or actively used during an involuntary medical emergency such as a seizure.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DEMAND] | [Safety Hazard: Oral Demand vs. Sharp Objects] Conducting oral hygiene or feeding demands while a client is simultaneously holding or using sharp objects (e.g., scissors) poses an extreme physical hazard for accidental facial laceration or puncture.
* EXC: [IS_OUTDOOR_MATERIAL] | [Safety Hazard: Gross Motor Obstacles vs. Sharp Objects] Permitting a client to navigate gross motor obstacle courses or playground equipment while possessing sharp objects like scissors poses an extreme risk of severe puncture injuries or lacerations in the event of a fall.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_CONTACT] | [Safety Hazard: Physical Contact vs. Sharp Objects] Engaging in close physical contact, such as physical prompting, tickling, or deep pressure, while a client handles sharp objects like scissors creates an acute, immediate risk of accidental puncture or laceration.
* EXC: [IS_PICA] | [Safety Hazard: Pica vs. Sharp Objects] Allowing access to sharp objects (like scissors) for a client actively engaging in pica (mouthing or attempting to swallow non-edible items) creates a catastrophic risk of internal laceration and severe injury.
* EXC: [IS_PROXIMITY_PAIRING] | [Safety Hazard: Proximity Pairing with Sharp Objects] Providing physical touch, deep pressure, or tickles while a client is utilizing sharp objects like scissors presents an unacceptable physical safety hazard.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_OVERLOAD] | [Safety Hazard / Physical Impossibility: Sharp Objects vs Sensory Overload] Sensory overload severely compromises attention and motor control, making the simultaneous use of sharp objects a major safety violation and methodological contradiction.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Safety Hazard / Physical Impossibility: Sharp Objects vs Severe Topography] It is both physically contradictory and a severe ethical/safety violation to expect a client to perform fine-motor tasks with scissors while they are actively exhibiting severe topographies such as bolting or physical destruction.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Safety Hazard / Physical Impossibility: Sharp Objects vs Lethargy] Handling sharp objects requires alert fine-motor coordination, making it a severe safety risk and physical contradiction when a client is passively slumping and severely fatigued.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Safety Hazard / Physical Impossibility: Sharp Objects vs Staff Aggression] The focused execution required for cutting with scissors cannot physically co-occur safely with the active, gross-motor act of directing physical aggression toward a therapist.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | [Safety Violation: Sharp Objects During Toileting ADLs] Allowing a client to possess or use sharp objects (e.g., cutting with scissors) while actively engaging in a toileting routine or wiping independently is a direct safety hazard and physically contradictory.
* EXC: [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL] | [Safety Violation: Sharp Objects During Traffic Navigation] It is a profound safety violation and physical contradiction to have a client handle sharp objects (e.g., scissors) while simultaneously navigating parking lots, crossing streets, or actively engaging in traffic environments.
* EXC: [IS_VIBRATING_ENVIRONMENT] | [Safety Violation: Sharp Objects in Vibrating Environments] Utilizing sharp objects for fine motor tasks (e.g., cutting with scissors) while in a vibrating or moving environment (e.g., vehicle or transport) presents a severe and physically contradictory safety hazard.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Safety Violation: Sharp Objects During Dysregulation Wait Out] Employing a 'wait out' procedure while a client is in possession of sharp objects (e.g., scissors) poses an immediate physical safety threat, as dysregulated behavior with sharp tools must be actively managed and disarmed.
* EXC: [IS_WATER_ENVIRONMENT] | [Safety Violation: Sharp Objects in Water Environments] Engaging in fine motor tasks with sharp objects (e.g., scissors) while interacting in wet environments (e.g., water play, washing hands, brushing teeth) is an unsafe physical mismatch due to lack of stable, dry task surfaces and slippage risks.

---

## [IS_SIB]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Ethical/Safety Requirement: SIB] Self-injurious behavior poses a direct, immediate physical threat to the client's health, strictly necessitating clinical and ethical handling under an imminent danger protocol.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ADL_TARGET] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SIB' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_ADL_TARGET' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Ignoring SIB] Planned ignoring cannot be safely or ethically used for Self-Injurious Behavior due to the immediate physical risk of harm to the client.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | [Ethical Violation: Aversives for SIB] Utilizing aversive procedures like planned ignoring or escape extinction for self-injurious behavior is ethically contraindicated due to immediate safety risks.
* EXC: [IS_CHAINING_FORMAT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SIB' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' forces 'IS_CHAINED'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINED' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching ADLs During Crisis] It is methodologically invalid and physically unsafe to conduct chained daily living activities (e.g., tying shoes, hygiene routines) while a client is in imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_EDIBLE] | [Medical Safety Hazard: Edibles During SIB] Administering edibles during active Self-Injurious Behavior introduces a high risk of choking or aspiration due to unpredictable, violent physical movements.
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SIB' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING' forces 'IS_ACADEMIC'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_ACADEMIC' cannot co-occur: [Safety Violation: Academics during Crisis] Implementing academic demands like counting or tracing while a client is in imminent danger (bolting, severe self-injury) is a severe safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Extinction During SIB] Using escape extinction (blocking escape and maintaining demands) in the presence of Self-Injurious Behavior (SIB) is ethically prohibited and medically dangerous.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Unmonitored SIB] Utilizing exclusionary timeout during self-injurious behavior removes the practitioner's ability to physically block the SIB, creating an acute physical safety hazard.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Ignoring SIB] It is an ethical and safety violation to apply extinction (ignoring) to active self-injurious behavior (SIB), as it requires immediate physical intervention.
* EXC: [IS_FALL_HAZARD] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Gross Motor With SIB] Engaging in dynamic gross motor activities (like swinging or jumping) while actively committing self-injurious behavior drastically increases the risk of catastrophic fall and injury.
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Methodological Mismatch: Skill Acquisition During SIB] Teaching gross motor targets is strictly contraindicated while the client is actively engaging in self-injurious behavior.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Definitional Contradiction: Independence vs. SIB] Self-Injurious Behavior is maladaptive, which definitionally contradicts the 'Independent' state that strictly requires displaying an absence of maladaptive behaviors.
* EXC: [IS_INTENSIVE_TEACHING] | [Ethical Violation: Teaching During SIB] Intensive teaching and maximum prompting must be ethically paused during episodes of severe self-injurious behavior.
* EXC: [IS_INTERNAL_STATE_TARGET] | [Methodological Mismatch: Coping vs SIB] A client actively engaging in severe self-injurious behavior is failing to successfully cope, accept 'no', or tolerate demands.
* EXC: [IS_LESSER_INTERVENTION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Lesser Intervention for SIB] Severe self-injurious behavior requires immediate physical blocking, not lesser interventions like stimulus fading or visual schedules.
* EXC: [IS_LESSER_PROMPT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting During SIB] Severe self-injury requires physical safety blocks, not lesser prompts like verbal or visual prompting.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Medical vs Operant Mismatch: Crisis and SIB] A medical crisis such as a seizure involves involuntary movements that cannot be categorized as operant self-injurious behavior.
* EXC: [IS_NET_TEACHING] | [Ethical Violation: NET During SIB] Natural Environment Teaching must be suspended when a client is engaging in severe self-injurious behavior.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DEMAND] | [Safety Hazard: Oral Demand vs. SIB] Delivering oral demands (e.g., feeding or brushing teeth) while a client is actively engaging in self-injurious behavior (such as head banging or biting themselves) presents an acute aspiration, choking, and traumatic injury hazard.
* EXC: [IS_PROXIMITY_PAIRING] | [Safety Hazard: Proximity Pairing during SIB] Utilizing proximity pairing (e.g., tickles, hand-holding) while a client is actively engaging in dangerous SIB (e.g., eye gouging) is unsafe and would inadvertently reinforce the self-injury.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Definitional Contradiction: Regulated State and SIB] Being fully regulated, calm, and cooperative contradicts the distress and active maladaptive engagement of self-injurious behavior.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Contradictory States: Regulated vs SIB] A client cannot be highly motivated and happily transitioning while actively engaging in severe self-injurious behaviors like banging their head against the wall.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Contradictory Motor Execution: Smooth Transition vs SIB] Engaging in self-injurious behavior fundamentally disrupts physical transitions, making it impossible for the transition to be smooth and resistance-free.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SIB' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_INDEPENDENT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Independence] Active states of imminent danger (severe aggression, SIB, elopement) strictly contradict the definition of independence, which mandates safe navigation and a complete absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SIB' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_TOILETING_ADL' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_VOCATIONAL] | [Methodological Mismatch: Vocational Tasks During SIB] Structured vocational work demands (e.g., assembly, sorting) are physically incompatible and methodologically contraindicated when a client is experiencing active self-injurious episodes.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Ethical Violation: Wait Out Procedure During SIB] It is an ethical violation of harm prevention protocols to apply a 'wait out' procedure while a client is actively engaged in self-injurious behavior (e.g., banging head, gouging eyes), which demands immediate safety intervention.

---

## [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_EDIBLE] | [Medical Safety Hazard: Edibles During Lethargy] Administering edible reinforcers or food while a client is extremely lethargic, exhibiting slowed responses, or passively slumping poses a serious physiological aspiration and choking hazard.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Escape Extinction During Lethargy] Forcing compliance via escape extinction when a client is visibly lethargic or fatigued due to poor sleep ignores biological necessities and is an ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Ethical Violation: Timeout for Lethargy] Using exclusionary timeout as a consequence for behaviors rooted in severe lethargy or sleep deprivation is medically inappropriate and unethical.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction for Lethargy] Using extinction to ignore behaviors resulting from genuine physiological fatigue or sleep deprivation is an ethical violation of client care.
* EXC: [IS_FALL_HAZARD] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Gross Motor During Lethargy] Permitting or prompting a fatigued, lethargic, or slumping client to engage in gross motor fall hazards (like therapy balls or jumping) poses a significant risk of physical injury due to compromised motor control.
* EXC: [IS_FINE_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Fine Motor With Lethargy] Requiring a significantly lethargic or slumping client to execute fine motor tasks, particularly those involving sharp objects like scissors, introduces a high physical injury risk.
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Methodological Mismatch: Motor Demands During Lethargy] It is methodologically and physically inappropriate to demand effortful gross motor targets (jumping, catching a ball) from a client who is lethargic, fatigued, and passively slumping.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_AROUSAL] | [Physiological Impossibility: High Arousal vs. Lethargy] High arousal states (high rate of movement, pacing rapidly, hyperactivity) are physiologically incompatible with sleep lethargy (slowed response times, passively slumping, severe fatigue).
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Physical Exhaustion] Mandating high-effort punishment such as overcorrection when a client is lethargic or physically exhausted is medically contraindicated and abusive.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EXERTION] | [Physiological Impossibility: Exertion vs. Lethargy] High exertion activities such as running, jumping, and completing obstacle courses cannot be physically performed by a client who is exhibiting sleep lethargy, fatigue, or passively slumping.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Physiological Impossibility: Active Crisis vs. Lethargy] Imminent danger topographies (running, punching, bolting, thrashing) require a high level of kinetic energy and are physiologically incompatible with passively slumping in a chair and severe lethargy.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Physiological Impossibility: Independence vs. Sleep Lethargy] Independence that involves fluency, transitioning smoothly, and happily running to designated areas is physically and methodologically precluded by lethargy, slowed responses, and passively slumping.
* EXC: [IS_INTENSIVE_TEACHING] | [Ethical Violation: Teaching a Sleeping/Lethargic Client] It is ethically and methodologically impossible to conduct intensive structured teaching (DTT/Fluency) on a client who is sleeping, passively slumping, or extremely lethargic.
* EXC: [IS_INTERNAL_STATE_TARGET] | [Physical Impossibility: Active Coping While Asleep] A client who is sleeping or highly lethargic cannot actively demonstrate internal state targets such as accepting changes, using deep breathing, or tolerating loud environments.
* EXC: [IS_NET_TEACHING] | [Ethical Violation: NET on Sleeping Client] It is practically and ethically impossible to conduct Natural Environment Teaching on a client who is actively sleeping or heavily lethargic.
* EXC: [IS_NO_MEDICAL] | [Medical Status Contradiction: Sleep / Physiological] Lethargy due to poor sleep represents a physiological/medical setting event. Claiming "None reported" for medical status contradicts the presence of this sleep-related physiological issue.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DEMAND] | [Safety Hazard: Oral Demand vs. Sleep Lethargy] Presenting oral demands, such as eating a variety of foods or brushing teeth, to a client who is severely lethargic, yawning, or passively slumping presents a critical risk of aspiration and choking.
* EXC: [IS_OUTDOOR_MATERIAL] | [Safety Hazard: Gross Motor Obstacles vs. Sleep Lethargy] Directing a client to navigate gross motor obstacle courses or playground equipment while they are passively slumping or severely lethargic poses a severe fall and physical injury risk.
* EXC: [IS_PROPERTY_DESTRUCTION] | [Physical Impossibility: Property Destruction and Lethargy] High-energy destructive behaviors, such as kicking holes in walls or overturning tables, physically contradict a physiological state of lethargy, yawning, and passively slumping.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Definitional Contradiction: Regulated State and Lethargy] A regulated state characterized as smiling, energetic, and highly interactive physically contradicts the lethargic presentation of slowed responses and passive slumping.
* EXC: [IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Physiological Lethargy] Using restrictive punishment to target yawning, fatigue, or biological symptoms of poor sleep violates medical and ethical safety protocols.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Physical Impossibility: Lethargy vs Severe Topography] The passive, low-energy state of lethargy (e.g., slumping in a chair) is physically incompatible with the explosive, high-energy output required for severe topographies like bolting or kicking holes in walls.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Safety Hazard / Physical Impossibility: Sharp Objects vs Lethargy] Handling sharp objects requires alert fine-motor coordination, making it a severe safety risk and physical contradiction when a client is passively slumping and severely fatigued.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Contradictory States: Regulated vs Lethargy] The low-energy, passive slumping associated with lethargy mutually excludes the active, highly motivated posture required for a regulated state.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Contradictory Motor Execution: Smooth Transition vs Lethargy] Passively slumping or exhibiting severely slowed, fatigued responses is physically incompatible with actively and happily running to the next area.
* EXC: [IS_SOCIAL_PLAY] | [Physical Impossibility: Lethargy vs Social Play] Sustaining cooperative play and initiating peer interaction require an alert, active state that physically contradicts passively slumping or sleeping.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Physical Impossibility: Lethargy vs Staff Aggression] Severe fatigue and passively slumping contradict the deliberate, forceful gross-motor acts of punching or head-butting staff.

---

## [IS_SLIP_HAZARD]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_FALL_HAZARD] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Slip Risk During Gross Motor] Combining gross motor jumping or running obstacle courses with slip hazard environments (such as wet bathroom floors or water play tables) drastically elevates the risk of slip-and-fall injuries.

---

## [IS_SMART_CHIP_DYSREGULATED]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Independence vs. Dysregulation] A client who is actively dysregulated (e.g., crying, visibly triggered by loud noises or routine changes) is not displaying the smooth, independent, and happily engaged state defined by the Independent category.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Definitional Contradiction: Regulated State and Dysregulation] A client cannot simultaneously be coded as presenting with dysregulation (crying, triggered) and exhibiting a calm, cooperative, regulated state.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Contradictory States: Regulated vs Dysregulated] A client cannot be simultaneously in a highly motivated, cooperative, and regulated state while also experiencing dysregulation (e.g., crying, reacting negatively to loud noises).
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Contradictory Motor and Emotional States: Smooth Transition vs Dysregulation] Transitioning smoothly and happily without resistance is physically incompatible with crying or reacting poorly to a routine change.

---

## [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AGGRESSION] | [State Contradiction: Aggression and Regulation] Active aggression directly contradicts states of being highly regulated, happily paired, or exhibiting quick instructional control.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_SENSITIVITY] | [Antecedent Contradiction: Auditory Sensitivity and Regulation] Being triggered into dysregulation by a loud noise contradicts an ongoing state of happy transition and extended pairing.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | [Methodological Contradiction: Aversives and Regulation] Implementing aversive procedures such as overcorrection or response cost is physically contradictory to a state of quick control and happy transition.
* EXC: [IS_BIO_HAZARD] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Regulated vs Severe Bio-Hazard] Severe biological hazards, such as fecal smearing, pica, and stripping completely naked, are indicators of extreme dysregulation that logically cannot co-occur with a state of being highly motivated, cooperative, and demonstrating quick instructional control.
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Crisis vs Regulation] A client requiring active crisis intervention (e.g., physical blocking, tactical breaks, agitated crying) is in an acute state of distress, which physically precludes them from simultaneously being categorized as happily regulated or under quick instructional control.
* EXC: [IS_DYSREGULATED] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Dysregulation vs Regulation] A client cannot be simultaneously dysregulated (agitated, crying, displaying precursors) and completely regulated (happy, highly motivated, under quick control).
* EXC: [IS_ELOPEMENT] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Elopement vs Regulation] Actively eloping (bolting, hiding, fleeing) is mutually exclusive with being happily paired, regulated, and engaged with the therapist.
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Self-Injury vs. Regulated State] Active self-injury such as biting one's own hand is a clear indicator of distress or dysregulation, completely contradicting the fully regulated, happy, and highly motivated state defined by the Smart Chip Regulated category.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Punishing Regulated Behavior] Applying positive practice overcorrection is methodologically incompatible with a state of being fully regulated, happy, cooperative, and highly motivated.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Crisis vs. Motivation] A client cannot simultaneously present imminent danger (e.g., elopement, severe aggression, self-injury) while being 'Highly Motivated', 'Happy', and demonstrating 'Quick Control'.
* EXC: [IS_LOW_AROUSAL] | [Clinical Contradiction: Regulated vs Low Arousal] A client who is withdrawn, fatigued, and passively slumping contradicts the operational definition of being highly motivated, happy, and regulated.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Clinical Contradiction: Regulated During Medical Crisis] A client experiencing a medical emergency (e.g., seizure, severe dehydration) is in physiological distress and cannot be categorized as highly motivated, happy, or regulated.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_CONTACT_WITH_THERAPIST] | [Clinical State Contradiction: Aggression vs. Regulated Status] Active physical aggression towards a therapist (e.g., head-butting, biting) is a clear indicator of distress and dysregulation, contradicting clinical states of being highly motivated, regulated, or having a happy transition.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION] | [Clinical State Contradiction: Physical Intervention vs. Regulated Status] Implementing a tactical break or physical blocking addresses severe behavioral escalation and danger, which contradicts a regulated state characterized by being highly motivated or transitioning happily.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT] | [Clinical State Contradiction: Safety Management vs. Regulated Status] The necessity of physical safety management (e.g., blocking an exit, using safety pads) indicates an acute behavioral crisis, which is mutually exclusive with a state of being behaviorally regulated, highly motivated, and engaged in a happy transition.
* EXC: [IS_PROJECTILE_BEHAVIOR] | [Methodological Mismatch: Projectile Behavior and Regulated State] Throwing instructional materials is a maladaptive behavior that directly contradicts being highly regulated, compliant, and displaying an absence of problem behavior.
* EXC: [IS_PROPERTY_DESTRUCTION] | [Definitional Contradiction: Property Destruction and Regulated State] Property destruction is a severe maladaptive behavior that is mutually exclusive with a highly motivated, happy, and regulated state.
* EXC: [IS_RESISTANT_TRANSITION] | [Definitional Contradiction: Resistant Transition and Regulated State] Engaging in whining, dropping to the floor, or resisting transitions fundamentally contradicts the operational definition of a motivated, highly regulated transition.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Medical Impossibility: Respiratory Distress and Regulated State] An acute respiratory event like an asthma attack or screaming at a high pitch is medically and behaviorally incompatible with being calm, relaxed, and energetic.
* EXC: [IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Regulated Behavior] Implementing overcorrection or tactical breaks when a client is highly motivated, making happy transitions, and demonstrating quick control violates basic Skinnerian behavioral principles and ethics.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_OVERLOAD] | [Contradictory States: Regulated vs Sensory Overload] Sensory overload represents a state of being neurologically overwhelmed, which prevents the calm, motivated engagement required for a regulated state.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Contradictory States: Regulated vs Severe Topography] Being in a calm, quick-to-control, and happy state is physically and methodologically incompatible with exhibiting severe topographies such as bolting, kicking holes in walls, or smearing feces.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | [Contradictory States: Regulated vs SIB] A client cannot be highly motivated and happily transitioning while actively engaging in severe self-injurious behaviors like banging their head against the wall.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Contradictory States: Regulated vs Lethargy] The low-energy, passive slumping associated with lethargy mutually excludes the active, highly motivated posture required for a regulated state.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_DYSREGULATED] | [Contradictory States: Regulated vs Dysregulated] A client cannot be simultaneously in a highly motivated, cooperative, and regulated state while also experiencing dysregulation (e.g., crying, reacting negatively to loud noises).
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Contradictory States: Regulated vs Staff Aggression] Directing physical aggression (punching, head-butting) at a therapist fundamentally contradicts the compliant, motivated parameters defining a regulated state.
* EXC: [IS_TANGIBLE_MAINTAINED] | [Functional Contradiction: Regulated vs Tangible Maintained Problem Behavior] A client exhibiting happy, motivated transitions cannot simultaneously be engaging in maladaptive behaviors triggered and maintained by denied access or interruption of preferred activities.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Wait Out Procedure During Regulated States] A 'wait out' procedure is inherently designed as a consequence for maladaptive behaviors or non-compliance. Applying it while a client is highly motivated, happily transitioning, and regulated represents a fundamental methodological contradiction.

---

## [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_REGULATED] | [Clinical Dependency of Smooth Transitions] A smooth, independent, or unresisting transition clinically relies on the individual being in a baseline state of regulation and cooperation.
* REQ: [IS_TRANSITION] | [Methodological Dependency] A smooth transition describes the qualitative success of a client shifting between activities or locations; therefore, it conceptually and physically requires a transition event to take place.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AGGRESSION] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Aggression and Transitions] A client engaging in active aggression cannot simultaneously transition smoothly and happily without resistance.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_SENSITIVITY] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Sensitivity and Smooth Transitions] A client triggered into dysregulation by loud noises cannot simultaneously transition smoothly and happily without resistance.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Aversives and Transitions] Aversives like overcorrection or escape extinction imply resistance or problem behavior, physically contradicting a smooth transition without resistance.
* EXC: [IS_BIO_HAZARD] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Smooth Transition vs Bio-Hazard] Engaging in extreme bio-hazardous behaviors (spitting at staff, smearing feces, disrobing) inherently involves severe non-compliance and physically contradicts transitioning smoothly, happily, and without resistance.
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Crisis vs Smooth Transition] Active crisis indicators, such as physical blocking to prevent elopement or agitated crying, represent acute resistance and distress, making it physically impossible to simultaneously transition smoothly and independently without resistance.
* EXC: [IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION' forces 'IS_REGULATED'. 'IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_REGULATED' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Behavioral Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Regulated State] A client actively engaging in imminent danger (aggression, elopement, SIB) cannot be described as calm, regulated, or displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_DYSREGULATED] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Dysregulation vs Smooth Transition] A smooth, happy, and independent transition is physically contradictory to a transition characterized by dysregulation, whining, and precursor behaviors.
* EXC: [IS_ELOPEMENT] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Elopement vs Smooth Transition] Eloping or bolting from the area is the behavioral opposite of a smooth, independent, and compliant transition.
* EXC: [IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION' forces 'IS_REGULATED'. 'IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_REGULATED' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Behavioral Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Regulated State] A client actively engaging in imminent danger (aggression, elopement, SIB) cannot be described as calm, regulated, or displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Escape Extinction vs Smooth Transition] Escape extinction cannot be applied to a smooth, cooperative, and independent transition, as there is no escape-maintained behavior occurring to extinguish.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION' forces 'IS_REGULATED'. 'IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT' forces 'IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT'. 'IS_REGULATED' and 'IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT' cannot co-occur: [Ethical and Methodological Violation] It is an ethical violation and a Skinnerian methodological impossibility to apply restrictive punishments (e.g., response cost, tactical break, overcorrection) to a client who is actively regulated, cooperative, and devoid of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION' forces 'IS_REGULATED'. 'IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE' forces 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION'. 'IS_REGULATED' and 'IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Extinction on Regulated Client] Attention extinction (planned ignoring) targets active maladaptive behaviors. Applying it when a client is regulated, cooperative, and displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior is a fundamental methodological error.
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Self-Injury vs. Smooth Transition] A client actively engaging in self-injurious behaviors directed at their hands or arms is in distress, which inherently contradicts transitioning smoothly, independently, and happily to a new area.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Punishing Smooth Transitions] It is methodologically contradictory to apply high-effort punishment during a smooth, independent, and happy transition, as there is no maladaptive behavior occurring to warrant a corrective procedure.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Crisis vs. Smooth Transition] Imminent danger behaviors such as bolting into parking lots, severe aggression, or self-injury completely disrupt and physically preclude a smooth, independent transition.
* EXC: [IS_LOWER_BODY_DISROBING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Disrobing During Smooth Transition] Stripping naked or removing pants is indicative of either an accident, problem behavior, or a toileting routine, which contradicts the definition of happily and smoothly transitioning between general areas.
* EXC: [IS_LOW_AROUSAL] | [Physical Impossibility: Smooth Transition While Slumping] A client who is passively slumping, sluggish, and physically fatigued cannot simultaneously be happily running or transitioning smoothly without resistance.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Physical Impossibility: Smooth Transition During Medical Crisis] An active medical crisis (seizure, asthma attack) prevents any form of smooth or happy physical transition to a new area.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_CONTACT_WITH_THERAPIST] | [Physical Impossibility: Therapist Directed Aggression vs. Smooth Transition] Directing aggression at a therapist (e.g., biting, head-butting, swiping) indicates significant resistance and distress, rendering a smooth, happy, and independent transition physically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION] | [Physical Impossibility: Physical Intervention vs. Smooth Transition] Requiring a tactical break or physical blocking implies severe behavioral resistance, which directly contradicts transitioning smoothly and independently without resistance.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT] | [Physical Impossibility: Safety Management vs. Smooth Transition] A transition characterized as smooth, independent, and without resistance cannot physically co-occur with an acute behavioral crisis requiring physical safety management, such as blocking an exit or using safety pads.
* EXC: [IS_PICA] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION' forces 'IS_REGULATED'. 'IS_PICA' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_REGULATED' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Behavioral Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Regulated State] A client actively engaging in imminent danger (aggression, elopement, SIB) cannot be described as calm, regulated, or displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_PROJECTILE_BEHAVIOR] | [Methodological Mismatch: Projectile Behavior and Smooth Transition] A smooth, happy, and independent transition is physically and methodologically incompatible with the disruption of throwing items and sweeping materials off a desk.
* EXC: [IS_PROPERTY_DESTRUCTION] | [Definitional Contradiction: Property Destruction and Smooth Transition] Successfully and smoothly transitioning to a new area cannot co-occur with violent resistance such as breaking toys or slamming doors.
* EXC: [IS_RESISTANT_TRANSITION] | [Definitional Contradiction: Resistant Transition and Smooth Transition] A transition logically cannot be simultaneously completely smooth and cooperative while also being resistant, prolonged, and protested.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Medical Impossibility: Respiratory Distress and Smooth Transition] A client actively screaming at a high pitch or experiencing asthma-related respiratory distress cannot simultaneously execute a happy, smooth, unassisted transition.
* EXC: [IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Smooth Transitions] Applying punishment procedures immediately concurrent with independent, smooth, and happy transitions is ethically abusive and methodologically incorrect.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_OVERLOAD] | [Contradictory States: Smooth Transition vs Sensory Overload] The neurological distress of sensory overload physically and methodologically prevents a client from happily transitioning without resistance.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Contradictory Motor Execution: Smooth Transition vs Severe Topography] A client cannot happily and independently transition to a new area while concurrently bolting away, kicking furniture, or engaging in severe topographical resistance.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | [Contradictory Motor Execution: Smooth Transition vs SIB] Engaging in self-injurious behavior fundamentally disrupts physical transitions, making it impossible for the transition to be smooth and resistance-free.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Contradictory Motor Execution: Smooth Transition vs Lethargy] Passively slumping or exhibiting severely slowed, fatigued responses is physically incompatible with actively and happily running to the next area.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_DYSREGULATED] | [Contradictory Motor and Emotional States: Smooth Transition vs Dysregulation] Transitioning smoothly and happily without resistance is physically incompatible with crying or reacting poorly to a routine change.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Contradictory Motor Execution: Smooth Transition vs Staff Aggression] Actively hitting or biting staff prevents a transition from occurring smoothly and independently.
* EXC: [IS_TANGIBLE_MAINTAINED] | [Functional Contradiction: Smooth Transition vs Tangible Maintained Problem Behavior] Transitions that occur smoothly and without resistance cannot be concurrently associated with behaviors maintained by the interruption of a preferred activity.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION' forces 'IS_REGULATED'. 'IS_TOILETING_ADL' forces 'IS_LOWER_BODY_DISROBING'. 'IS_REGULATED' and 'IS_LOWER_BODY_DISROBING' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Contradiction: Disrobing vs Regulated State] Lower body disrobing is an acute maladaptive behavior, which is physically and behaviorally incompatible with displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior or being fully regulated.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Wait Out Procedure for Smooth Transitions] It is methodologically illogical to utilize a 'wait out' procedure for a transition that is explicitly described as smooth, happy, and completed without resistance.

---

## [IS_SOCIAL_PLAY]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_PEER_OR_SIBLING] | [Physical/Environmental Requirement for Social Play] Social play targets and interactions (such as cooperative play, sharing items, or turn-taking with a peer) physically and methodologically depend on the presence and participation of a peer or sibling to be performed.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Ignoring during Social Play] Implementing planned ignoring is methodologically incompatible with facilitating and reinforcing interactive social play and peer turn-taking.
* EXC: [IS_CONTAGIOUS] | [Ethical Violation: Infection Control] It is a severe ethical and health safety violation to facilitate close-proximity social play, sharing of items, and peer interaction when a client is actively presenting contagious illness symptoms.
* EXC: [IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED] | [Physical Contradiction: Proximity Requirements] Social play requires physical proximity to peers for sharing items and turn-taking, which strictly contradicts an active behavioral protocol requiring staff or peers to maintain a safety distance due to the threat of aggression or severe behavior.
* EXC: [IS_ELOPEMENT] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Elopement vs Social Play] Active elopement (bolting, fleeing from staff) is behaviorally incompatible with sustaining seated, structured, or cooperative peer social play.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Timeout During Cooperative Play] A client cannot be concurrently serving an exclusionary timeout (removed from the environment) and actively participating in cooperative social play with peers.
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Self-Injury vs. Cooperative Play] A client actively biting their own arms or engaging in self-injurious scratching cannot simultaneously sustain cooperative, positive social play and peer interaction.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Punishing Prosocial Behavior] Applying high-effort punishment is methodologically incompatible with the client actively engaging in positive, cooperative social play and sharing items with peers.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Behavioral Contradiction: Crisis vs. Social Play] Imminent danger behaviors (such as punching, biting, running into the street) preclude the ability to safely engage in cooperative social play and turn-taking with peers.
* EXC: [IS_LOWER_BODY_DISROBING] | [Ethical Violation: Disrobing During Peer Play] It is an ethical and social violation to allow a client to strip completely naked while engaged in cooperative social play with peers.
* EXC: [IS_LOW_AROUSAL] | [Physical Impossibility: Social Play While Withdrawn] A client who is passively slumping, lethargic, and withdrawn cannot actively initiate and sustain cooperative peer play.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Physical Impossibility: Social Play During Medical Crisis] A client experiencing an active medical emergency cannot engage in cooperative social peer play.
* EXC: [IS_PROJECTILE_BEHAVIOR] | [Methodological Mismatch: Projectile Behavior and Social Play] Throwing instructional materials disrupts the environment and contradicts the sustained cooperative engagement and turn-taking required for functional social play.
* EXC: [IS_PROPERTY_DESTRUCTION] | [Definitional Contradiction: Property Destruction and Social Play] Engaging in property destruction, such as breaking toys, is incompatible with the positive peer interaction and sharing required for social play.
* EXC: [IS_RESISTANT_TRANSITION] | [Definitional Contradiction: Resistant Transition and Social Play] Attempting to elope back to a previous area or protesting transitions is incompatible with functionally sustaining cooperative play and turn-taking.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Medical Impossibility: Respiratory Distress and Social Play] Screaming at a high pitch or struggling with respiratory capacity physically prevents a client from appropriately engaging in turn-taking and sustained social interaction.
* EXC: [IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Social Play] Punishing a client while they are successfully initiating peer play or sharing items contradicts the fundamental goal of applied behavior analysis to reinforce prosocial skills.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Contradictory Contexts: Social Play vs Severe Topography] A client cannot sustain appropriate cooperative peer play while concurrently engaging in severe maladaptive behaviors such as bolting into the street or kicking holes in walls.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Physical Impossibility: Lethargy vs Social Play] Sustaining cooperative play and initiating peer interaction require an alert, active state that physically contradicts passively slumping or sleeping.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Contradictory Contexts: Social Play vs Staff Aggression] Engaging in appropriate turn-taking or sharing with a peer is methodologically and physically incompatible with actively punching or biting a therapist.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Wait Out Procedure During Social Play] A 'wait out' procedure is applied to manage maladaptive behavior. It is methodologically invalid to apply this strategy while the client is successfully engaged in appropriate peer interaction and sustained cooperative social play.

---

## [IS_SPONTANEOUS]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Methodological Dependency: Spontaneity] By clinical definition, a spontaneous behavior or demonstration strictly requires the absence of prompting, making it methodologically dependent on independent performance.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_INDEPENDENT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Independence] Active states of imminent danger (severe aggression, SIB, elopement) strictly contradict the definition of independence, which mandates safe navigation and a complete absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_INDEPENDENT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Independence] Active states of imminent danger (severe aggression, SIB, elopement) strictly contradict the definition of independence, which mandates safe navigation and a complete absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_ELOPEMENT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_ELOPEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_INDEPENDENT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Independence] Active states of imminent danger (severe aggression, SIB, elopement) strictly contradict the definition of independence, which mandates safe navigation and a complete absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_INDEPENDENT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Independence] Active states of imminent danger (severe aggression, SIB, elopement) strictly contradict the definition of independence, which mandates safe navigation and a complete absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_ERROR_OR_STRUGGLE] | [Methodological Contradiction: Prompt Dependence vs Spontaneous] A behavior cannot be characterized as a 'spontaneous demonstration' if it simultaneously required prompts, fading, or was inconsistent and prompt-dependent.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION' forces 'IS_ESCAPE_MAINTAINED'. 'IS_INDEPENDENT' and 'IS_ESCAPE_MAINTAINED' cannot co-occur: [Definitional Contradiction: Escape Maintained vs Independent] Engaging in active escape-maintained maladaptive behavior directly contradicts demonstrating an independent, smooth transition.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT' forces 'IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT'. 'IS_INDEPENDENT' and 'IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Punishing Independent Behavior] Applying restrictive punishment (tactical breaks, response cost) to a client who is behaving independently and appropriately without maladaptive behaviors is an ethical and methodological violation.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE' forces 'IS_NON_DANGEROUS_BEHAVIOR'. 'IS_INDEPENDENT' and 'IS_NON_DANGEROUS_BEHAVIOR' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Contradiction: Independence vs Maladaptive Behavior] A client cannot simultaneously be documented as 'displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior' while actively engaging in non-dangerous maladaptive behaviors such as dropping to the floor and crying or ignoring instructions.
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_HAND_COMPROMISE' forces 'IS_SIB'. 'IS_INDEPENDENT' and 'IS_SIB' cannot co-occur: [Definitional Contradiction: Independence vs. SIB] Self-Injurious Behavior is maladaptive, which definitionally contradicts the 'Independent' state that strictly requires displaying an absence of maladaptive behaviors.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Punishing Spontaneous Skills] It is methodologically inappropriate and contradictory to apply high-effort punishment immediately surrounding a spontaneous demonstration of a mastered, appropriate skill.
* EXC: [IS_INTENSIVE_TEACHING] | [Methodological Contradiction: Prompted vs Spontaneous] Intensive teaching relies on maximum prompting and errorless teaching, which directly contradicts the definition of a spontaneous, unprompted skill demonstration.
* EXC: [IS_LESSER_PROMPT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Prompted and Spontaneous] A single behavior or response cannot be simultaneously prompted (via lesser prompts) and spontaneously demonstrated.
* EXC: [IS_LOW_AROUSAL] | [Physical Impossibility: Spontaneous Action While Lethargic] A lethargic, slumping, and physically fatigued client cannot simultaneously make spontaneous and unprompted skill demonstrations.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Physical Impossibility: Spontaneous Demonstration During Crisis] An active medical emergency completely precludes the ability to spontaneously and voluntarily demonstrate clinical skills.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_INDEPENDENT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Independence] Active states of imminent danger (severe aggression, SIB, elopement) strictly contradict the definition of independence, which mandates safe navigation and a complete absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_PROMPT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Prompting vs. Spontaneity] A spontaneous behavior is defined by the absence of prompts. It is methodologically impossible for a target response to be both physically prompted and spontaneously demonstrated.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_INDEPENDENT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Independence] Active states of imminent danger (severe aggression, SIB, elopement) strictly contradict the definition of independence, which mandates safe navigation and a complete absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_PICA] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_PICA' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_INDEPENDENT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Independence] Active states of imminent danger (severe aggression, SIB, elopement) strictly contradict the definition of independence, which mandates safe navigation and a complete absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_PREMACK_CONTINGENCY] | [Methodological Contradiction: Premack vs. Spontaneity] A behavior occasioned by a Premack contingency (First/Then prompt) is explicitly teacher-directed. It is methodologically contradictory to label a response as spontaneous if it was contingent on a Premack prompt.
* EXC: [IS_PROJECTILE_BEHAVIOR] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_PROJECTILE_BEHAVIOR' forces 'IS_DYSREGULATED'. 'IS_INDEPENDENT' and 'IS_DYSREGULATED' cannot co-occur: [Definitional Contradiction: Dysregulated vs Independent] A client cannot be simultaneously dysregulated (e.g., crying, whining, visibly frustrated) and independent (demonstrating an absence of maladaptive behavior, transitioning smoothly and happily).
* EXC: [IS_PROPERTY_DESTRUCTION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_PROPERTY_DESTRUCTION' forces 'IS_DYSREGULATED'. 'IS_INDEPENDENT' and 'IS_DYSREGULATED' cannot co-occur: [Definitional Contradiction: Dysregulated vs Independent] A client cannot be simultaneously dysregulated (e.g., crying, whining, visibly frustrated) and independent (demonstrating an absence of maladaptive behavior, transitioning smoothly and happily).
* EXC: [IS_RESISTANT_TRANSITION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_RESISTANT_TRANSITION' forces 'IS_DYSREGULATED'. 'IS_INDEPENDENT' and 'IS_DYSREGULATED' cannot co-occur: [Definitional Contradiction: Dysregulated vs Independent] A client cannot be simultaneously dysregulated (e.g., crying, whining, visibly frustrated) and independent (demonstrating an absence of maladaptive behavior, transitioning smoothly and happily).
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_SIB' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_INDEPENDENT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Independence] Active states of imminent danger (severe aggression, SIB, elopement) strictly contradict the definition of independence, which mandates safe navigation and a complete absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_INDEPENDENT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Independence] Active states of imminent danger (severe aggression, SIB, elopement) strictly contradict the definition of independence, which mandates safe navigation and a complete absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_WAIT_OUT' forces 'IS_NON_DANGEROUS_BEHAVIOR'. 'IS_INDEPENDENT' and 'IS_NON_DANGEROUS_BEHAVIOR' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Contradiction: Independence vs Maladaptive Behavior] A client cannot simultaneously be documented as 'displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior' while actively engaging in non-dangerous maladaptive behaviors such as dropping to the floor and crying or ignoring instructions.
