## [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Ethical/Safety Requirement: Staff Aggression] Aggression directed towards staff inherently creates an immediate physical safety risk, strictly requiring ethical and clinical management under an imminent danger protocol.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ADL_TARGET] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_ADL_TARGET' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Safety Violation: Ignoring Directed Aggression] Utilizing planned ignoring during active staff-directed aggression (e.g., hitting, biting staff) is a physical safety violation.
* EXC: [IS_AUTOMATIC_MAINTAINED] | [Function Contradiction: Automatic and Staff Directed] Staff-directed aggression is inherently socially mediated and cannot logically be classified as purely automatically maintained.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | [Safety Violation: Aversives during Directed Aggression] Implementing planned ignoring or response cost during active, staff-directed aggression presents a major physical safety risk.
* EXC: [IS_CHAINING_FORMAT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' forces 'IS_CHAINED'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINED' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Teaching ADLs During Crisis] It is methodologically invalid and physically unsafe to conduct chained daily living activities (e.g., tying shoes, hygiene routines) while a client is in imminent danger.
* EXC: [IS_EDIBLE] | [Medical Safety Hazard: Edibles During Aggression] Providing edible items while a client is actively aggressing toward staff introduces an aspiration risk and acts as a severe methodological violation by risking accidental reinforcement of aggression.
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | [Physical Impossibility: Tutoring During Aggression] A client cannot focus on or complete structured academic tutoring (like worksheets or patterning) while actively engaged in physical aggression toward the therapist.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Extinction With Aggression] Applying unmodified escape extinction to direct, severe physical aggression risks dangerous escalation and exposes the practitioner to preventable physical harm.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Ignoring Staff Aggression] Using extinction (planned ignoring) for direct physical aggression toward staff (like punching or head-butting) places practitioners at unacceptable physical risk.
* EXC: [IS_FALL_HAZARD] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Gross Motor With Aggression] A client engaging in active physical aggression while navigating gross motor fall hazards (like a therapy ball or obstacle course) poses a severe mutual fall and physical injury risk to both client and staff.
* EXC: [IS_FINE_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Physical Impossibility: Fine Motor During Aggression] It is physically impossible to safely and accurately execute fine motor targets (like tracing or stringing beads) while simultaneously directing active physical aggression toward staff.
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Methodological Mismatch: Skill Acquisition During Aggression] Teaching gross motor targets cannot safely or methodologically occur while the client is actively engaging in staff-directed aggression.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Definitional Contradiction: Independence vs. Aggression] Staff-directed aggression is a maladaptive behavior, fundamentally contradicting the 'Independent' state requiring an absence of maladaptive behaviors.
* EXC: [IS_INTENSIVE_TEACHING] | [Ethical Violation: Teaching During Aggression] Intensive teaching must be suspended if a client is actively engaging in staff-directed aggression.
* EXC: [IS_INTERNAL_STATE_TARGET] | [Methodological Mismatch: Coping vs Aggression] A client actively assaulting staff is fundamentally failing to demonstrate coping skills or the ability to accept 'no'.
* EXC: [IS_LESSER_INTERVENTION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Lesser Intervention for Aggression] Active aggression against staff requires immediate physical crisis management, making lesser interventions inappropriate and mismatched.
* EXC: [IS_LESSER_PROMPT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting During Aggression] Active staff-directed aggression requires physical safety blocks, not lesser prompts.
* EXC: [IS_LOW_AROUSAL] | [Physical Impossibility: Low Arousal vs Active Aggression] A client cannot be physically fatigued and passively slumping while simultaneously engaging in high-energy active aggression toward staff.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Medical vs Operant Mismatch: Crisis and Aggression] A client experiencing an involuntary medical emergency (e.g., seizure) cannot simultaneously engage in operant, staff-directed aggression.
* EXC: [IS_NET_TEACHING] | [Ethical Violation: NET During Aggression] Natural Environment Teaching must be completely suspended during episodes of active aggression directed at staff.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DEMAND] | [Safety Hazard: Oral Demand vs. Staff Directed Aggression] Administering oral demands such as feeding or toothbrushing while a client is actively directing aggression at the therapist (biting, swiping) poses an immediate physical safety risk and severe choking hazard.
* EXC: [IS_PROXIMITY_PAIRING] | [Safety Hazard: Proximity Pairing during Staff Aggression] Attempting to pair physically (tickles, deep pressure) while a client is actively biting or punching staff is both physically impossible to execute safely and methodologically contraindicated.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Definitional Contradiction: Regulated State and Staff Aggression] Actively engaging in open-handed hitting or biting directed at a therapist strictly precludes the client from being documented as relaxed and regulated.
* EXC: [IS_REMOTE] | [Physical Impossibility: Remote Delivery and Direct Staff Aggression] A client cannot successfully head-butt, bite, or swipe at a remote therapist over a telehealth screen.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Safety Hazard / Physical Impossibility: Sharp Objects vs Staff Aggression] The focused execution required for cutting with scissors cannot physically co-occur safely with the active, gross-motor act of directing physical aggression toward a therapist.
* EXC: [IS_SLEEP_LETHARGY] | [Physical Impossibility: Lethargy vs Staff Aggression] Severe fatigue and passively slumping contradict the deliberate, forceful gross-motor acts of punching or head-butting staff.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Contradictory States: Regulated vs Staff Aggression] Directing physical aggression (punching, head-butting) at a therapist fundamentally contradicts the compliant, motivated parameters defining a regulated state.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Contradictory Motor Execution: Smooth Transition vs Staff Aggression] Actively hitting or biting staff prevents a transition from occurring smoothly and independently.
* EXC: [IS_SOCIAL_PLAY] | [Contradictory Contexts: Social Play vs Staff Aggression] Engaging in appropriate turn-taking or sharing with a peer is methodologically and physically incompatible with actively punching or biting a therapist.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_INDEPENDENT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Contradiction: Imminent Danger vs Independence] Active states of imminent danger (severe aggression, SIB, elopement) strictly contradict the definition of independence, which mandates safe navigation and a complete absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_TOILETING_ADL' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' and 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_VOCATIONAL] | [Methodological Mismatch: Vocational Tasks During Aggression] Engaging a client in vocational tasks (e.g., wiping tables, sorting mail) is methodologically invalid and physically unsafe to conduct simultaneously with active staff-directed aggression.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Ethical Violation: Wait Out Procedure During Staff-Directed Aggression] Actively 'waiting out' a client during staff-directed aggression (e.g., punching, biting) is physically and ethically invalid, as it requires immediate blocking and safety measures to prevent injury.

---

## [IS_TACTILE_DISTRESS]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Escape Extinction During Tactile Distress] Denying escape or breaks to a client experiencing acute tactile distress (such as a rash or skin irritation) is an ethical violation that ignores physical/medical wellbeing.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Ethical Violation: Timeout for Tactile Distress] Placing a client in exclusionary timeout for behaviors that are driven by physical/tactile distress (like a rash) is an unethical denial of medical/sensory care.
* EXC: [IS_EXTINCTION_SENSITIVE] | [Ethical Violation: Extinction for Tactile Distress] Ignoring a client's behaviors that are secondary to genuine tactile or medical distress (like rashes) is an ethical violation of their medical rights.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Definitional Contradiction: Independence vs. Tactile SIB] Tactile distress includes maladaptive topographies such as repeated skin scratching and skin picking, which definitionally contradicts the 'Independent' category's requirement for an absence of maladaptive behavior.
* EXC: [IS_NO_MEDICAL] | [Medical Status Contradiction: Dermatological] "Rash / Skin irritation" is a dermatological medical condition. It is logically contradictory to report this while simultaneously claiming "None reported" for medical issues.
* EXC: [IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Tactile Distress] Utilizing restrictive punishments like tactical breaks for uncontrollable medical/tactile issues such as rashes or skin irritation is a clear ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Ethical Violation: Wait Out Procedure During Tactile Distress and Tissue Damage] Employing a 'wait out' procedure is ethically contraindicated when a client is experiencing tactile distress that involves tissue damage (e.g., scratching repeatedly or skin picking), necessitating active physical or medical intervention.

---

## [IS_TANGIBLE_MAINTAINED]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Attention Extinction for Tangibles] Planned ignoring is an extinction procedure for attention-maintained behaviors, making it methodologically incorrect for behaviors maintained by tangible access.
* EXC: [IS_AUTOMATIC_MAINTAINED] | [Function Contradiction: Automatic and Tangible] A single behavioral instance cannot logically be both strictly automatically maintained (non-social) and tangible-maintained (socially mediated).
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_REINFORCER] | [Methodological Mismatch: Escape Reinforcer for Tangible Function] Providing a break or escape to functionally reinforce a behavior that is exclusively maintained by access to tangibles represents a methodological mismatch in Skinnerian principles.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Definitional Contradiction: Regulated State and Tangible Maintained Problem Behavior] A behavior identified as tangible-maintained (reacting to denied access) implies a maladaptive response occurred, contradicting the specific criteria for being regulated (absence of problem behavior).
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Functional Contradiction: Regulated vs Tangible Maintained Problem Behavior] A client exhibiting happy, motivated transitions cannot simultaneously be engaging in maladaptive behaviors triggered and maintained by denied access or interruption of preferred activities.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Functional Contradiction: Smooth Transition vs Tangible Maintained Problem Behavior] Transitions that occur smoothly and without resistance cannot be concurrently associated with behaviors maintained by the interruption of a preferred activity.

---

## [IS_TOILETING_ADL]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_CHAINED] | Derived Chain (IS_TOILETING_ADL -> IS_CHAINING_FORMAT -> IS_CHAINED): [Methodological Requirement for Complex ADL Instruction] Toileting routines are inherently complex, multi-step sequences of behavior. Teaching toileting ADLs in applied behavior analysis methodologically requires the use of shaping, task analyses, or chaining formats to break down and teach the sequence. | [Methodological Dependency: Chaining Format] Chaining intervention formats (such as Forward Chaining, Backward Chaining, Total Task Chaining, and Task Analysis) are systematically designed to teach complex, multi-step routines. Applying a chaining methodology clinically and logically requires a chained target behavior (e.g., ADLs, vocational routines) to break down and teach.
* REQ: [IS_CHAINING_FORMAT] | [Methodological Requirement for Complex ADL Instruction] Toileting routines are inherently complex, multi-step sequences of behavior. Teaching toileting ADLs in applied behavior analysis methodologically requires the use of shaping, task analyses, or chaining formats to break down and teach the sequence.
* REQ: [IS_LOWER_BODY_DISROBING] | [Physical/Environmental Requirement] Performing toileting routines (such as using the restroom or toilet training) fundamentally and physically requires the removal or lowering of pants and underwear (lower body disrobing).
* REQ: [IS_SLIP_HAZARD] | [Physical/Environmental Requirement] Toileting ADLs physically take place in bathroom or restroom settings, which intrinsically contain plumbing fixtures and hard flooring that constitute a slip hazard.
* REQ: [IS_WATER_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical/Environmental Requirement] Toileting ADLs are fundamentally performed in a bathroom or restroom, which inherently contains plumbing and serves as a water environment.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_TOILETING_ADL' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_CRISIS_INDICATOR' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Ethical and Physical Violation: Inappropriate Toileting Environment] Completing toileting ADLs in a designated dark sensory room is physically, hygienically, and ethically inappropriate; these routines require a functional bathroom environment with adequate lighting, plumbing, and sanitation.
* EXC: [IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_TOILETING_ADL' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_DISTANCE_REQUIRED' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_EDIBLE] | [Sanitary Violation: Contamination Risk] Providing and consuming edible reinforcers during a toileting routine creates a severe sanitary hazard and biological contamination risk.
* EXC: [IS_ELOPEMENT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_TOILETING_ADL' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_ELOPEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_TOILETING_ADL' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_ENVIRONMENTAL_MODIFICATION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Bodily Autonomy] Utilizing escape extinction (physically or continuously forcing compliance) during toileting routines violates clinical ethical guidelines regarding client dignity, bodily autonomy, and the prevention of trauma.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Ethical Violation: Inappropriate Seclusion Area] Utilizing a bathroom or restroom facility as a location for an exclusionary timeout poses significant safety risks and violates ethical guidelines prohibiting seclusion in potentially hazardous or unmonitored sanitary environments.
* EXC: [IS_FOOD_PREP_AREA] | [Sanitary and Environmental Mismatch: Food Prep and Toileting] Engaging in a toileting or wiping routine within a kitchen or dining area represents a severe sanitary violation and physical contradiction.
* EXC: [IS_FRAGMENTABLE_MATERIAL] | [Sanitary Contradiction: Fragmentable Materials and Toileting] Utilizing fragmentable play materials like kinetic sand or playdough during a toileting or wiping routine creates a significant sanitary and contamination risk.
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Safety and Sanitary Mismatch: Gross Motor and Toileting] Executing high-movement gross motor tasks (e.g., catching a ball, jumping with two feet) while attempting to complete a toileting routine is unsafe and physically incompatible.
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Physical Impossibility: Hand Compromise and Toileting] If a client is actively biting their own hands or scratching their arms, they are physically unable to safely or hygienically wipe independently or complete a toileting routine.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_AROUSAL] | [Physical Mismatch: High Arousal and Toileting] Rapidly pacing the room or demonstrating a high rate of movement is physically and safely incompatible with sitting on a toilet to complete a routine.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EXERTION] | [Safety Mismatch: High Exertion and Toileting] Engaging in high exertion activities like jumping, pacing rapidly, or navigating an obstacle course during a toileting routine poses a severe safety and sanitary hazard.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_MOBILITY_ENVIRONMENT] | [Sanitary and Environmental Mismatch: Playground Toileting] An outdoor playground or gross motor area does not contain private toileting facilities; conducting a toileting routine in an open playground is physically inappropriate and unethical.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Medical Exclusivity: Emergency vs ADL Demands] A medical crisis such as a seizure or severe asthma attack requires immediate life-saving intervention, completely precluding the ability to complete a toileting routine.
* EXC: [IS_OUTDOOR_MATERIAL] | [Physical Impossibility: Outdoor Materials in Toileting Facilities] It is physically and conceptually nonsensical to deploy large outdoor gross motor materials (e.g., playground equipment) within a private indoor bathroom setting during toileting ADLs.
* EXC: [IS_OUTDOOR_REINFORCER] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_TOILETING_ADL' forces 'IS_WATER_ENVIRONMENT'. 'IS_OUTDOOR_REINFORCER' forces 'IS_HIGH_MOBILITY_ENVIRONMENT'. 'IS_WATER_ENVIRONMENT' and 'IS_HIGH_MOBILITY_ENVIRONMENT' cannot co-occur: [Environmental Mismatch: Playground and Bathroom Routine] A playground setting intrinsically contradicts an indoor bathroom/restroom water environment meant for brushing teeth or structured hand-washing routines.
* EXC: [IS_PEER_OR_SIBLING] | [Privacy Violation: Peer/Sibling Involvement in Toileting] It is a severe ethical and dignity violation of BACB guidelines to involve peers or siblings in a client's highly private toileting and hygiene ADL routines.
* EXC: [IS_PEER_TARGET] | [Privacy Violation: Peer Targets during Toileting] Targeting peer interaction or collaborative play skills during a client's private toileting routines represents a fundamental breach of client dignity, privacy, and clinical ethics.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_TOILETING_ADL' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_TOILETING_ADL' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_PICA] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_TOILETING_ADL' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_PICA' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_PROXIMITY_PAIRING] | [Ethical Violation: Dignity and Boundary Breach] Applying deep pressure, tickles, or physical pairing during toileting ADLs violates client dignity, physical boundaries, and ethical guidelines regarding private personal care.
* EXC: [IS_REMOTE] | [Ethical Violation: Telehealth Privacy Breach] Observing or conducting toileting routines via a telehealth camera constitutes a severe violation of client privacy, dignity, and HIPAA/ethical guidelines.
* EXC: [IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Punishing Biological Functions] Applying restrictive punishments such as overcorrection or response cost to toileting routines or accidents is developmentally inappropriate and violates ethical standards of compassionate care.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Safety Violation: Sharp Objects During Toileting ADLs] Allowing a client to possess or use sharp objects (e.g., cutting with scissors) while actively engaging in a toileting routine or wiping independently is a direct safety hazard and physically contradictory.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_TOILETING_ADL' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_SIB' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_TOILETING_ADL' forces 'IS_LOWER_BODY_DISROBING'. 'IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION' forces 'IS_REGULATED'. 'IS_LOWER_BODY_DISROBING' and 'IS_REGULATED' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Contradiction: Disrobing vs Regulated State] Lower body disrobing is an acute maladaptive behavior, which is physically and behaviorally incompatible with displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior or being fully regulated.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_TOILETING_ADL' forces 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT'. 'IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION' forces 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER'. 'IS_CHAINING_FORMAT' and 'IS_IMMINENT_DANGER' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting Chains During Crisis] Implementing formal chaining formats (forward/backward chaining) requires a compliant state and is methodologically impossible during an active imminent crisis.
* EXC: [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL] | [Physical Impossibility: Environmental Mismatch] Toileting routines inherently occur in restrooms or bathrooms, which is physically mutually exclusive with navigating a parking lot or practicing safety crossing in a street.

## [IS_TOKEN_ECONOMY]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_TOKEN_MATERIAL] | [Methodological Requirement] Implementing a token economy fundamentally requires the use of token materials (e.g., tokens, points, or a token board) to track and deliver conditioned reinforcement.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical Violation: Operant Conditioning During Medical Crisis] Attempting to run a token economy during a physiological emergency like a seizure or severe asthma attack is methodologically invalid and constitutes gross medical neglect.

---

## [IS_TOKEN_MATERIAL]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_TOKEN_ECONOMY] | [Methodological Dependency] The use of token materials inherently signifies that a token economy is being implemented; the material has no clinical function outside of this system.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical Violation: Materials During Medical Crisis] Managing token materials during a medical crisis like a seizure is clinically inappropriate as all focus must be on emergency medical response.

---

## [IS_TOXIC_HAZARD]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Harm Prevention Protocol] Implementing attention extinction or planned ignoring when a learner accesses or interacts with toxic household cleaning supplies violates ethical mandates for harm prevention, risking severe chemical exposure or ingestion.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | [Ethical Violation: Chemical Safety Risk] Using aversive procedures like escape extinction or planned ignoring while a learner handles household cleaning supplies represents a severe ethical and safety hazard, risking chemical burns or ingestion.
* EXC: [IS_EDIBLE] | [Safety Hazard: Toxic Ingestion Risk] Presenting edible reinforcers in the presence of or concurrently with household cleaning supplies poses a severe risk of toxic ingestion and chemical contamination.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Safety Violation: Hazardous Timeout Environment] Placing a client in an exclusionary timeout area that contains toxic hazards (e.g., household cleaning supplies) is a severe safety and ethical violation due to the risk of unmonitored ingestion or injury.
* EXC: [IS_INFANT_TOY] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Toxins with Infant Toys] Combining toxic household cleaning supplies with infant items like cause-and-effect toys or bubbles presents a critical chemical ingestion and safety risk for early learners.
* EXC: [IS_INTENSIVE_TEACHING] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Intensive Teaching with Toxins] Utilizing toxic household cleaning supplies as instructional materials during rapid-paced, highly repetitive Intensive Teaching (e.g., DTT) poses an unacceptable risk of chemical exposure or ingestion.
* EXC: [IS_LOWER_BODY_DISROBING] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Chemical Exposure on Bare Skin] Handling toxic household cleaning supplies while stripping completely naked or removing lower body clothing creates a severe risk of chemical burns or hazardous exposure to bare skin.
* EXC: [IS_LOW_AROUSAL] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Handling Toxins While Lethargic] Lethargy and sluggishness severely impair cognitive and motor functioning, making it profoundly unsafe for a client to handle toxic household cleaning supplies.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Physical Impossibility: Handling Toxins During Medical Emergency] A client cannot safely or functionally handle toxic household cleaning supplies while experiencing an active medical emergency such as a seizure.
* EXC: [IS_MESSY_MATERIAL] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Toxins in Messy Play] Combining messy play elements like kinetic sand or a water play table with toxic household cleaning supplies introduces a severe risk of inadvertent chemical mixing and ingestion.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DEMAND] | [Safety/Poisoning Hazard: Oral Demand with Toxic Materials] It is a severe poisoning risk and physically dangerous to concurrently mandate oral demands (e.g., eating, brushing teeth) while a client is handling or exposed to toxic hazards like household cleaning supplies.
* EXC: [IS_PICA] | [Severe Poisoning Risk: Pica with Toxic Hazards] Exposing a client who actively engages in pica (mouthing, licking, or swallowing non-edible items) to toxic hazards such as household cleaning supplies constitutes an extreme, life-threatening medical safety risk.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Ethical Violation: Toxic Exposure Risk] Using a 'wait out' procedure while a client is interacting with or has access to toxic household cleaning supplies constitutes a critical safety and ethical violation, as it removes the necessary intervention to prevent ingestion or chemical harm.

---

## [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_NON_CLINIC] | [Physical/Environmental Requirement] Navigating parking lots and addressing behaviors like running into the street fundamentally involve traffic-based community spaces, thereby requiring a non-clinic environment.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_ACADEMIC] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Cognitive Distraction during Traffic Navigation] Teaching cognitive and fine-motor academic skills (e.g., tracing, counting) during active traffic hazard navigation creates a profound cognitive and visual distraction, strictly dividing the learner's attention away from life-threatening environmental hazards.
* EXC: [IS_ACADEMIC_MATERIAL] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Material Distraction in Traffic] Requiring the learner to manage and focus on academic materials (such as worksheets, flashcards, or whiteboards) while navigating a parking lot or crossing the street distracts essential visual attention away from oncoming traffic, presenting a physical safety hazard.
* EXC: [IS_ADL_TARGET] | [Methodological & Safety Contradiction: ADLs in Traffic] Running structured Activities of Daily Living (ADL) targets such as brushing teeth, dressing, or using utensils while simultaneously practicing safe street crossing or navigating a parking lot is methodologically absurd and creates an immense physical safety hazard.
* EXC: [IS_ATTENTION_EXTINCTION] | [Ethical Violation: Severe Safety Hazard] Utilizing planned ignoring when a learner is navigating a parking lot or running into the street is a critical safety and ethical violation, as it actively prevents necessary physical intervention to protect the learner's life from vehicular traffic.
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_BLOCKER] | [Severe Physical Danger: Sensory Blocking in Traffic] Allowing a learner to use auditory blocking devices (such as noise-cancelling headphones) while navigating traffic hazards completely removes their ability to hear approaching vehicles or verbal warnings, posing a critical, life-threatening danger.
* EXC: [IS_AVERSIVE] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Aversive Procedures in Traffic] Enforcing aversive procedures such as escape extinction or overcorrection in the middle of an active traffic hazard (e.g., a street or parking lot) exposes the learner to immediate vehicular danger and restricts necessary life-saving movements.
* EXC: [IS_CONFIDENTIAL_DISCUSSION] | [Ethical Violation: Compromised Supervision During Traffic Hazard] Engaging in highly distracting, confidential administrative discussions (like reviewing data graphs or IEP goals) while actively managing a traffic hazard or practicing safety crossing is a severe ethical and safety violation due to compromised supervision in a life-threatening environment.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical Impossibility: Mutually Exclusive Environments] A designated dark sensory room and an active traffic hazard (such as a street or parking lot) are completely distinct, mutually exclusive physical environments that cannot be occupied simultaneously.
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | [Safety Violation: Environmental Distraction] Engaging a client in localized, visually demanding academic tasks (e.g., worksheets, tracing) while actively navigating a parking lot or crossing a street is dangerously distracting and physically incompatible with executing life-saving pedestrian skills.
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Safety Violation: Pedestrian Seclusion] Attempting to implement an exclusionary timeout procedure in an active traffic environment (e.g., street or parking lot) is physically hazardous and represents gross clinical negligence.
* EXC: [IS_FOOD_PREP_AREA] | [Physical Environment Contradiction: Food Prep and Traffic Hazard] A kitchen or dining room does not contain parking lots or active traffic hazards, making simultaneous occurrence physically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Safety/Ethical Violation: Punishment in Traffic Hazard] Administering high-effort punishment (e.g., positive practice overcorrection) in a high-danger traffic environment like a parking lot or street is extremely unsafe and an ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_HOME_SETTING] | [Physical Environment Contradiction: Home Setting and Traffic Hazard] Navigating a parking lot or a street crossing safely cannot physically occur inside the enclosed architectural setting of a bedroom, living room, or kitchen.
* EXC: [IS_INDOOR_SETTING] | [Physical Environment Contradiction: Indoor Setting and Traffic Hazard] An indoor environment like a bathroom, living room, therapy room, or classroom cannot physically host a parking lot navigation or street crossing scenario.
* EXC: [IS_INTENSIVE_TEACHING] | [Methodological Mismatch: DTT in Traffic Environments] Intensive teaching formats such as Discrete Trial Training (DTT) and Fluency Based Instruction require highly controlled instructional settings and cannot be safely or effectively conducted while navigating a parking lot or crossing a street.
* EXC: [IS_LOWER_BODY_DISROBING] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Public Disrobing in Traffic] Engaging in lower body disrobing or stripping completely naked while actively navigating a parking lot or street crossing presents an extreme physical safety risk and profound legal/public exposure concerns.
* EXC: [IS_LOW_AROUSAL] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Low Motor Readiness in Traffic] A client exhibiting low arousal (e.g., sluggish, highly fatigued, lethargic) lacks the necessary motor readiness, coordination, and reaction time to safely practice traffic hazard skills like navigating a parking lot.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Physical Safety Hazard: Medical Emergency in Traffic] Experiencing a medical crisis such as a seizure while practicing safety crossing or navigating a parking lot represents an extreme physical safety and life-threatening hazard.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DEMAND] | [Safety/Choking Risk: Oral Demand during Traffic Navigation] Placing oral demands (e.g., eating a variety of foods, brushing teeth) while a client is actively navigating a parking lot or practicing safety crossing in traffic poses a severe choking hazard and unsafe visual distraction.
* EXC: [IS_OUTDOOR_MATERIAL] | [Physical Safety Risk: Outdoor Play Items in Traffic] It is physically dangerous and functionally contradictory to utilize outdoor gross motor play materials (e.g., playground equipment, large obstacle courses) within active traffic hazard zones such as streets or parking lots.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Safety Violation: Sharp Objects During Traffic Navigation] It is a profound safety violation and physical contradiction to have a client handle sharp objects (e.g., scissors) while simultaneously navigating parking lots, crossing streets, or actively engaging in traffic environments.
* EXC: [IS_TOILETING_ADL] | [Physical Impossibility: Environmental Mismatch] Toileting routines inherently occur in restrooms or bathrooms, which is physically mutually exclusive with navigating a parking lot or practicing safety crossing in a street.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Ethical Violation: Harm Prevention in Traffic] Employing a 'wait out' procedure (withdrawing physical guidance and attention) when a client is in a traffic hazard situation (e.g., running into the street or parking lot) is a severe safety violation; immediate intervention is medically and ethically mandated.
* EXC: [IS_WATER_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical Impossibility: Contextual Mismatch] Navigating a street or parking lot is physically mutually exclusive with being in a water environment such as a bathroom, washing hands at a sink, or engaging at a water play table.

---

## [IS_TRANSITION]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Physical Impossibility: Transition During Medical Emergency] Transitioning back to a standard class routine or transitioning to a teacher is physically and medically impossible during an acute crisis like a seizure; emergency stabilization is required.

---

## [IS_VIBRATING_ENVIRONMENT]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_NON_CLINIC] | [Physical/Environmental Requirement] Engaging in vehicle transport inherently takes place dynamically in the community, physically requiring a non-clinic setting.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_EXCLUSIONARY_TIMEOUT] | [Safety Violation: Seclusion in a Moving Vehicle] Enforcing an exclusionary timeout within a moving vehicle is physically impossible to execute safely, violates vehicle safety and seatbelt protocols, and risks severe injury during transit.
* EXC: [IS_FALL_HAZARD] | [Safety Violation: Unsecured Gross Motor Activity] Engaging in dynamic gross motor activities (e.g., therapy ball, obstacle courses, swinging, jumping) inside a moving vehicle is physically incompatible with seatbelt safety laws and presents an acute risk of traumatic injury.
* EXC: [IS_FOOD_PREP_AREA] | [Physical Environment Contradiction: Food Prep and Vehicle] A kitchen or dining room is a stationary room within a building, structurally contradicting the environment of a moving vehicle or transport.
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Safety Violation: Gross Motor in a Vehicle] Practicing large gross motor targets like jumping or catching a ball within a moving vehicle poses a severe safety hazard and violates seatbelt/restraint requirements.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EXERTION] | [Safety Violation: High Exertion in a Vehicle] Running, jumping, or pacing rapidly while inside a vehicle represents a severe physical impossibility and acute safety hazard.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_MOBILITY_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical Environment Contradiction: Playground and Vehicle] A playground or expansive gross motor obstacle course cannot physically fit or co-occur within the confined space of a moving vehicle.
* EXC: [IS_HOME_SETTING] | [Physical Environment Contradiction: Home Setting and Vehicle] A home setting (e.g., bedroom, kitchen, living room) is a stationary building, structurally and physically incompatible with being inside a moving vehicle.
* EXC: [IS_INDOOR_SETTING] | [Physical Environment Contradiction: Indoor Setting and Vehicle] An indoor structural setting (e.g., therapy room, bedroom, hallway) is physically contradictory to being contained within a moving vehicle.
* EXC: [IS_OUTDOOR_MATERIAL] | [Physical Impossibility: Outdoor Gross Motor in Transport] It is physically impossible to construct or utilize large outdoor materials, such as playground equipment or gross motor obstacle courses, inside a standard vibrating environment like a moving vehicle or transport.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Safety Violation: Sharp Objects in Vibrating Environments] Utilizing sharp objects for fine motor tasks (e.g., cutting with scissors) while in a vibrating or moving environment (e.g., vehicle or transport) presents a severe and physically contradictory safety hazard.

---

## [IS_VISUAL_DEPENDENT_TASK]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AGGRESSION] | [Physical & Methodological Impossibility: Precision Visual Tasks during Aggression] It is physically and methodologically impossible for a learner to successfully execute visually dependent fine-motor tasks (such as tracing letters, writing, or matching items) while actively engaging in severe physical aggression like punching, kicking, or hair-pulling.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Visual Acuity Requirement] It is physically and methodologically impossible to perform tasks requiring accurate visual discrimination (such as tracing letters, identifying colors, matching items, or reading worksheets) in a dark sensory room devoid of sufficient lighting.
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Physical Contradiction: Gross Motor vs Fine Visual Task] Performing gross motor activities such as jumping or navigating an obstacle course is physically incompatible with simultaneously executing stationary, fine-motor visual tasks like tracing letters or completing worksheets.
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Physical Impossibility: Hand Compromise and Visual Tasks] A client actively engaging in hand-directed self-injury (e.g., biting hands, severe scratching) cannot physically grasp writing utensils to trace letters, write their name, or complete worksheets.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_AROUSAL] | [Physical Mismatch: High Arousal and Visual Tasks] A client who is highly agitated, pacing rapidly, or hyperactive cannot physically sustain the stationary visual attention and fine motor control required for tracing letters or focusing on worksheets.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EXERTION] | [Physical Contradiction: High Exertion vs Visual Tasks] Rapidly pacing, jumping, or navigating a gross motor obstacle course makes completing fine-motor visual tasks, such as writing and tracing, physically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Physical Mismatch: Imminent Danger and Visual Tasks] A client engaging in imminent danger such as elopement, severe head banging, or bolting cannot safely or physically complete worksheets, tracing, or visually dependent fine-motor tasks.
* EXC: [IS_LOW_AROUSAL] | [Physical Impossibility: Visual Attention During Low Arousal] A client who is passively slumping, lethargic, or sluggish physically lacks the physiological readiness required to actively sustain visual attention for complex tracing or worksheet tasks.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Physical Impossibility: Visual Tasks During Medical Emergency] A client cannot physiologically sustain visual attention or complete worksheets while experiencing a medical crisis such as a seizure, dehydration, or respiratory distress.

---

## [IS_VOCAL_COMPROMISE]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_EDIBLE] | [Medical Hazard: Choking and Aspiration] Administering edible items while a client is engaging in loud, sustained vocal protests or screaming presents a high risk of choking and aspiration, making concurrent application medically unsafe.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Contradictory Behavior States: Independence vs Vocal Compromise] A learner displaying an 'absence of maladaptive behavior' and transitioning 'smoothly and independently' cannot simultaneously be engaging in loud, sustained protests or high-pitch screaming.
* EXC: [IS_LOW_AROUSAL] | [Physical Impossibility: Arousal State Contradiction] A client physically cannot be in a state of extreme low arousal (passively slumping, lethargic) while simultaneously exhibiting high-arousal vocal compromise (screaming at a high pitch, loud sustained protests).
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Physical Impossibility: Volitional Vocal Protests During Seizure] A client actively experiencing a tonic-clonic seizure physically cannot engage in volitional vocal compromise behaviors such as repetitive vocal scripting or sustained loud protests.
* EXC: [IS_NET_TEACHING] | [Methodological Mismatch: NET During Vocal Compromise] Natural Environment Teaching (NET) relies on the learner's intrinsic motivation and functional play; if the learner is screaming at a high pitch or engaged in loud sustained protests, the conditions for NET are methodologically destroyed.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Physiological Impossibility: Regulated Affect vs Vocal Escalation] It is physiologically and definitionally impossible for a client to be calm, cooperative, and displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior while simultaneously screaming at a high pitch.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DEMAND] | [Physiological Impossibility: Deep Breathing While Screaming] A client cannot physiologically perform deep breathing exercises or blow bubbles while simultaneously engaging in high-pitched screaming or loud, sustained vocal protests.
* EXC: [IS_VOCAL_VERBAL_TARGET] | [Physical Impossibility: Competing Vocal Behaviors] A client cannot physically engage in loud, sustained vocal protests or high-pitch screaming while simultaneously performing structured vocal verbal targets such as conversational turn-taking or answering WH-questions.

---

## [IS_VOCAL_VERBAL_TARGET]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AUDITORY_BLOCKER] | [Methodological Impossibility: Sensory Blocking during Vocal Targets] It is methodologically impossible to accurately run precision vocal-verbal targets (such as echoic imitation or conversational turn-taking) while the learner's auditory input is intentionally blocked by noise-cancellation headphones or loud media, as they cannot hear the discriminative stimulus or their own response.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_AROUSAL] | [Methodological Mismatch: High Arousal and Verbal Targets] It is methodologically contradictory to attempt teaching complex conversational turn-taking or structured verbal intraverbals while a client is highly agitated, crying, or pacing rapidly.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Methodological Mismatch: Imminent Danger and Verbal Targets] Attempting to teach conversational turn-taking or complex tacting while a client is actively banging their head, bolting, or gouging eyes is methodologically invalid and ignores immediate life-safety priorities.
* EXC: [IS_INAPPROPRIATE_LANGUAGE] | [Ethical Violation: Teaching Inappropriate Language] Targeting the acquisition of peer greetings or conversational turn-taking using profanity, threats, or derogatory language is clinically inappropriate and an ethical violation.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Physical Impossibility: Vocal Production During Respiratory/Neurological Crisis] A client actively experiencing a tonic-clonic seizure or an acute asthma attack is physically incapable of producing controlled vocal-verbal targets such as answering WH questions or tacting objects.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DISTRESS] | [Medical Contraindication: Vocal Demands during Oral Distress] It is ethically and medically contraindicated to mandate vocal-verbal targets (e.g., conversational turn-taking, echoics, manding) when a client is experiencing acute oral distress, such as dental pain or severe tissue damage from biting their lips.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Medical Hazard: Forced Vocalizations During Respiratory Distress] Placing vocal demands (e.g., echoics, conversational turn-taking) on a client experiencing an asthma attack or respiratory distress directly exacerbates physical harm and restricts oxygen.
* EXC: [IS_VOCAL_COMPROMISE] | [Physical Impossibility: Competing Vocal Behaviors] A client cannot physically engage in loud, sustained vocal protests or high-pitch screaming while simultaneously performing structured vocal verbal targets such as conversational turn-taking or answering WH-questions.
* EXC: [IS_WAIT_OUT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Attention and Demand Variables] A 'wait out' procedure requires withholding attention and ceasing demands. This methodologically contradicts executing vocal verbal targets, which inherently require active social prompting, interaction, and demand placement.

---

## [IS_VOCATIONAL]

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_AGGRESSION] | [Physical & Methodological Impossibility: Vocational Routines during Aggression] It is physically and methodologically impossible to accurately perform structured vocational tasks (such as assembling items or sorting mail) while simultaneously engaging in severe topographies of physical aggression.
* EXC: [IS_DARK_ENVIRONMENT] | [Physical and Methodological Mismatch: Environmental Constraints for Vocational Tasks] Vocational tasks such as clocking in, completing assembly tasks, sorting mail, and following visual checklists require functional lighting and cannot be physically or methodologically executed in a dark sensory room.
* EXC: [IS_GROSS_MOTOR_TARGET] | [Physical Contradiction: Gross Motor vs Vocational Assembly] Catching a ball or practicing jumping is physically incompatible with focused, stationary vocational tasks such as sorting mail or completing fine assembly tasks.
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Physical Impossibility: Hand Compromise and Vocational Tasks] Actively biting or scratching one's hands precludes the physical capability required to sort mail, wipe tables, or complete fine vocational assembly tasks.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_AROUSAL] | [Methodological Mismatch: High Arousal and Vocational Tasks] Rapid pacing or severe agitation physically and methodologically prevents the sustained attention required to sort mail or assemble items.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Methodological Mismatch: Imminent Danger and Vocational Tasks] It is a severe safety and methodological violation to prompt structured vocational tasks like sorting mail or wiping tables while a learner is in a state of imminent danger (e.g., severe aggression, bolting).
* EXC: [IS_INFANT_TOY] | [Ethical Violation: Age-Inappropriate Materials] Infant toys such as bubbles and cause-and-effect toys are developmentally inappropriate and violate the dignity of older learners engaged in vocational tasks like clocking in or completing assembly tasks.
* EXC: [IS_LOWER_BODY_DISROBING] | [Ethical Violation: Dignity in Vocational Settings] Removing pants or stripping completely naked during vocational training (e.g., clocking in, sorting mail) severely violates community workplace norms and basic client dignity.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical Violation: Vocational Demands During Medical Crisis] Imposing vocational expectations (e.g., clocking in, assembly tasks) during a severe medical crisis such as a seizure or asthma attack is highly unethical and medically neglectful.
* EXC: [IS_MESSY_MATERIAL] | [Dignity Violation: Messy Play in Vocational Training] Utilizing sensory messy materials such as a water play table or kinetic sand during structured adult vocational tasks (e.g., clocking in, sorting mail) violates age-appropriateness and professional workplace norms.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Methodological Mismatch: Vocational Tasks During Severe Topography] It is physically and methodologically impossible to safely and correctly implement structured vocational demands (e.g., assembly tasks, clocking in) while a client is actively engaged in severe, dangerous topography.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | [Methodological Mismatch: Vocational Tasks During SIB] Structured vocational work demands (e.g., assembly, sorting) are physically incompatible and methodologically contraindicated when a client is experiencing active self-injurious episodes.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Vocational Tasks During Aggression] Engaging a client in vocational tasks (e.g., wiping tables, sorting mail) is methodologically invalid and physically unsafe to conduct simultaneously with active staff-directed aggression.

---

## [IS_WAIT_OUT]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_NON_DANGEROUS_BEHAVIOR] | [Ethical and Methodological Dependency] A 'wait out' procedure (allowing a behavior to occur without intervention until it ceases) methodologically and ethically requires the target behavior to be strictly non-dangerous. Utilizing a wait out approach for dangerous or self-injurious behaviors would violate clinical safety protocols.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_CHAINING_FORMAT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Active Instructional Chaining vs. Demand Removal] Active instructional chaining formats (Forward Chaining, Backward Chaining, Total Task Analysis) require systematic, step-by-step prompting and instruction, which is methodologically incompatible with a Wait Out procedure that explicitly involves the deliberate removal of demands and cessation of prompting.
* EXC: [IS_DISCRETE] | [Methodological Mismatch: Active Discrete Trials vs. Demand Removal] Running active discrete trials (e.g., academic readiness, tacting, matching, echoing) is methodologically contradictory to using a Wait Out procedure, as one requires active instructional demands and engagement while the other necessitates the complete withdrawal of demands.
* EXC: [IS_EDUCATIONAL_TUTORING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Academic Demand vs. Demand Suspension] Educational tutoring requires the active presentation of academic demands (e.g., worksheets), whereas a wait-out procedure specifically requires the temporary removal and suspension of demands. They cannot occur simultaneously.
* EXC: [IS_ELOPEMENT] | [Ethical Violation: Negligence During Elopement] Utilizing a wait-out procedure (suspending demands and waiting without physical intervention) when a client is actively eloping toward exits or traffic is a severe safety and ethical violation, as immediate intervention is required to prevent harm.
* EXC: [IS_ESCAPE_EXTINCTION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Opposing Demand Paradigms] Escape extinction requires the practitioner to follow through with placing and maintaining a demand, whereas a wait-out procedure necessitates dropping or pausing the demand. These are methodologically opposite and mutually exclusive.
* EXC: [IS_GI_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Waiting Out Medical Symptoms] Applying an extinction or 'wait out' procedure to a suspected medical symptom, such as severe GI distress, is an ethical violation of BACB guidelines that require addressing medical needs immediately.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_EFFORT_PUNISHMENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Active Punishment vs Wait Out] Overcorrection is an active, high-effort consequence requiring physical prompting, whereas a 'wait out' procedure relies on extinction/ignoring. Simultaneously applying both is methodologically impossible.
* EXC: [IS_IMMINENT_DANGER] | [Ethical Violation: Waiting Out Imminent Danger] Applying an extinction or 'wait out' procedure to behaviors that involve imminent danger (e.g., self-injurious behavior, elopement) violates BACB ethical codes mandating immediate intervention to protect the client from harm.
* EXC: [IS_INDEPENDENT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Independence vs Wait Out Procedure] A wait-out procedure is implemented for noncompliance or behavioral refusal, which directly contradicts a behavioral state defined by an absence of maladaptive behavior and smooth, independent transitions.
* EXC: [IS_INTENSIVE_TEACHING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Intensive Demands vs Wait Out] Intensive teaching strategies like DTT necessitate a high rate of instructional demands, directly contradicting a wait-out procedure, which strictly requires the temporary cessation of all therapist demands and interaction.
* EXC: [IS_INTERNAL_STATE_TARGET] | [Methodological Mismatch: Active Coping Coaching vs Wait Out] Actively teaching and prompting internal state targets, such as using deep breathing or asking for a break, is fundamentally incompatible with a wait-out procedure that demands zero interaction or prompting.
* EXC: [IS_LESSER_INTERVENTION] | [Methodological Mismatch: Redirection vs Wait Out] Lesser interventions such as redirection or presenting a visual schedule require active therapist engagement, which violates the parameters of a wait-out procedure.
* EXC: [IS_LESSER_PROMPT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Prompting During Wait Out] The delivery of lesser prompts (verbal, gestural, or visual) constitutes an instructional demand, fundamentally contradicting the withholding of interaction required during a wait-out procedure.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_CRISIS] | [Ethical Violation: Withholding Medical Care] Utilizing a wait-out behavioral procedure during a medical crisis (e.g., seizure, acute asthma) constitutes life-threatening medical neglect.
* EXC: [IS_MEDICAL_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Ignoring Medical Distress] Waiting out symptoms of genuine medical distress (e.g., extreme pain, ear infections, seizure activity) as if they were strictly operant behaviors is medically neglectful and violates ethical guidelines.
* EXC: [IS_NET_TEACHING] | [Methodological Mismatch: NET vs Wait Out] Natural Environment Teaching (NET) requires active engagement to capture learner motivation, rendering it methodologically incompatible with a wait-out procedure that demands the removal of all interaction.
* EXC: [IS_ORAL_DISTRESS] | [Medical Neglect: Waiting Out Oral Distress] Implementing a passive wait-out procedure is medically neglectful and dangerous when a client is experiencing acute oral distress, dental pain, or tissue damage, which requires immediate medical/clinical intervention rather than behavioral extinction.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_CONTACT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Physical Contact during Wait Out] A wait-out procedure necessitates minimizing interaction and physical contact; providing affirmative physical touch, deep pressure, or tickles completely contradicts the fundamental behavioral methodology of waiting.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_CONTACT_WITH_THERAPIST] | [Safety Violation: Waiting Out Therapist-Directed Aggression] It is a severe safety violation to employ a passive wait-out procedure when a client is actively engaging in dangerous physical aggression directed at the therapist (e.g., biting, head-butting, swiping), which firmly mandates active physical safety management.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_INTERVENTION] | [Methodological Contradiction: Active Physical Intervention vs Wait Out] Employing active physical interventions (e.g., physically blocking an exit, tactical breaks) is methodologically mutually exclusive with a wait-out procedure, which requires passive non-intervention and withholding of contact.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_PROMPT] | [Methodological Contradiction: Physical Prompting during Wait Out] Administering partial or full physical prompts completely negates the methodology of a wait-out procedure, which relies on strategically withholding active guidance until compliance occurs independently.
* EXC: [IS_PHYSICAL_SAFETY_MANAGEMENT] | [Safety Contradiction: Passive Wait Out vs Active Safety Management] A wait-out procedure cannot be safely or methodologically implemented when a client concurrently requires active physical safety management (e.g., using safety pads, blocking swallow attempts) to prevent immediate physical harm.
* EXC: [IS_PROXIMITY_PAIRING] | [Methodological Mismatch: Reinforcement vs Extinction] Proximity pairing involves active rapport building and reinforcement (e.g., tickles, toy pairing), which conceptually contradicts the passive withholding of attention and demands required in a wait-out procedure.
* EXC: [IS_PROXIMITY_PROMPT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Active Prompting vs Wait Out] Using active instructional prompts (gestural, positional, model) violates the core methodology of a wait-out procedure, which requires the temporary removal of demands.
* EXC: [IS_REGULATED] | [Methodological Mismatch: Extinction During Regulated State] A wait-out procedure involves withholding reinforcement/demands during noncompliance or escalation. It is methodologically invalid to apply this when a client is fully regulated, happy, and readily responding.
* EXC: [IS_RESPIRATORY_DISTRESS] | [Medical Neglect: Withholding Intervention for Respiratory Distress] Using a wait-out procedure (withholding attention or intervention) during active respiratory distress such as an asthma attack constitutes medical neglect and presents a severe safety hazard.
* EXC: [IS_RESTRICTIVE_PUNISHMENT] | [Methodological Mismatch: Active Punishment vs Passive Withholding] Implementing active restrictive punishments (like overcorrection) is methodologically incompatible with the simultaneous execution of a passive wait-out procedure.
* EXC: [IS_SENSORY_DISTRESS] | [Medical Neglect: Withholding Intervention for Sensory/Pain Distress] Applying a wait-out behavioral extinction procedure to a physiological medical symptom like a severe headache is medically negligent and ethically prohibited.
* EXC: [IS_SEVERE_TOPOGRAPHY] | [Ethical Violation: Wait Out Procedure During Severe Topography] Implementing a 'wait out' procedure for severe, dangerous topography (e.g., bolting, gouging eyes, smearing feces) is an ethical violation of the harm prevention code, requiring immediate active intervention rather than waiting.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Safety Violation: Sharp Objects During Dysregulation Wait Out] Employing a 'wait out' procedure while a client is in possession of sharp objects (e.g., scissors) poses an immediate physical safety threat, as dysregulated behavior with sharp tools must be actively managed and disarmed.
* EXC: [IS_SIB] | [Ethical Violation: Wait Out Procedure During SIB] It is an ethical violation of harm prevention protocols to apply a 'wait out' procedure while a client is actively engaged in self-injurious behavior (e.g., banging head, gouging eyes), which demands immediate safety intervention.
* EXC: [IS_SMART_CHIP_REGULATED] | [Methodological Contradiction: Wait Out Procedure During Regulated States] A 'wait out' procedure is inherently designed as a consequence for maladaptive behaviors or non-compliance. Applying it while a client is highly motivated, happily transitioning, and regulated represents a fundamental methodological contradiction.
* EXC: [IS_SMOOTH_TRANSITION] | [Methodological Contradiction: Wait Out Procedure for Smooth Transitions] It is methodologically illogical to utilize a 'wait out' procedure for a transition that is explicitly described as smooth, happy, and completed without resistance.
* EXC: [IS_SOCIAL_PLAY] | [Methodological Mismatch: Wait Out Procedure During Social Play] A 'wait out' procedure is applied to manage maladaptive behavior. It is methodologically invalid to apply this strategy while the client is successfully engaged in appropriate peer interaction and sustained cooperative social play.
* EXC: [IS_SPONTANEOUS] | Implicit Exclusion: 'IS_WAIT_OUT' forces 'IS_NON_DANGEROUS_BEHAVIOR'. 'IS_SPONTANEOUS' forces 'IS_INDEPENDENT'. 'IS_NON_DANGEROUS_BEHAVIOR' and 'IS_INDEPENDENT' cannot co-occur: [Methodological Contradiction: Independence vs Maladaptive Behavior] A client cannot simultaneously be documented as 'displaying an absence of maladaptive behavior' while actively engaging in non-dangerous maladaptive behaviors such as dropping to the floor and crying or ignoring instructions.
* EXC: [IS_STAFF_DIRECTED_AGGRESSION] | [Ethical Violation: Wait Out Procedure During Staff-Directed Aggression] Actively 'waiting out' a client during staff-directed aggression (e.g., punching, biting) is physically and ethically invalid, as it requires immediate blocking and safety measures to prevent injury.
* EXC: [IS_TACTILE_DISTRESS] | [Ethical Violation: Wait Out Procedure During Tactile Distress and Tissue Damage] Employing a 'wait out' procedure is ethically contraindicated when a client is experiencing tactile distress that involves tissue damage (e.g., scratching repeatedly or skin picking), necessitating active physical or medical intervention.
* EXC: [IS_TOXIC_HAZARD] | [Ethical Violation: Toxic Exposure Risk] Using a 'wait out' procedure while a client is interacting with or has access to toxic household cleaning supplies constitutes a critical safety and ethical violation, as it removes the necessary intervention to prevent ingestion or chemical harm.
* EXC: [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL] | [Ethical Violation: Harm Prevention in Traffic] Employing a 'wait out' procedure (withdrawing physical guidance and attention) when a client is in a traffic hazard situation (e.g., running into the street or parking lot) is a severe safety violation; immediate intervention is medically and ethically mandated.
* EXC: [IS_VOCAL_VERBAL_TARGET] | [Methodological Mismatch: Attention and Demand Variables] A 'wait out' procedure requires withholding attention and ceasing demands. This methodologically contradicts executing vocal verbal targets, which inherently require active social prompting, interaction, and demand placement.

---

## [IS_WATER_ENVIRONMENT]

**Requires (Must Co-Occur):**
* REQ: [IS_SLIP_HAZARD] | [Physical/Environmental Requirement] Clinical environments and activities that involve water inherently introduce wet surfaces, which physically guarantees the presence of a slip hazard.

**Excludes (Cannot Co-Occur):**
* EXC: [IS_HAND_COMPROMISE] | [Physical Impossibility: Hand Compromise and Water Routine] If a client is actively engaging in hand-biting or scratching, they cannot physically perform a thorough and hygienic hand-washing or tooth-brushing routine.
* EXC: [IS_HIGH_MOBILITY_ENVIRONMENT] | [Environmental Mismatch: Playground and Bathroom Routine] A playground setting intrinsically contradicts an indoor bathroom/restroom water environment meant for brushing teeth or structured hand-washing routines.
* EXC: [IS_SHARP_OBJECT] | [Safety Violation: Sharp Objects in Water Environments] Engaging in fine motor tasks with sharp objects (e.g., scissors) while interacting in wet environments (e.g., water play, washing hands, brushing teeth) is an unsafe physical mismatch due to lack of stable, dry task surfaces and slippage risks.
* EXC: [IS_TRAFFIC_HAZARD_SKILL] | [Physical Impossibility: Contextual Mismatch] Navigating a street or parking lot is physically mutually exclusive with being in a water environment such as a bathroom, washing hands at a sink, or engaging at a water play table.

---
